# Reader workbench: inquiry, worked problems, passage cues, syntopic reading and loop closure (v1)

**Status:** draft for owner review (2026-10-12), **revised after the integration review the same day** (§20). The review checked every phase against the code on `main` at `68213d96` and against the in-flight branches. It then split each phase into a small first slice that ships a learner benefit on its own and a second slice that adds the model work. It also folded in a Codex reviewer's high-ROI list (§20.3). **Owner decision, 2026-10-12 (§20.7):** cold follow-ups have no count cap; the existing EIG/EVSI machinery chooses, numbers and dates them (§9.3). A coverage audit of the source dialogue against this spec is `docs/reviews/reader-dialogue-coverage.md`. **Owner decisions, 2026-10-12, restoration (§20.8):** every idea the audit found narrowed, dropped or deferred is restored into a phase, or listed in §17 as an explicitly labelled DEFERRED item with what it would take and a trigger. W1 now separates three tiers of signals and allows the middle one to steer hypotheses and routing, never mastery. The backend that serves canonical sources as practice and probes is a separate track, `docs/specs/spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md` (branch `sources/canonical-practice`); this spec consumes it and does not design it. **Integrated with the experience design and re-reviewed against the brainstorm (2026-10-13, §20.9):** every phase's "what the learner sees" names its sections in `docs/specs/ux_reader_workbench_v1.md`, the contradictions between the two documents are resolved, the UX document's open questions are owner decisions D29–D41 with its recommended defaults (D42–D43 settle two contradictions), the brainstorm's remaining details are built or labelled (ideas other specs own are listed in §17.1), and every consumption of the sources spec now uses its design and names as committed at `ae82eb08`. **Spec–UX consistency review (2026-10-13, §20.10):** a critic found no absent brainstorm idea and no unlabelled cut, and seven contradictions between this spec and the UX document; all seven are resolved in both (D44–D46, G59–G62). **Adaptive hints folded in (2026-10-13, §20.11):** RW2b's hints now come from the hint service of `docs/specs/spec_adaptive_hints_v1.md` (its phase H3): the ladder's levels gain kinds (questions, waypoints with a horizon, labelled subgoal plans, next steps, worked pieces), hints are placed on that spec's approach maps, one writer, one chooser and one assists table serve every surface, and the first offer is sampled from the chooser's own probabilities with the propensity logged. All twelve of that spec's proposed edits are applied, seven with an adaptation; the conflicts and their resolutions are in §20.11 (D8, D24 and D47; G63–G64).

**Source:** `docs/brainstorming/Improving Reader's design.md` (the owner's design dialogue, committed with this spec). Its three rounds:
1. the agent's brainstorm: intentions, textbook pain points, learning loops, features A–G, the workbench layout, retrieval;
2. the owner's reply: interactive figures from selections, syntopic reading, misconception-aware reading, scaffolding that scales with prior knowledge and struggle, "hints I couldn't reconcile with my own work", natural-language questions to the library with an optional tutor, and session summaries feeding the router, then a short unfamiliar cold problem;
3. the owner's proposal that canonical sources become a natural-language retrieval tool for the router and the tutor.

**Owner preferences in force** (from `daily_loop_frictionless_defaults.md`, `implementation_goal.md` and the owner decisions recorded across the specs):
- **Honest labels, generous defaults.** Live changes go behind kill switches. There are no shadow stages by default.
- **LLM judgment over hand-picked constants.** Where a choice is a judgment (which help to offer first, which passage deserves a cue), an LLM or the Decider makes it from typed learner state. Constants appear only as caps.
- **Learner text over skip buttons.** The precedent is "I'm stuck" (`docs/reviews/stuck-prompt.md`): a free-text box replaces a bare skip wherever the text can be used.
- **Embeddings shortlist, judges decide** (`spec_embedding_consumers_v1.md` §1, R1–R6). No cosine cutoff is hand-set and no calibrator is built.
- **Your questions and hints are the placement signal** (frictionless S5): they show where the learner is shaky, and asking never lowers the record.
- **No artificial count caps** (owner, 2026-10-12): value of information, time and money bound how much LearnLoop does. Display limits that protect attention (the cue density) are labelled as such and the learner can change them.
- **Codex on the owner's ChatGPT subscription** may run every paid LLM role, recorded as `estimated_only`. The Decider is `perplexity/pplx-decider-v1.1-27b` on OpenRouter (router R0, merged). The ChatGPT-plan provider (merged on `main` at `57d31c63`; the owner's sign-in pending) is the fast route for short structured calls.

## 0. Thesis

The dialogue's sentence is the design rule:

> Does this interaction help the learner return to the thing they wanted to accomplish, more capable than when they encountered the obstacle?

Its companion question, asked of every feature before it is built (round 1 §1): *"What is preventing this person from accomplishing their immediate learning goal, and what is the smallest intervention that would help them move forward?"*

The Reader today is a good source viewer with an Ask rail, capture presets, quick checks and exercise import (§3). The features are separate rooms. The learner's real loops cross them:
- **encounter → question → resolution → resume**: the Ask history is flat, so one question that leads to a prerequisite loses the original question;
- **attempt → error → diagnosis → repair → retry**: an imported exercise becomes a practice item, but working it happens elsewhere, and hints don't refer to the learner's own work. The owner's own pain point: "when I would be given a hint I wouldn't be able to reconcile with the work I have already done";
- **read → notice → check**: the Guide already knows which passages touch a recent misunderstanding, but says so only in a side list;
- **question → sources → explanation**: library search finds passages but can't say what each source contributes, or how two books differ;
- **session → memory → later check**: nothing brings a returning learner back to where they were, nothing summarizes a reading session for the router, and nothing turns assisted understanding into a later independent check.

**The Reader becomes a source-grounded workbench.** Reading still looks like reading. When the learner investigates, the Ask rail branches and reconnects. When the learner works a problem, the source stays beside a step-by-step workspace that survives navigation and restarts, and whose hints start from the learner's own steps. Passages that touch something the learner got wrong before carry a quiet margin mark. A question to the library returns the passages, organized by what each source contributes, with the tutor one click away. When the learner comes back, the Reader shows where they were and what is still open; LearnLoop gives the router a summary and schedules short unfamiliar problems for later — as many as are worth the learner's time, chosen by how much each would tell LearnLoop (§9.3).

The restoration (§20.8) carries the rest of the dialogue into the same Reader:
- **The learner's books become the instructional substrate** (round 3). The tutor looks things up in them on its own, offers another source's explanation when one didn't help, and offers a textbook exercise to practise or to tell two explanations apart. The retrieval and selection behind this is `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md`; the Reader is where the learner meets it.
- **The workbench asks before it repairs.** When two explanations of an error are open, the first rung of help is a short question whose answer tells them apart, and LearnLoop suggests it first when its judgment says asking is worth it (§5.2, §5.5), as in the dialogue's central scenario.
- **Hints differ in kind as well as amount** (§20.11). A hint can be a question, a goal to aim for a few steps ahead, a plan of labelled subgoals, the next step, or a worked piece, written for the learner's current work by the adaptive-hints spec's service and placed on a small map of the routes through the problem. Which kind comes first is a judgment over the learner's state, logged so LearnLoop can find out which kinds help this learner.
- **What the learner says while reading counts as signal, never as grade.** Missed predictions, "still unclear", next-step attempts and check verdicts steer hypotheses, hints and follow-ups (W1, tier 2).
- **Explorable figures show areas, sums and motion**, starting with the dialogue's own two-sided exponential (§8). **Comparisons can be kept**, as proposals in the existing source-object layer (§7.8). **Reading paths** say what each section is for at the depth the learner chose (§10).

**One page, five postures** (round 1 §3: "reading, investigating an idea, solving a problem, and reviewing knowledge should feel like related stages of one workflow rather than separate interfaces"; round 1 §4: "none of these starts with opening a particular application or navigating to a tab"). The Reader stays one screen with the source always on it. Reading, investigating, working, comparing and returning are postures of its right-hand side, and the learner's own act chooses the posture: asking investigates, "work through this" works, "where else is this explained?" compares. Every detour pushes an entry on a return stack, shown as a detour strip, and one key goes back. The presentation is the UX document's §3; the phases below build the postures they need.

Five rules carry over from the dialogue:
1. **Preserve the learner's evolving reasoning,** not only what was asked and whether the final answer was right.
2. **Help is a deliberate, recorded quantity.** The learner can always get more. What LearnLoop offers first, in amount and in kind, adapts to prior knowledge and struggle, and every piece of help is recorded as assistance, so assisted success is never mistaken for independent competence.
3. **Sources keep their identity.** What a source claims, what LearnLoop infers and what the learner believes stay separate. Cross-source reading preserves differences instead of fusing them into a consensus.
4. **No intervention is a legitimate outcome.** Cues are marks in the margin, never interruptions.
5. **The learner's purposes govern** (round 1 §10, after Matuschak: "learning technology should support a person's own purposes, not impose a universal definition of what they should know"). The learner picks the depth of a reading path and overrides any label, sets how much help is offered first, sets how many marks appear, and can undo every commitment LearnLoop makes on their behalf (W11).

## 1. Scope

**In scope (v1).** Each phase has a first slice (**a**) that ships on its own, mostly from existing parts, and a second slice (**b**) that adds the new model work. The order is in §13.

Phases marked *(restored)* were added or widened by the owner's restoration decision (§20.8); the dialogue rows each one restores are listed in `docs/reviews/reader-dialogue-coverage.md`.

- **RW1a. Inquiry.** Port the unmerged `origin/feat/reader-clarification-branches` (branching Ask); split the rail into components; inquiry status on the existing outstanding-question queue; a return stack for every detour, shown as a detour strip, with `Alt+←` back; a reconnect line with a passage-scoped quick check; the reader report's workflow funnel. *(Restored:)* a friction audit before and after; one interaction model on every source kind the Reader opens. *(UX integration:)* the shared voice and evidence components, the static quick-check marker that replaces today's pulse, and the experience gates (§4.1).
- **RW1b. Intent box and active reading.** A selection intent box routed by the Decider (the palette stays one key away); "Let me try the next step" and "Why does this step work?" on a selected derivation or worked example. *(Restored:)* the five learner intentions organize the actions; "What does this assume?", "Another way to see this" and "Reconstruct this later"; missed answers become learner text (`reader_formative`). *(UX integration:)* the box appears unfocused and typing focuses it; selection never switches the rail tab; routing starts when typing pauses; the book's next lines fold while the learner tries the next step (§4.2).
- **RW1c. A tutor that looks things up** *(restored)*. In the Reader, the tutor retrieves from the learner's sources on its own, answers in three modes (the source's own words, a grounded explanation, a bridge to the learner's work), says so when the books don't cover the question, offers "another way" and "that didn't help" through the sources spec's intents, and renders the sources spec's tool requests (practice, passages, comparison) plus two Reader buttons (figures, try first) that the learner presses.
- **RW2a. The workbench as a resumable scratchpad.** Step-by-step work beside the source, saved as it is typed, reopened by "Resume this problem" after navigation or restart; Submit through the ordinary attempt path with the source-visible rule (§5.4). No model calls of its own. *(UX integration:)* it takes half the window; it opens at once with the verbatim stem while an import binds in the background; whether the book starts visible follows the entry's purpose; Abandon is reversible; back from feedback returns to the problem (§5.1).
- **RW2b. Checks and adaptive help.** "Check my work" (first questionable step), the help ladder with the reconciliation contract, the adaptive first offer, symbolic checks, "this doesn't connect to my work" feedback on every hint, workbench text into the learner-text-gaps pipeline. *(Restored:)* a discriminating question on the first rung when rival explanations are open; the check's error-kind guess, step dependencies and assumptions; more transformation kinds; in-session updates. *(Adaptive hints, §20.11:)* hints come from the adaptive-hints spec's service (its H3): kinds per level, waypoints with a horizon and labelled subgoal plans, placed on its approach maps; "Check my work" names the map node each valid step reaches; the first offer is sampled from the chooser's probabilities with the propensity logged; the "doesn't connect" shortcuts have defined effects.
- **RW2c. What helps this learner** *(restored)*. A replayable record of which help and which passage preceded recovery, and of where the learner's work tends to go off, read by the hint writer and the chooser; a logged draw between equally good passages; worked examples that take the learner's own route (the sources spec's `worked_example` intent); "Compare methods" after a correct answer.
- **RW2d. Workbench signals in the learner field** *(restored; algorithm successor)*. Mid-work stuck steps and help before work become field signals, live behind a kill switch.
- **RW3a. Margin cues from the Guide.** The Guide's existing `recent_misunderstanding` passages shown as restrained margin marks; the Guide already reads the misconception from the diagnoser's overlay on `main` (`fix/guide-overlay`, merged at `f2f02e32`). *(UX integration:)* each mark says why it is there; at most one LearnLoop mark per screenful, on top of the learner's density setting.
- **RW3b. Discovered cues.** New passage links found per open hypothesis, named gap or demonstrated facet through T6 retrieval and a listwise judge; a logged holdout. *(Restored:)* cues ordered by the probability that their target is still open; a "Try this step first" cue on worked examples; source-authored contrasts preferred; a learner density control.
- **RW4a. Library questions and source comparison.** "Ask" in library search, answered beside the open source: passages grouped by source and role with a one-line contribution each, the tutor on request; pin passages from different sources and compare them through the sources spec's `compare_sources`; "Practice this question" through `find_practice` (with the sources spec's candidates once merged). *(Restored:)* prerequisite and application passages through the concept graph; facet targets.
- **RW4b. Exercises from my sources.** The Reader's view of the sources spec's textbook-exercise candidates: passage, page and "use this one". Until that spec merges, the learner-initiated fallback search described in §7.4.
- **RW4d. Syntopic reading, kept** *(restored)*. The comparison is the sources spec's `compare_sources`, callable by the learner and, through that spec's tool requests, by the tutor. The Reader keeps source claims, LearnLoop's inferences and the learner's own take apart on screen; "Keep this comparison" stores proposals in the existing `source_objects` and `source_object_relations` layer.
- **RW4e. Instructional roles** *(restored)*. Several roles per passage (including counterexample, contrast, intuition, prerequisite, alternative treatment), with confidence and provenance, accumulated as `source_objects` proposals from the Reader's own judges, plus an owner-run batch labeller; used as positive retrieval filters through the sources spec's `roles` argument.
- **RW5a. Explorable figures.** "Make this explorable" on a selected equation, figure or region of a figure: a declarative, verified figure spec drawn by a trusted renderer, inline under its passage, with predictions. *(Restored:)* shaded areas with integral readouts, PMF bars and sums, parameter animation, region selection, "what changed?" answers — enough for the dialogue's two-sided exponential. *(Re-review:)* what the figure demonstrates and what it assumes; "not right?" asks for a better version.
- **RW5b. More figure kinds and offered figures** *(restored)*. Heat maps, vector fields and trajectories (objectives, gradients, attention-like weights, dynamical systems); figures offered by the tutor and the library when a retrieved equation can support one.
- **RW5d. Show me** *(2026-10-25)* — see `spec_reader_show_me_figures_v1.md`.
- **RW6a. "Last time here".** A deterministic recap on returning to a source: last position, open inquiries, unfinished problems, missed predictions, with resume links. *(Restored:)* the session's formative records (`ReaderObservationV1`); an optional "Wrap up" in under a minute. *(UX integration:)* "Since last time" shows what the learner has since demonstrated; the wrap-up offer appears where the learner lands, not in the Reader being closed.
- **RW6b. Digest, router card and cold follow-ups.** An optional narrative over the recap, reader activity in the router's learner-state card, and cold follow-ups served through DL2's "Check once". Follow-ups have no count cap: the existing value-of-information machinery (probe EIG over the learner's open hypotheses, `scheduling/evsi.py` for the next decision) chooses, numbers and dates them, and the daily plan's finite minutes and the spend caps bound them (§9.3; owner decision 2026-10-12, §20.7). *(Restored:)* formative questions; textbook exercises and typed intents from the sources spec; discounting what reading already showed (the sources spec's exposure rule); novelty labels; background generation when nothing fits; reopening the Reader where the topic was studied; open hypotheses and candidate actions on the card. *(UX integration:)* scheduled checks as a dated timeline with no totals; undo with an optional reason, kept as calibration data.
- **RW7a. Reading paths in the Guide** *(restored from the deferred §10)*. At the depth the learner picks, each section is core, prerequisite, enrichment, already shown or assumed, with a reason, overrides and one exercise worth trying. Display only.
- **RW7b. The plan follows the path** *(restored; algorithm successor)*. DL1's reading block opens the next core or prerequisite section, behind a kill switch.

**Consumed from `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md` (at `ae82eb08`), never designed here:** the tutor's practice request and `find_practice`'s callers (S0); textbook exercises as practice candidates, verified and attached to families (S1); `PracticeConstraints.intent`, `target_hypotheses` and the typed filters, the valuation adapter `value_candidates` and the exposure rule (S2a); `find_sources` with its intents and policies, and `compare_sources` (S3); the tutor's `tool_requests` and `practice_availability` (S4); the intervention records (S5). Each consuming phase works without it and gains it when it merges. Where a Reader phase needs one of these before the sources spec has built it, the Reader phase builds it once, to the sources spec's design and in that spec's module, and the sources spec extends it (§18, G53).

**Deferred, labelled, with what each would take and a trigger (§17):** generated-code figures in an isolated surface (including whole-paper interactive systems); page-image retrieval; an OS-level or MCP learning service; local rerankers; learned cue policies (the learned hint chooser is the adaptive-hints spec's); formative signals as numeric observations; full step-level solution graphs with every branch (approach maps now give routes at subgoal level, §20.11); formal verification of proof steps; cross-learner data-driven hints; a learned, causal model of which help works for this learner; a randomized whole-workflow experiment; claim extraction over the whole library with a claim matcher; a CAS calculator; photo-of-work input; a read-only recap on the phone; randomized hint-quality experiments (not planned). **Owned by other specs** (§17.1): the router's availability pass is now built by the sources spec (S4's `practice_availability`), with its router wiring deferred there; the hint service, its approach maps, a learned hint chooser and unsolicited hints are the adaptive-hints spec's; and the other ideas the dialogue raised that live in other specs are listed with their owners so none is silently absent.

---

## 2. Standing constraints

- **W1. Reading and help are never evidence; what the learner says may steer.** This restates P3 invariant 6 ("Reading is not evidence", `spec_p3_reader_integration.md` §1.1, with §8.2's firewall) and the learner field's F2 ("Never evidence") and F3 ("Live changes go through the playbook", `spec_learner_field_v1.md` §1), and follows the field's own guard: "Signals change routing and predictor state, never evidence, mastery, certification or the demonstrated record" (§3.1). Signals fall in three tiers, as the dialogue proposed (round 2 §6):

  | Tier | What | May change | Never |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1. Engagement | Dwell, scrolling, highlights, opening a passage, cue taps, figure play, inquiry status, recaps, digests and narratives | Retrieval context, suggestions, display. `authority_class = 'salience_only'` (P3 §8.2) | Anything in the next column, or any hypothesis |
  | 2. Formative | Explicit learner answers and learner text that are not graded attempts: questions, "what's still unclear?", next-step, why-step and assumption answers, figure predictions and "what changed?" answers, "Check my work" verdicts on unfinished work, hints taken mid-problem, "this doesn't connect" text, answers to a discriminating question, "not relevant, because…" on a cue | **Hypotheses:** learner-text gaps names candidate prerequisites and misconceptions from the text, as it already does for reader questions. **Routing:** the hint chooser, the next hint, cue targets, `ReaderActivityV1`, the field's question signals. **Follow-up valuation:** the open questions RW6b values (§9.3) | The **mastery posterior** (the facet and LO estimator), `grading_evidence`, `facet_recall_state`, `facet_capability_evidence`, certification, coldness, FSRS state and the demonstrated record. No numeric update of any posterior: tier-2 rows write no `probe_observations` and no estimator input. Numeric weak observations are deferred (§17). (What the learner was *shown* is still recorded as exposures and reveals, as today; that is a fact about the material, not a signal from the learner) |
  | 3. Independent performance | A submitted attempt, graded blind, carrying the assistance it recorded (§5.4) | Evidence, as today | — |

  - **The only evidence a workbench session produces is its final submitted attempt.** Abandoned work, checks and hints stay tier 2.
  - **Changing what is served is F3's business.** Tier-2 inputs to content (named gaps, follow-up commitments the learner can undo, cue marks) follow the learner-text-gaps precedent and take no successor. A tier-2 signal that changes serving, such as workbench signals feeding Phase 6's fast-track rule (RW2d) or reading paths steering the plan (RW7b), is an algorithm successor with a kill switch and receipts.
  - **"Posterior" in P3 invariant 6 means the mastery posterior.** The learner field's harness arms may use typed questions as weak predictor observations (field §3.1 item 2, activated only under its §7.4); W1 does not forbid what that spec plans.
  - **Mechanically:** no tier-1 or tier-2 row has a path to the evidence ingestion API (P3 §8.2), and the salience-firewall test covers every new payload `event` and every new learner-text source kind.
- **W2. Honest labels.** Every surface distinguishes source-authored, AI-written and learner-written content (P3 invariant 9), with the UX document's three voices: a glyph, a rule style and a spoken prefix for the source (`§`), LearnLoop's model (`◇`) and the learner (`›`), so the distinction survives greyscale and screen readers (UX §3.4). Model judgments are labelled as such. "Checked" or "verified" appears only when a non-LLM check (a sandboxed sympy check, an exact key, a quote match) passed. Evidence status uses the knowledge well's discrete markers, never a percentage: `○` untested, `◌` worked with help (a check on your own pending), `●` demonstrated on your own (`KnowledgeWellView.tsx:446`). A cue never says the learner *is* confused, only that a passage connects to something they worked on. No surface promises a check the valuation has not scheduled.
- **W3. Generous defaults, live behind kill switches.** Every phase ships on by default with its switch. No step is added to protect a model, and no confirmation dialog guards a safe action: consequences are stated in the control's label before it is pressed (UX §8).
- **W4. LLM judgment and value of information over constants.** First-offer help (its level, kind and, for a waypoint, horizon) and cue relevance are model judgments over typed learner state. Follow-ups and discriminating questions are valued by the existing EIG and EVSI machinery (§9.3, §5.2); a model judges only whether an item tests the capability and what each rival explanation predicts. Cues are ordered by the probability, read from existing state, that their target is still open (§6.2). Numbers in this spec are caps on money, time or display, never thresholds that decide learning. There is no count cap on follow-ups (§20.7).
- **W5. Learner text over skip buttons.** The intent box, "what's still unclear?", "I'm stuck" in the workbench, "this doesn't connect to my work" on a hint, "not relevant, because…" on cues, "what changed?" on a figure and "why?" on undoing a scheduled check take free text. A one-tap path still exists (an empty box is allowed), as with "I'm stuck". Every such box carries "where this goes" (UX §4.10), worded for that box: whether a model may read it to name a gap (questions, stuck and "doesn't connect" text, discriminating answers, missed formative answers), or what it is used for instead ("not relevant" judges the cue, §6.2; undo's "why?" values checks, §9.3; "your take" is a learner relation, §7.8; a figure's "not right?" goes to the figure author, §8.1), and that it never grades.
- **W6. Embeddings shortlist, judges decide.** No embedding score alone creates a cue, a role or a match (R1). Shortlists are top-k; a judge decides everything shown.
- **W7. Cold means no source cue.** P3 invariant 10 and `find_sources`' policies (§7.2): a cold attempt gets no source, hint, tutor or figure; opening one ends cold status. A source-visible attempt never certifies.
- **W8. Grading stays learner-blind.** The grader sees the final answer as today. Steps, hints and their kinds, approach maps, checks and learner history never enter `BlindGradingContext` (the adaptive-hints spec's standing constraint H2 says the same).
- **W9. Replay makes zero model and embedding calls.** Every paid output (checks, hints, cues, organizer lines, figures, digests) is persisted and never regenerated on rebuild.
- **W10. Spend controls.** Every paid step uses the Phase 1 framework (`learnloop/ai/spend.py`): per-route caps, `--dry-run` on every batch command, receipts. Codex is `estimated_only`. Every LLM, Decider, verifier or learner-card verdict appends to `judgment_ledger`.
- **W11. Agency.** The learner can always ask for more help, turn cues off, hide the source before working, and undo every automatic action. Every LearnLoop-initiated element can be switched off from the element itself, and a switched-off feature leaves no disabled control behind (UX U13). Every learner action has a keyboard path (UX §3.5).
- **W12. The reading hot path never waits for a model** (P3 invariant 1). Cue links and organizer lines are computed in the background or on request and appear when ready. **The attention budget** (UX §4.12): in the reading and working postures nothing LearnLoop starts moves focus, opens a modal or a toast, animates, blinks or plays a sound; a result that lands never moves focus; selecting text never changes the rail tab (with `reader_intent.enabled`; off restores today's flip to Notes); skim mode mutes every mark and offer LearnLoop starts.
- **W13. Reuse before building.** One retrieval substrate (T6), one listwise judge (`practice_index/listwise.judge_listwise`), one practice-proposal function (`find_practice`, extended only by the sources spec), one source-practice backend (`spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md`: `find_sources`, `compare_sources`, the tool interface, the contamination policy and the exposure rule are its design), one question producer (the quick-check producer), one learner-text compiler (learner-text gaps), one scheduled-check mechanism (DL2's "Check once"), one value-of-information machinery (probe EIG in `diagnosis/probe_families.py` and `diagnosis/probes.py`, EVSI in `scheduling/evsi.py`, reached through one candidate-valuation adapter, the sources spec's `instructional/valuation.py:value_candidates`), one per-source object layer (`source_objects`, P3 §7), one hint service (`learnloop/hints/`, the adaptive-hints spec's: one writer, one chooser, one assists table and its approach maps for every surface). A phase that needs a second one stops and records why.
- **W14. Every model-backed action has defined states and a landing place** (UX §4.0): pending (with a measured "usually about N s" only when the report has a p50 for the route, and cancel), landed, failed (the learner's input kept), model unreachable, sidecar down, capped (naming the route's daily cap and what still works) and switched off (absent). Each result lands in a known place: an Ask answer in its exchange, a check and a hint under their steps, a bridge under its hint, organizer lines beside their hits, a figure under its passage, the narrative in the recap.

---

**Successor numbering.** `SUCCESSOR_CHAIN` (`algorithm_versions.py:99`) is linear. DL1 is `mvp-0.14` on `main`; R1 takes `mvp-0.15` and learner-text gaps `mvp-0.16` in the merge chain. New vaults start at `LATEST_ALGORITHM_VERSION`. This spec needs **two** successors, both restored phases that change what is served: RW2d (workbench field signals) and RW7b (the plan follows a reading path) (§12). Each takes the next free `mvp-0.N` tag at its merge, never a named tag. If a phase agent or its reviewer finds another is needed, the same rule applies.

### 3.4 Corrections to the first draft (found by the integration review)

1. **The Guide misses the diagnoser's statements under the split.** `_learner_signal` reads `repository.active_errors_by_learning_object` (`db/repositories.py:1966`), which selects raw `error_events`. Since Slice 1 M2, a blind-graded attempt's events carry only the grader's localization; the misconception statement is laid over from the live diagnosis by `error_events_for_attempt` (`:1978`). On split vaults the Guide therefore falls back to the error type ("Revisit this to resolve: conceptual error"). **Fixed on `main`** by `fix/guide-overlay` (merged at `f2f02e32`): `Repository.active_errors_by_learning_object_overlaid` (`db/repositories.py:1977`) is the per-LO counterpart of the overlay, the Guide reads it (`reader_guidance.py:142`), and its tests (`tests/test_reader_guidance.py`, `tests/test_active_errors_overlay.py`) hold G30. RW3a's margin marks read the same helper and carry no fix of their own.
2. **Source blocks already have instructional roles.** Ingest assigns `role_hint` deterministically, and T6 already excludes `exercise` and `solution` blocks in practice and feedback contexts. The first draft's LLM role index over every section duplicated this; it is cut (§7.1).
3. **A source-visible reading question is not an attempt.** `administer_reading_question` plus `submit_reading_question` writes an interaction event; no grader or diagnoser runs. A Reader workbench submission must go through `submit_attempt` instead (§5.4).
4. **New interaction-event kinds need a table rebuild.** `interaction_events.kind` has a CHECK, last widened by migration 103 with a full temp-then-rename rebuild. This spec adds **no** event kind; it uses existing kinds with a payload `event` field (§4.4).
5. **`passage_gap` and `same_claim` as T1 match kinds do not fit.** T1's judge answers `same` / `different` / `unsure` about a pair. A cue is a relevance judgment of passages for a target, which is what T6's listwise judge already does. RW3b uses T6 plus `judge_listwise` (§6.2).
6. **No LaTeX parser is installed.** sympy 1.14 is a dependency, but neither `lark` nor `antlr4` is, so `parse_latex` is unavailable. Symbolic checks use PF's pattern (the model writes a sympy check, the sandbox runs it with a negative control) on a narrow set of transformations (§5.3).
7. **`find_practice` cannot express "unseen" or "not a family revealed today".** `PracticeConstraints` gains exact filters for RW6b (§9.3). The sources spec's S2a now designs them with the rest of its typed constraints (`unattempted_only`, `exclude_family_ids`, `exclude_revealed_families`, `facet_match`, `max_minutes`; its §8.1); RW6b uses those names.
8. **`search_sources` is taken.** `learnloop/reader/source_search.py:42` already defines `search_sources` (the legacy keyword search). The new function stays `find_sources`; router §15.3's `search_sources(query)` names it (§18).
9. **Every new config key moves the defaults fingerprint.** `DEFAULTS_SNAPSHOT_BY_ALGORITHM` (`config/template.py:817`) pins the effective defaults per version. Content-only phases recompute the latest entry with a recorded "no scoring, scheduling, projection or serving change" note, as Phase 1, project understanding M1 and T7 did. Branches that merge later recompute again.
10. **Reveal kinds need a table rebuild too.** A new `workbench_hint` kind would rebuild `reveal_events` as migration 179 did. A workbench hint is tutor prose, so it uses the existing `tutor_answer` kind with a distinguishing `basis` (§5.6).
11. **Library questions require a note.** `ask_question` in the `library` context needs `note_id` (`tutor_qa.py:355`). The cross-source tutor in RW4a uses the `reader` context with a pinned-span provider (§7.3).
12. **T6 latency.** On the linear_algebra copy a T6-grounded tutor answer waited about 7 s for retrieval alone (rewrite 1.8 s, embedding 0.8 s, verification 3.9 s; `docs/reviews/slice-2-t6.md` open question 2). Workbench bridging therefore runs after the hint is shown, never before it (§5.3).

*Found by the restoration (2026-10-12):*

13. **Learner-text source kinds have a CHECK.** `learner_text_gaps.source_kind` and `learner_text_gap_jobs.source_kind` accept only `tutor_question`, `stuck_answer` and `diagnosis` (`migrations/177_learner_text_gaps.sql:19`, `:88` on `learner/text-gaps`), and three tables reference `learner_text_gaps` by foreign key. The first draft's `workbench_stuck` adapter would have failed its insert. This spec adds two kinds, `workbench_text` (stuck steps, "doesn't connect" text, answers to a discriminating question) and `reader_formative` (missed next-step, why-step and assumption answers, "what still isn't clear" after a reconnect check, missed figure predictions with their "what changed?" text), in **one** widening migration: temp-then-rename with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF`, a `foreign_key_check` before commit, and a durability-lane test. It lands in the first phase that adds an adapter (RW1b if learner-text gaps has merged by then, else RW2b). The dispatcher `learner_text()` (`sources.py:431` on the branch) gains the two kinds.
14. **`source_objects.object_type` has no counterexample, contrast or intuition.** The CHECK (`094_source_objects.sql:28`) admits eight types; `authorial_role` is free text. RW4e stores the finer instructional role in `authorial_role` and maps the type (worked example → `worked_example`, exercise → `problem`, definition or theorem → `definition` or `claim`, everything else → `motif_or_passage`). No rebuild.
15. **Relation types are coarser than a comparison.** `source_object_relations.relation_type` has no "equivalent" or "different assumptions". RW4d maps the sources spec's comparison relations (its §9.4) as `equivalent` → `supports` (or `alternate_definition` between two definitions), `different_assumptions`, `different_notation` and `complementary` → `refines`, `disagreement` → `contradicts`, `unclear` → `unresolved`. The fine label stays on the comparison row (`source_comparisons.comparison_json`), and `library_asks.kept_json` maps each kept relation id to its fine label (relations have no content column, `094_source_objects.sql:57`). No rebuild.

*Found by the UX integration and the re-review (2026-10-13):*

16. **Quick checks record no match verdict.** `record_action` stores the learner's response and `status = 'answered'` (`reader_quick_check.py:156`); nothing says whether it matched. RW1b's missed answers need one, so RW1a's migration on `reader_authored_questions` also adds `self_check TEXT CHECK (self_check IS NULL OR self_check IN ('matches', 'partly', 'no'))`.
17. **The sources spec's `find_sources` has no `post_attempt` value and no graph expansion** (its §9.1: policies `reading`, `assisted_practice`, `cold_attempt` and the internal `authoring`; `post_attempt` narrowed to "assisted for the attempted item, reading otherwise", its §5.1). This spec's restored `post_attempt` policy is therefore withdrawn: RW2c's "Compare methods" reads the item's own key, which feedback already shows (`FeedbackScreen.tsx:2139`), and, for an imported textbook exercise, the book's solution through the item's `canonical_exercise` (the sources spec's §7.4), by id rather than by retrieval (§5.10). RW4a composes the graph expansion as its caller (§7.2) and asks the sources spec for an `expand` argument (§18).
18. **Selecting text moves the rail today** (`ReaderScreen.tsx:1094`, `:1237`, `:2127` flip it to Notes). Under the intent box it doesn't (§4.2, G57); with `reader_intent.enabled = false` today's behaviour returns.
19. **The quick-check signal animates without end** (`app.css:1225`–`:1242`). RW1a replaces it with a static marker for every learner, not only under reduced motion (§4.1). It is a display fix, like the guide-overlay fix, and needs no switch.

### 3.5 What the dialogue asks for that does not exist

- No branching or status on Reader inquiries beyond the flat `resolution` column.
- No workspace for solving: no step capture, no resumable work beside the source, no work-aligned hints, no localization before submission.
- No passage-level cue; the Guide's misunderstanding reason never reaches the text.
- Library search returns a ranked list without per-source contributions; nothing compares sources.
- No interactive figures.
- No re-entry recap, no reader input to the router, no follow-up check after assisted work.
- The tutor in the Reader sees only the selection and its neighbours (`tutor_qa.py:1299`, `_reader_source_spans`); it never forms its own query.
- Source objects and their relations are written as proposals but read by no retrieval or UI.
- No reading path says what a section is for.

---

## 4. RW1 — Inquiry, the intent box and active reading

### 4.1 RW1a: what the learner sees

**Presentation:** UX §3.1–3.3 (postures, transitions, what persists), §3.4 (voices and evidence markers, built here as shared components), §3.5 (Reader-wide keys), §4.2 (inquiry, detour strip, reconnect), §4.12 (the attention budget), §7 (accessibility); wireframes W1, W3, W4.

**Branching Ask.** Port `origin/feat/reader-clarification-branches` onto `main`, keeping its contract:
- `reader.ask.parentExchangeId` is the parent's `readerAnswerEventId`;
- the parent's spans override the current selection;
- only ancestors enter the prompt (the latest 12, with omitted counts in the manifest);
- the rail shows the tree, with search, expand, "new thread" and return-to-passage.
- The port composes with T6: a follow-up's retrieval query is the follow-up's own question; its ancestors are context only. Resolve the three conflicts (`reader_dialogue.py`, `tutor_qa.py`, `handlers/reader.py`) against T6's injected span provider and, if it has merged first, learner-text gaps' `learner_gap` fields.

**Inquiries.** An inquiry is a root exchange and its descendants.
- **The detour strip** (the dialogue's "small breadcrumb trail", round 1 A). While a detour exists, one line between the header and the body shows the trail: `↩ §5.4 ¶3 › why weighted by p? › conditioning on a coin flip`. Every crumb is a button that opens that exchange and scrolls the source to its passage. It stays visible in every posture, so the current inquiry shows while the learner works a problem, as in the dialogue's workbench layout ("Current inquiry: Mixture CDF → Conditioning on component → Integration bounds").
- **The return stack.** Every detour pushes an entry: a branch, a peek at a cited passage, opening another source, entering the workbench, a library answer. `Alt+←` and `Alt+→` walk it like an editor's back and forward; the first crumb is always "where I was before this detour", the restored position and selection. It is reader UX state, not source truth (P3 §12): it lives for the visit, and a restart restores only its root, P3's reading position.
- **"Back to where I was"** is the first crumb and `Alt+←` to it (`reader_source_restored`).
- **Status reuses what exists.** The root's `question_events.resolution` is the inquiry's status, so open inquiries already appear in Today's outstanding-question queue. "Revisit once" maps to the existing `test_me_later` preset on the root's anchor and "Make it something I can do" to `help_me_remember`. No new status vocabulary. **Reopen** adds `open` to the resolve RPC's accepted values (no schema change).

**Reconnect.** When a child branch is marked resolved, or the learner returns to the root passage through the strip or `Alt+←`, one line asks: *your question "why weighted by p?" — does this make sense now?* It quotes the learner's own words (UX U3) and shows in two places: dim, under the root passage, but only on a return the learner started (the one inline line LearnLoop ever adds to the text column, UX S16), and at the top of the inquiry in the rail.
- **Yes** resolves the root and is logged as an explicit self-report (used by §15's calibration block).
- **A text box, "What's still unclear?",** starts a new branch from the root with that text as its question (W5). Being a reader question, it reaches learner-text gaps with no new adapter.
- **"Quick check"** asks the quick-check producer for one formative question scoped to the root passage (§4.3). Answered, the expected answer is revealed beside the learner's for a self-check (*matches · partly · no*); it is never an attempt; "add to practice" escalates it through the existing path. "Partly" and "no" make the answer a `reader_formative` learner text (tier 2, W1; adapter in RW1b), so the pipeline can name what was missing. The label is "quick check", not "check me": in this spec "check me" always means an ordinary attempt on your own (§6.1), and the two must not share a name (D43).
- Ignoring the line does nothing; it disappears when the learner scrolls more than a screen on (W12), and nothing is recorded.

**The shared pieces every later phase uses** (built here because the rail split touches every component):
- **Voices and markers** (W2; UX §3.4): one component each for source text, model text and learner text, with glyph, rule, label and screen-reader prefix, and one for the evidence markers `○ ◌ ●` and the check markers. Later phases render model text only through the model-voice component (G55).
- **The static quick-check marker** (UX S12; §3.4 item 19): the Guide tab shows `◆` and the number of waiting checks, statically; the pulsing tab, the blinking diamond and the pinned uppercase strip are removed for every learner.
- **Meaning-bearing copy** (labels such as "model's reading", "counts as one hint", "not checked") uses the `--dim` token, never `--faint`, which is about 3.5:1 on the default background (`#666778` on `#0e0e0e`, `app.css:4`, `:11`), under WCAG's 4.5:1 for small text. The rail split moves today's `--faint` microcopy as it goes (G55).
- **The model-action states** of W14, as one component every model-backed action uses (G56).

**Splitting the screen.** RW1a moves the rail into components (`screens/reader/AskRail.tsx`, `GuideRail.tsx`, `NotesRail.tsx`) after the port lands, so later phases don't fight over one 4,000-line file. It is a move, not a redesign: the existing `ReaderScreen` tests pass unchanged, plus the branch's.

**The reader report** (`learnloop reader report [--json] [--since]`, no model calls, no learner text) reports **workflows, with denominators**, not only counts:
- **selection → action → usable result → back to reading:** selections; actions invoked by kind; for model-backed actions, queued, completed, failed (with reason), cancelled and latency (p50/p95, model wait separated from learner time); whether the learner returned to the source passage within the visit;
- **inquiry:** inquiries opened, branch depth and breadth, resolved, dismissed, reopened, still open after 7 days; reconnect answers (yes / still unclear / quick check);
- **detours** (UX §9.2): the **return rate** (the share of detours — a branch, a peek, another source opened — that end back at the origin passage within the visit) and the **detour cost** (minutes away, model wait separated);
- **proactive load** (UX §9.2): elements LearnLoop started per reading hour (marks shown, reconnect lines, section-boundary offers, quick checks waiting), against the learner's density setting;
- **waiting behaviour** (UX §9.2): the share of model waits during which the learner scrolled, selected or typed;
- **visits:** visits, active minutes (idle excluded), time to first action after opening a source.

Each later phase adds its own funnel (§15). Model-backed rows carry model and prompt version so a report can be read per route.

**A friction audit before adding interface** (the dialogue's P0, restored). Before RW1a changes any UI, its agent writes a friction audit of today's Reader into the phase's review file: for six core workflows (ask about a selection and get back to reading; follow one prerequisite and return; import and work an exercise; find where else an idea is explained; capture "test me later"; resume after a restart), the number of steps, which of them work from the keyboard (against the UX key map, §3.5 there), where the learner waits on a model, where focus moves or the rail changes without the learner's act, and where context is lost. It is read from the code and, once the funnel has data, from the funnel. Every later phase's review file repeats the audit for the workflows it touched, so the friction a phase adds is visible. The owner's timed walkthrough (§15) stays optional; the audit needs no owner time.

**One interaction model on every source kind** (round 1's "consistent across books, PDFs, videos and notes", restored). Selection, the intent box, inquiry, cues, library questions, the recap and the workbench work wherever the Reader resolves a selection to a span: Marker markdown, the original-page view of a PDF, and video transcripts (`reader.watch_plan`). On the learner's own notes pane the intent box offers only the actions that need no source anchor (ask, test me later, help me remember, connect it). Figures from a transcript take the transcript text and no image. Each phase's tests include one transcript fixture.

### 4.2 RW1b: what the learner sees

**Presentation:** UX §4.1 (the intent box), §4.3 (active reading), §4.10 (where your words go); wireframe W2.

**The intent box.** Selecting text shows a small box just below the selection (above it near the bottom of the view): "What do you want to do with this?", with a key row *↵ ask · tab actions · esc*. It appears about 150 ms after the mouse is released, so a drag in progress never flickers it.
- **It does not take focus** (UX S3; the dialogue's guard against "unpredictable text selection", round 1 §4). Scroll keys keep scrolling, `Ctrl+C` copies, and the first printable key focuses the box and is typed into it. Enter and Tab work without typing. `?` with a selection focuses it.
- **Selecting never changes the rail tab** (today it flips to Notes, §3.4 item 18). The rail changes only when the learner acts; a routed Ask opens the Ask tab because the learner asked (D41).
- The learner types in their own words ("why are the densities weighted?", "quiz me on this later", "make this a problem I can work"). Enter routes it.
- **Tab** (or clicking "actions") shows the palette, grouped by intention (below), with today's presets, shortcuts and right-click tag menu unchanged in behaviour. Options for phases that haven't landed are absent, not disabled.
- An empty Enter runs Ask with no model call.
- The routed action shows as one line before it runs, for example "→ asking · help me reason · change". "change" cancels it and opens the palette with the text kept. There is no confirmation step.
- **Where it doesn't appear:** for a selection inside the rail or the workbench; in skim mode unless the learner presses `?` or Tab. It closes on Esc or a click elsewhere, recording nothing. While it shows, the Reader's single-key shortcuts are off.

**Routing** is one typed `ChoiceQuestion` to the Decider (route `ai.routing.reader_intent`, added to `DECIDER_TASKS`: never a chat fallback). The options are the actions that exist when the call is made: ask (with its answer mode); worked example, explain differently, why it matters; remember, test me later, connect it, mark confusing, not worth remembering; import as exercise; and, as later phases land, "work through this", "let me try the next step", "why does this step work", "what does this assume", "another way to see this", "reconstruct this later", "compare sources", "practice this question", "make this explorable", "what matters here", "what do I need first" and "where is this used".
- The learner's text becomes the question (for asks) or the annotation's learner text (for everything else).
- **If the Decider is unavailable,** times out (`reader_intent.timeout_seconds`, 4) or is spent out, the action is Ask.
- **Routing while the learner pauses** (UX S4). Up to four seconds between Enter and anything happening is too slow for the most frequent interaction, so the box routes speculatively once typing pauses for `reader_intent.speculate_after_ms` (600). On Enter, a routing for the unchanged text is used at once; a changed text is routed again. Speculation runs only on non-empty text that changed since the last routing, counts against `reader_intent.max_calls_per_day`, and stops for the day once 80% of that cap is used (Enter still routes). Kill switch `reader_intent.speculative_enabled` (off: route on Enter only).
- **Verdicts.** Every routing appends to `judgment_ledger` (task `reader_intent`, judge `decider`), with `used` or `discarded` in its rationale for speculative calls. A "change" appends the learner's correction (judge `learner_card`), so routing error stays measurable without a review chore; the report counts it on used routings only.

**Five intentions organize the actions** (round 1 §1, restored). The dialogue's point was that what the learner is trying to do matters more than which panel is open. The palette and the Decider's options are grouped by intention, and the routed line names it ("Understand · asking · help me reason · change"). This spec fixes the set of actions; the grouping and labels follow the UX document (§4.1 there), and a sixth group, **Mark**, holds what describes the passage rather than an intention:

| Group | Actions |
|---|---|
| **Orient**: what matters, where to start, what I need first | "What matters here?" opens the Guide's reading path (RW7a); "What do I need first?" lists the passage's prerequisite LOs with their status and a prerequisite passage each (RW4a's graph expansion) |
| **Understand** | Ask (with its answer mode); explain differently; why does this step work?; what does this assume?; another way to see this; how do my sources treat this? (compare); make this explorable |
| **Apply** | Work through this; let me try the next step; practice this question; add this exercise to practice (import); find an exercise in my sources (RW4b) |
| **Consolidate** | Revisit once (test me later); help me remember; reconstruct this later; quick check on this passage |
| **Transfer** | Where else is this explained? (a library ask); where is this used? (a library ask with the applications expansion, RW4a); practice this in a new setting (`find_practice` with the session's facets and `unattempted_only`); connect it; why it matters |
| **Mark** | Highlight; note; confusing; not worth remembering |

The Decider returns the intention with the action (Mark actions carry none); the report counts actions by intention (§15). The grouping changes no action's effect (G3).

**Active reading on derivations and worked examples** (the dialogue's "predict the next step" and "justify this step"). Two selection actions, also offered in the intent box:
- **"Let me try the next step."** The producer writes the question "What comes next after this line?" from the selection and the following blocks, keeping the next step as the expected answer. **The book's next lines fold** while the learner tries (UX S5), or the answer would sit right under the question: in the extracted-text view the following blocks collapse under a dotted rule (*the next 3 lines are folded while you try · show*); on the original-page view an opaque cover hides the page below the selection's region. The fold shows at once, before the question has been written (*writing the question — the lines stay folded*, with a one-key unfold). The learner writes their attempt; then the source's next step is shown beside it and the fold opens.
- **"Why does this step work?"** The producer asks for the justification of the selected step, with the source's or the model's justification as the expected answer, labelled accordingly.

Both are formative quick-check rows (§4.3), answered as today's quick checks are: no attempt, "add to practice" to escalate. The learner sees the next step only after answering or pressing "show me", then self-checks: *matches · partly · no* (stored in `self_check`, §3.4 item 16).

*Restored (round 1's "justify steps, identify assumptions, compare alternative methods" and "delayed reconstruction"):*
- **"What does this assume?"** The producer asks which assumptions the selected step or result relies on (style `assumptions`), with the source's stated conditions, or the model's, as the expected answer, labelled accordingly.
- **"Another way to see this."** Calls `find_sources(intent="alternative", exclude_span_keys=[the open passage])` from the sources spec with the selection as the query, and shows the best one or two passages from other sources or other roles (a geometric view beside an algebraic one) with RW4a's contribution line, labelled "from a different book" or "a worked example instead". Until that intent merges, it is RW4a's "where else is this explained?" with the open passage excluded.
- **"Reconstruct this later."** On a derivation or worked example, the producer writes a `reconstruct` question ("Derive … without looking at the book") with the source's derivation as the expected answer. It is escalated to a practice item through the quick-check's existing escalation, and a `test_me_later` commitment on that item is created, so DL2 serves it as a delayed, cold reconstruction. The receipt says so ("In a few days you'll be asked to rebuild this derivation without the book · undo") and is undoable like any capture.

**Missed answers are learner text** (tier 2, W1). When the learner self-checks a next-step, why-step or assumption answer as "partly" or "no", the answer, the selection and the expected answer become a `reader_formative` learner text (§3.4 item 13). Learner-text gaps names what was missing (a prerequisite, a belief) exactly as it does for reader questions. Matched answers write nothing new. Until learner-text gaps merges, the adapter is not built and the answers stay in the report.

### 4.3 Data

- **Branches:** no table. The parent lives on the `reader_answer_submitted` payload (as on the branch).
- **Inquiry status:** `question_events.resolution` and commitments, unchanged.
- **Quick-check scopes (one migration, RW1a):** `reader_authored_questions` gains
  - `scope_kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'section' CHECK (scope_kind IN ('section', 'inquiry', 'selection'))`;
  - `question_style TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'recall' CHECK (question_style IN ('recall', 'next_step', 'why_step', 'assumptions', 'reconstruct'))` (RW1b writes the last four; RW1a's migration adds the column with every value so there is one migration and no later rebuild);
  - nullable `anchor_span_ids_json` and `inquiry_root_event_id`;
  - nullable `self_check TEXT CHECK (self_check IS NULL OR self_check IN ('matches', 'partly', 'no'))` (§3.4 item 16; section quick checks leave it null, so they stay byte-identical).

  `section_id` stays `NOT NULL`: a passage-scoped row carries the anchor's section. **The section lookup (`latest_reader_authored_question`) filters `scope_kind = 'section'`,** or a reconnect row would shadow the section's quick check. Reuse keys: section rows as today; inquiry rows per (`inquiry_root_event_id`, prompt version); selection rows per (extraction, anchor spans, style, prompt version). A `dismissed` row still suppresses re-authoring for its own key.
- **Events:** no new kinds (§3.4 item 4). Routing, corrections, reconnect answers and the funnel use existing kinds with a payload `event` field and `authority_class = 'salience_only'`:
  - `reader_action_invoked` (`event`: `intent_routed`, `intent_corrected`, `reconnect_answered`, `next_step_requested`, `detour_opened`, `detour_returned`, …);
  - `reader_job_queued` and `reader_job_completed` for every model-backed action (status, failure reason, latency, model, prompt version, `speculative` for intent routings, and the learner's activity during the wait for the waiting-behaviour measure);
  - `reader_source_restored` for "back to where I was".

  The salience-firewall test gains a case per new `event` value.

### 4.4 Routes, spend, privacy

- **Routes.** `reader_intent` (decider; about $0.0004 a call at the listed price; `reader_intent.max_calls_per_day` 200, at most $0.08 a day with speculation). The reconnect and active-reading questions use the quick-check producer's existing route and its caps. "Another way" uses T6's routes (and the sources spec's, once merged); `reader_formative` texts use learner-text gaps' routes and caps.
- **Privacy.** The selection and the learner's text go to OpenRouter's Decider provider (router D6 covers the disclosure). With speculative routing, text the learner has typed but not yet submitted is sent after a pause; the disclosure says so, and `reader_intent.speculative_enabled = false` stops it. Nothing else is new.

### 4.5 Successor

None: content and display. Kill switches:
- `reader_inquiry.enabled` (off: the flat rail, exactly as today);
- `reader_intent.enabled` (off: the palette opens on selection and the rail flips to Notes, as today); `reader_intent.speculative_enabled` (off: route on Enter only);
- `reader_active_reading.enabled` (off: no next-step, why-step, assumption, another-way or reconstruct actions);
- `reader_formative.enabled` (off: no `reader_formative` learner texts are queued; answers stay in the report).

### 4.6 RW1c: a tutor that looks things up (restored)

The owner's round-3 ask: "serve the learner explanations directly from the canonical source based on natural language queries from the tutor". Today the tutor grounds on T6 spans in the practice, feedback and library contexts (`TutorSpanProvider`), but in the Reader context it sees only the selection and its neighbours (§3.5).

**Presentation:** UX §4.2 (three answer modes, *another way*, *that didn't help*, offered follow-ons), §4.0 (pending as *looking in your books…* then *writing…*); wireframes W3, W14.

**What the learner sees.** Ask in the Reader answers from the learner's books first, then explains:
- **Context it starts from:** the selection, the inquiry's ancestors and, when personalized, the latest session narrative for this source (labelled model-written; round 2's "the narrative summary helps the tutor and learner recover the intellectual context").
- **Three modes** (round 3 §4), chosen per answer by the tutor and labelled:
  1. **"From your books"**: a short cited excerpt (an equation, a definition, a worked step) when a passage answers the question directly. Source-authored, quoted, no paraphrase, and quote-verified against its block in code: an excerpt whose text is not in the passage is dropped, and the answer falls back to mode 2.
  2. **A grounded explanation** with links back to the passages (today's mode, now with retrieved passages).
  3. **A bridge to your work**: a short explanation that ties a passage to the learner's own recent work or open gap on the same LOs ("Your approach matches §3.4; the difference is the boundary condition — compare its limits with your step 4"), quoting the learner's words in the learner's voice. It is offered only when the learner has such work or an open gap, and only when the "personalized" toggle (RW4a) is on. It reuses the pinned-span provider the sources spec's "Explain this with my work" uses (its §10.3), built once.
- **When the books don't cover it** (round 1 §7: "a retrieval system should be able to say that it lacks adequate evidence instead of inventing an apparently authoritative derivation"; round 3 §4: retrieval grounding alone does not establish correctness). When verification keeps no passage, the answer opens with "Your books don't cover this directly — this is LearnLoop's model's explanation", and nothing in it is labelled as from the books. The report counts these abstentions and the dropped excerpts (§15).
- **"Explain it another way"** under every answer retrieves with `find_sources(intent="alternative")` (sources spec), with `exclude_span_keys` set to every passage already shown in this inquiry, so the second explanation comes from another source or another role.
- **"That didn't help"** opens "What's still unclear?" (W5). The text starts a branch (a reader question, so learner-text gaps reads it), and the next answer retrieves with `find_sources(intent="remediate")` with the gap or misconception it names as `target_hypotheses`. The sources spec's `remediate` intent reads the misconception's `correction_source_span_ids`.
- **Offers, not actions.** Two kinds of buttons sit under an answer. Nothing reaches the learner until they press one: LearnLoop shows, serves, schedules, imports and opens nothing on its own, and the tutor never does.
  - **The sources spec's requests**, rendered here (its SC11): the deterministic practice request every answer carries (its S0, §6.2: "Practice this", the same `find_practice` call as RW4a's "Practice this question" — router §15.3's suggested first use) and, once its S4 merges, the tutor's own `tool_requests` (practice, a passage for an intent, a comparison through `compare_sources`), each with the tutor's one-line reason labelled as LearnLoop's.
  - **When each one runs** (D44; the sentence here used to say "practice and passages run when shown", against the rule above and the UX document's buttons). Fetching and showing are separate. The two lookups, `find_practice` behind "Practice this" and `find_sources` behind a passage request, start in the background when the answer lands, as that spec's D5 runs them (eager, cheap, time-boxed), so a press shows the result without a wait; pressed before the lookup lands, the button shows *looking in your books…* in place. What a lookup fetched stays out of sight until the press, and the impression (`record_proposal_impressions`, surface `reader`, that spec's §6.5) and any span exposure are recorded only then: a lookup nobody opened leaves its proposal or `source_tool_calls` run row and nothing else. A comparison is one heavier model call and runs only when pressed (D5's lazy case). Pressing "Practice this" shows proposals; only "Practise" on one serves it (that spec's §6.3). A lookup that found nothing turns its button into a plain line, *nothing in your practice or books fits this yet*; one that failed or timed out keeps its button and runs again on the press.
  - **Two Reader buttons** decided in the Reader, not requested by the tutor: "Make this explorable" when a cited block is an `equation` or `figure` that RW5b's feasibility judgment marked `yes`, and "Try the next step first" when a cited block is a derivation or worked example (RW1b's next-step question at that line).

**How it works.**
- **The tutor forms its own query.** In the Reader context, `ask_question` gains a source-span provider, as practice, feedback and library already have: T6's rewrite step writes the query from the learner's question, the inquiry's ancestors and, when personalized, the open gaps on the passage's LOs; `find_sources(policy="reading", verify=True)` runs it. The selection and its neighbours stay first in the citation allowlist; retrieved spans are added after them.
- **Policies hold, derived from the caller.** The policy is never an argument: the sources spec derives it on the server from the call's context (its §5.2, SC2). The Reader context gets `reading`; inside a workbench session it gets `assisted_practice` for the session's item (a row this spec asks that table to carry, §18); during a cold attempt nothing is retrieved and every tool is refused (W7).
- **Receipts.** The `find_sources` manifest is stored with the exchange, as T6's tutor manifest is; the tutor's tool requests leave the sources spec's `source_tool_calls` rows. Exposure is recorded where text is shown.
- **Before the sources spec merges:** "another way" and "that didn't help" use the base `find_sources` (built once by RW4a or the sources spec's S3, whichever lands first, to that spec's §9.1 signature) with `policy="reading"` and the shown passages excluded; "Practice this" calls `find_practice` with the learner's question directly (the same RPC S0 later fills); the tutor writes no tool requests. The modes and the two Reader buttons work unchanged.

**Data.** No table: exchanges are `question_events` with the manifest; buttons pressed are `reader_action_invoked` events (`event`: `tutor_offer_pressed`, with the request's `source_tool_calls` id when it came from the tutor).

**Routes, spend, privacy.** The tutor's existing route and caps; T6's routes for retrieval; the sources spec's routes and caps for its intents and tools. The bridge adds the open-gap statements and recent workbench steps on the same LOs to the tutor's context, the same class of data the hint writer already sends (§5.8).

**Successor.** None: retrieval is context (T6's ruling) and offers are learner picks. Kill switches `reader_tutor.retrieval_enabled` (off: today's selection-and-neighbours tutor), `reader_tutor.offers_enabled` (off: no buttons under answers in the Reader). The sources spec's `source_service.tutor_tools` gates the tutor's requests themselves.

---

## 5. RW2 — The exercise workbench

### 5.1 RW2a: the resumable scratchpad

**Presentation:** UX §5.1–5.3 (opening, the step editor, saving and leaving), §5.9 (submit), §5.10 (I'm stuck), §5.12 (the practice screen); wireframes W5, W8, W15.

**Where it opens** (each entry is recorded on the session as `entry`):
- **In the Reader:** "work through this" on an imported exercise, on a quick check (escalated to a practice item first, through the existing path), or on a selected problem; "resume" (the `✎` margin mark, the recap, the rail, the practice card); "check me" on a cue and "practice this question" (§6.1, §7.3); "one exercise worth trying" in the Guide (RW7a); "a short problem instead" from a discriminating question (§5.2), which opens a second session with a crumb back to the first, which stays open (D32).
- **On the practice screen:** a "Work in steps" button on any item (remembered once chosen). It is **not** the default answer surface in v1 (D9): the item stem on top, the step editor full width, no source pane.
- **From the daily plan:** the same as the practice screen.

**The split** (UX S1). In the Reader the source stays in the left pane and the workbench takes **half the window**, resizable and remembered per device (at least 480 px), not the rail's 360 px: multi-line derivations with live KaTeX, a verdict column and anchored hints don't fit a rail. The rail's tabs fold into the workbench header. Below the 1,040 px breakpoint the app already uses (`app.css:533`) the sides stack: the workbench takes the screen, the stem stays pinned at its top, and "show in the book" opens the source in a sheet.

**Opening at once** (UX S2). On a selected problem that is not yet an item, the workbench opens immediately with the verbatim selection as the stem, and the learner can write step 1 while `import_exercises` (a paid call that can take tens of seconds) runs in the background (*setting up checks and hints for this exercise…*). The steps belong to the session from the first keystroke; the session binds to the item when the import completes (`practice_item_id` is null until then, §5.7). Check my work, Help and Submit say "available once setup finishes" until the item is bound. If the import fails, the steps stay and the header offers "try again"; nothing is lost and no attempt can be written without an item.

**Whether the book starts open** follows the entry's purpose (UX S7; D9): "work through this", "resume" of a session that had it open, and the Guide's exercise open **with** the book; "check me", "practice this question", "a short problem instead" and plan items open **without** it, because they exist to test. One line above "Your work" states the choice and its consequence before the first step — *● with the book open (practice beside the book — counts as one hint) ○ without it (your own attempt; you can open it any time)* — and typing the first step accepts it. `Ctrl+Shift+B` flips it; with the book hidden, the source pane says why and how to bring it back.

**The layout:**
- **Header:** *WORKING · Exercise 5.14 · from the book, verbatim · original page ↗* (hidden while the book is hidden) and, from RW2b, the help style.
- **Problem:** the verbatim stem, with "original page" when it came from a source.
- **Your work:** an ordered list of steps. Each step is edited with the existing `LiveMarkdownEditor` (markdown with live KaTeX). Steps can be added, edited (a new version supersedes the old; the old text is kept and shown dimmed on request) or deleted (a tombstone, with *deleted · undo* in place until the learner leaves the problem).
- **Actions (RW2a):** **I'm stuck · Submit · Leave for now** on the main row; **Abandon** in the `⋯` menu, so it is never one stray key away. RW2b adds **Check my work · Help**. The assistance so far shows in the footer as it accrues (*book open = 1 hint · help used: none*).

**Saving and resuming** (the main RW2a benefit):
- Every step change is saved as it is typed, through a write queue modelled on the practice screen's draft checkpoints (`PracticeScreen.tsx:306`). A crash loses at most the keystrokes since the last flush. The header says *saved · just now*; a failing write says *not saved yet — retrying · your text is safe in this window*, and a missing sidecar *keeping your steps here; they'll save when it's back*, never "error".
- **"Leave for now"** (`Esc` twice, navigating away, closing the app or a restart) keeps the session `open`. **"Abandon"** closes it at once, with no confirmation, and says *abandoned · your steps are kept · reopen*: **reopen** sets the session back to `open` (UX S13). Abandoned sessions never wrote an attempt, so reopening is always possible, from the workbench, the recap or the practice card. On Leave, Submit or Abandon the workbench collapses back into ordinary reading at the same passage (the dialogue's "the workspace can collapse back into normal reading").
- **"Resume this problem"** appears on the source (the `✎` mark beside the problem's passage), in the rail, in RW6a's recap and on the item's practice card. It reopens the same steps, the same source anchor, the same book visibility and, after RW2b, the same assistance history, scrolled to the last edited step.
- An open session older than `workbench.open_session_days` (30) is shown as "unfinished" in the recap, without alarm, and never auto-closed.

**I'm stuck** (`Ctrl+D`) opens a text box, "Write what you can, or what's confusing you. Being stuck here is useful information." The text becomes a step of kind `stuck`, dimmed in the learner's voice and never deleted on its own. In RW2a it is kept as learner work; RW2b sends it to the hint writer and to learner-text gaps. Submitting with no steps and an empty box is the existing empty stuck answer (`declared_stuck = true`, scored 0 with no model call), and the submit line says so first ("Submitting now records 'stuck' — that's fine").

**Submit** (`Ctrl+Enter`) composes the answer (the steps in order, stuck steps and tombstones excluded) and sends it through `submit_attempt` (§5.4). Above the button, one line states what the attempt records before it is sent: *book open · 3 hints (a check, a level-1 question, a level-3 hint) → practice with help ◌*, or *your own attempt*. Every assist §5.4 counts appears in it and in the footer, a check included (G62). It never promises a later check, which only RW6b's valuation can decide (W2).

**Back from feedback returns to the problem** (UX S6). Today feedback's back (`Esc` or `b`) returns to the queue or the plan (`FeedbackScreen.tsx:1975`, `:1982`). For an attempt with `entry_surface = 'desktop_reader_workbench'`, back opens the Reader at that problem, the submitted session shown read-only with its steps and assistance, and a second `Esc` returns to reading at its passage. The lookup is one read RPC, `workbench.session_for_attempt`. Attempts from anywhere else go back as today. During a plan reading block, DL1's "Back to today's plan" is unchanged.

### 5.2 RW2b: checks and the help ladder

**Presentation:** UX §5.4 (check my work), §5.5 (the ladder), §5.6 (anchored hints), §5.7 ("this doesn't connect"), §5.8 (a question before a hint), §5.11 (help style and tone); wireframes W5, W6, W7.

**Check my work** (`Ctrl+K`) reads the steps and returns, for each step, `valid`, `questionable` or `unchecked`, and names the **first questionable step** with a reason that quotes it. It never gives the correction, never a score, and never colours a step red. Verdicts sit in a narrow column beside the steps; editing a checked step dims its marker (*edited since check*), and a stale result says which steps changed since. Each verdict is labelled by method:
- **"checked"** (`✓`, or `≠` when a non-LLM check found the step doesn't follow) only when a symbolic check ran and passed or failed (§5.3);
- **"model's reading, not checked"** (`≈` or `?`) otherwise (W2).

**Why a check counts as help.** The dialogue's level 0 allowed "an optional self-check when requested" as independent work. A check that names the first questionable step gives exactly level 2's information (localization), so it is recorded as one hint-equivalent (§5.4), and the button says so ("check my work · counts as a hint"). The dialogue's level-0 self-check is therefore narrowed, with this reason, to the learner's own review: any check by LearnLoop tells the learner something about their work, so none is free of assistance.

*Restored (rows 18 and 60 of the coverage audit):* the check also returns
- **a partial solution graph:** per step, `depends_on` (the earlier step ids it uses) and any `assumption` it introduces. A questionable step then invalidates only the steps that depend on it, so "what stands" (contract rule 3) can keep later independent work, and `continue_from` can be any valid step the questionable one does not feed;
- **an error-kind guess** for the first questionable step: `transcription` | `missing_prerequisite` | `invalid_algebra` | `wrong_method` | `conceptual` | `unclear`, with its quoted reason. It is shown as "the model's guess" (W2), goes to the diagnoser's trace as context (§5.4), and is never written as a diagnosis or an error event. The diagnoser at Submit remains the only classifier with authority.

*Adaptive hints (§20.11):* **the check reads the approach map.** When the item has one (the adaptive-hints spec's §4.3: a small graph of subgoal nodes and the distinct routes through the problem, built in the background when the workbench binds the item), the check also names, for each step it marks `valid`, the map node and route the step reaches (`map_node_id`, `route_id` in the verdicts), using the nodes' recognizers. That is the hint's placement for free: the node the `continue_from` step reaches is where a hint starts (rule 7), so the writer does not have to guess where the learner is. A valid step on a route the map lacks is placed as `new_route` (adaptive-hints §4.4). Nothing new is shown: the map contains the solution, so it reaches the learner only through hints and never reaches the grader (W8).

**The help ladder.** Help always starts from the learner's **last valid checkpoint**. **The level is the amount of the solution the help does, and it alone decides what the attempt records (§5.4). The kind is the form the help takes at that level, and, for a waypoint, the horizon is how many steps ahead it points** (adaptive-hints §4.2, whose contract column each kind obeys; rule 8). A horizon of one is a next step, so `next_step` and `waypoint` are the two ends of one scale.

| Level | What it is | Kinds at this level (adaptive-hints §4.2) | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | No help | — | — |
