| 1 | A question: about the approach, an assumption, the goal or the first move | `approach_question` (with no work yet, often "what would your first move be?", which also places the learner for the next hint); `discriminating`, the form rung 1 takes when two or more rival explanations are open (below) | "What does F(x) have to equal at the left end of the support?" |
| 2 | Localize: the first questionable step, no correction | `localize` | "Step 4 is where your expression stops being a CDF." |
| 3 | A targeted hint tied to the learner's step: one of the level-3 kinds | `waypoint` (an intermediate goal state on the learner's route, `horizon` ≥ 2 steps ahead: what will be true, not how to get there); `subgoal_plan` (the rest of the route as a few labelled subgoals, the current one marked, no step content; labels concrete for a weaker background, abstract when the background is there); `next_step` (the single next action from `continue_from`, horizon 1); `error_specific` (what is off in a quoted step and the principle it breaks, without the corrected result); `discriminating` (a check whose result differs between the rivals); `worked_parallel` (the same move on a different instance, preferably a worked example from the learner's own source); `authored` (one of the item's own hints, verbatim) | `error_specific`: "Your derivative in step 3 matches the PDF; check the constant of integration in step 4 against F(0) = 0." `waypoint`: "Aim for a CDF that is 0 at the left end of the support and 1 at the right; you are about two steps from that." |
| 4 | Partial worked help, starting from the learner's valid steps | `continuation` (the next portion forward, in the learner's notation); `worked_chunk` (a chunk worked **backward from the goal**, each piece saying what it establishes, how and why the next needs it, leaving the bridge from `continue_from` to it for the learner) | Writes step 5 in the learner's notation |
| 5 | Full walkthrough, keeping the learner's route where it is valid | `walkthrough` | Completes the solution from step 4 |

**Waypoints, with care** (adaptive-hints §1.2). The research the owner read argues for goals a few steps ahead instead of next steps, but its direct evidence is mixed: in Cody et al. (2022), waypoints given unsolicited at random intervals cost low-prior-knowledge students time and steps and did not significantly help high-prior ones, and learners who could not bridge to a waypoint tried, failed and gave up; given only when a learner needed help, they helped the weakest learners and cut help avoidance (Alam et al. 2023). So in the workbench a waypoint is one kind among several, chosen from the learner's state (§5.5), **never shown unasked** (help appears only when the learner opens it, U10), and always with a way down: **after a waypoint, "a smaller step toward this goal" serves the next step toward the same target node at the same level** (a learner pick, UX §5.6), and Help reopened on that step keeps the target: every option at level 3 or above aims at it, and `next_step` toward it is always among them (§5.5).

**The service.** Every hint, whatever its kind, is written by the adaptive-hints spec's hint service (`request_hint`, its §4.1; route `hint_writer`), which checks its shared contract rules and leak control before anything is shown (its §4.6). The workbench passes its steps with ids, the check's verdicts and step graph, the about-step and `continue_from`, the rivals and the learner's feedback as the request's current work, and checks the rules below that only a step list makes exact. The Reader specifies no writer, chooser or map builder of its own (W13, G64).

- **Help** (`Ctrl+H`) opens the ladder inline below the focused step, never modal and never over the learner's work, with one level **offered first** (§5.5) and every other level visible. The offered rung names its kind in plain words and, for a waypoint, how far ahead it points (UX §5.5–5.6). The learner can take any level at any time (W11); no rung is ever greyed out for being "too early". **Choosing and taking are separate keys** (digits or arrows choose, Enter takes), so a stray key can't reveal a worked step, and there is no "are you sure?". Each rung states its consequence (*hint*, or *worked help ◌* for levels 4–5).
- **Authored hints are seeds and the fallback** (adaptive-hints §4.7). The item's `hints` stay on the item and nothing writes to it. The hint writer receives them, and when one fits the learner's checkpoint it may serve it verbatim as a level-3 hint of kind `authored`, labelled "from the item"; the map builder reads them as a coarse subgoal list. Under the chooser's fallback, rung 3 serves them in order while they last (§5.5). A capped or unavailable hint service keeps them and says so ("Tailored hints are unavailable right now (reason). The item's own hints are still here"). The behaviour is the first draft's "authored hints first"; the role is now stated.
- **"This doesn't connect to my work"** sits under every hint: an optional text box with four shortcuts and a pick of the step it refers to, defaulting to the hint's step. The next hint reads it with the step snapshot, at once; there is no waiting for two failed hints. **The next hint stays at the level of the hint it answers**, so a reply never raises the help without the learner's take step; for more, the learner takes a higher rung. Each shortcut has a defined effect on the next request (adaptive-hints §4.2's shortcut effects, with this spec's meanings; §20.11 C2):
  - **"That's not where I'm stuck"** (UX S14: the owner's pain is a hint aimed at the wrong place) makes the picked step the next hint's `about_step`: the hint must cite it and is shown under it (rules 1 and 3), its checkpoint follows rule 2 relative to it (the picked step itself when it stands, otherwise the last standing step before it), its placement is recomputed from there, and it must not repeat the earlier localization.
  - **"Too big a jump"** asks for a smaller step: after a waypoint, a shorter horizon toward the same target (down to `next_step`); otherwise a finer kind at the same level (a plan becomes the next step; a worked parallel becomes a step in the learner's own problem).
  - **"I'm using a different approach"** keeps the learner's route (rule 4): the next hint is placed on the learner's route — an existing route of the map, or `new_route` when the map lacks it — and names the learner's step it builds on. It never switches to the reference route; only "Show me the book's way" does that.
  - **"The notation"** passes the learner's symbols and text to the writer as a notation note: the next hint keeps its idea and restates it in the learner's notation, quoting at least one of their symbols and defining any it introduces. It is the one shortcut exempt from rule 5's change of strategy.
  - Every reply appends a `learner_card` verdict (task `hint_fit`, the hint service's, verdict `different`) so the report can count unusable hints by shortcut and kind.

**The reconciliation contract** answers the owner's pain point. It is checked in code before a hint is shown: the hint service checks its shared rules on every surface (adaptive-hints §4.6), and the workbench's step ids, verdicts and step graph make rules 1–3 and 7 exact here:
1. **It cites the learner.** Every hint at level 2 or higher names at least one of the learner's step ids that exists, and quotes it.
2. **It continues from a real checkpoint.** `continue_from` must be a step marked `valid` or `unchecked` that does not depend, directly or through other steps, on the first questionable step (the step graph above); where the check returned no graph, a step before the first questionable step. The questionable step is never the checkpoint: a hint may be about it and quote it (rules 1 and 3), but it builds on work that stands.
3. **It says what stands, first, where the learner is looking.** The hint opens by stating which of the learner's steps stand ("Your steps 1–3 stand"). It is shown under its `about_step`, the step it addresses (usually the first questionable step, or the step "that's not where I'm stuck" picked), which must exist and be quoted (rule 1); its label names both (*on your step 3 · continues from step 2*). The first draft showed a hint under its checkpoint while the UX document showed it under the questionable step and called that step its checkpoint; both documents now keep the two apart (D45; UX §0 move 5, §5.6).
4. **It keeps the learner's route.** It never switches to the reference's route unless the learner's route is invalid or the learner pressed "Show me the book's way". "I'm using a different approach" means the opposite: the next hint must build on the learner's route and name the learner's step it builds on (UX §5.7; the first draft's "Different approach" shortcut read both ways, §20.9). A valid but different route is said to be valid ("your route works — the book goes another way"); PF-c's valid-alternative rule applies to checks as it does to grading.
5. **It changes strategy when it isn't working.** After a "doesn't connect" reply (except "the notation"), or two hints on the same step, a new hint must differ from the earlier ones on that step in its **kind or** its declared `strategy` slug (for example `boundary_check`, `worked_parallel`, `representation_switch`, `counterexample`).
6. **It asks before it repairs** (restored; the dialogue's central scenario, round 1 §8: "two plausible explanations… A discriminating question can distinguish those possibilities before selecting a repair"). See below.
7. **It is placed** (adaptive-hints §4.6 rule 6). When an approach map exists, the hint names its route and its target node. It starts from the node the `continue_from` step reaches (from the check's verdicts, or the writer's quoted placement when no check has run), on the learner's route (rule 4), and the target's distance along that route matches the chosen horizon, or is the last node short of the goal when the route is shorter. Work that matches no route and is not clearly valid gets an `approach_question` about what the learner is trying, never a confident hint toward a route they are not on (adaptive-hints §4.4). Without a map the hint is placed relative to the reference solution, and waypoints and plans are not offered.
8. **It obeys its kind's contract** (adaptive-hints §4.2, checked by the service): a waypoint names a target state and no transformation toward it and never a node marked `leak`; a plan's labels each map to a node and carry no expression from a later node; a discriminating question names two or more rivals whose predicted answers differ; a worked chunk leaves at least one gap for the learner and gives each piece its what, how and why; and so on for every kind in §5.2's table.

A hint that fails 1, 2, 5, 7 or 8 is re-asked once with the failure named, then shown as a level-3 `next_step` from `continue_from` without its unverified claims.

**A question that tells explanations apart** (restored, RS2). When a check leaves a questionable step and two or more rival explanations of it are open — the check's own error-kind guess and its alternatives, the open hypotheses and durable misconceptions on the item's facets (the records the hint writer already receives, §5.8), the rival causes of a diagnosed earlier attempt on the item (`unresolved_cause_factors.candidate_causes_json`), and gaps named from this session's text — **rung 1 is a level-1 question of kind `discriminating`** (`strategy = discriminate`), and the chooser weighs offering it first (§5.5). These rivals are the hypothesis records the service's `discriminating` precondition and contract read (adaptive-hints §4.2, §4.5).
- **What it is.** A short question whose answer differs under each rival: "Before we fix step 4: what should F(0) be for this distribution?" separates a misunderstood support from an integration slip. It never states either rival and never contains the correction (leak control applies).
- **How it is chosen.** When the kind is `discriminating` (the rung 1 the learner takes, or a level-3 check the chooser drew), the hint writer (`hint_writer`) proposes a few candidate questions, each with the answer it predicts under each rival. Each candidate is compiled as an instrument with one conditional row per rival and ranked with the existing `instrument_expected_information_gain` (`probe_families.py:467`) under the rivals' current weights: the open episode's posterior or the facet's `hypothesis_marginal` where they exist, uniform otherwise (labelled). A candidate whose rivals all predict the same answer has zero EIG and is dropped by construction. The best one is offered; nothing is hand-weighted (W4). If no candidate has positive EIG, no discriminating question is shown: rung 1 serves an `approach_question` and says why ("no question separates these two readings; here is a question about your approach"). This EIG ranking is the Reader's addition to the kind, and §18 asks the adaptive-hints spec to use it on every surface.
- **What its answer does** (tier 2, W1). It goes into the step trace, so the diagnoser sees it at Submit, and into the next hint's context, which now repairs the explanation the answer pointed to. As `workbench_text` it reaches learner-text gaps. It moves no posterior and writes no probe observation.
- **A short problem instead.** When the sources spec's discriminating search is merged, Help also offers "a short problem from your books that tells these apart · about N min": `find_practice(intent="discriminate", target_hypotheses=…)` over the same rivals, valued by that spec's `value_candidates`. The learner may take it now — it opens as a second workbench session with the book hidden and a crumb back to the original problem, which stays open (D32); an ordinary attempt — or leave it to RW6b, which values it with the other follow-ups.
- **Its label says what it is before it is answered:** *a question that tells apart two explanations of step 3 · not graded · counts as a hint*. After the answer, one line says what it settled ("That settles it: your support is right; the gap is the constant") or that both readings still stand, then the hint follows at the offered level.
- **One at a time.** A second discriminating question on the same step, at either level, appears only when the learner asks for it (by taking rung 1 again), and it must clear the same EIG ranking (a zero-EIG question is never offered). After one has been answered, `discriminating` leaves the chooser's options on that step.
- **When it is not offered first.** With a single open explanation rung 1 is an ordinary question. With two or more, whether rung 1 is the *suggested* rung is the chooser's sampled judgment (§5.5, §20.11 C1): its instructions say that asking first both helps and tells the explanations apart, help style "guide me" leans away from it, and the learner choosing a higher rung skips it. Help always helps (W11): the question stays on rung 1 for a learner who wants it, and every other level stays visible.

**Leak control.** A hint at level 3 or lower must not contain the final answer: the service runs `answer_leaks_expected` (`tutor_qa.py:816`) on every hint, and no waypoint or subgoal label ever carries the state of a map node marked `leak` (adaptive-hints §4.6). A leaking hint is re-asked once, then withheld with "this hint would give the answer away; take level 4?".

**Bridging to the book.** After a hint is shown, a background request may add "Compare with the book": a verified passage the hint relies on, side by side ("compare its integration limits with your step 4"). It uses `TutorSpanProvider` in the `practice` context unchanged (T6's item-bound scope: no exercise or solution blocks, nothing linked to the item, answer-overlap spans withheld, listwise verification); once the sources spec's S3 merges, that provider chooses `remediate` when the item's LO has an active misconception (its §9.5), and the bridge gains it with no change here. It never delays the hint (§3.4 item 12); while it runs, the hint shows *finding the matching step in the book…*. A `worked_parallel` hint that already quotes a passage from the learner's source (T6-verified, adaptive-hints §4.2) gets no second bridge to the same passage. RW2c adds a worked example that takes the learner's route and a logged choice between equally good passages (§5.10).

**In-session updates** (restored, row 74: "don't wait for the session to end"). Every `workbench_text` (a stuck step, a "doesn't connect" reply, a discriminating answer) is queued to learner-text gaps when it is written, not at the end of the session. A gap named while the learner is still working joins the open explanations the next hint, the hint chooser and the discriminating question read, and RW3b recomputes the cue links of a new hypothesis in the background. That is the dialogue's provisional, in-session state, kept to hypotheses and routing (tier 2); RW6a's summary consolidates at the end.

### 5.3 Symbolic checks: explicit, declared kinds

The check writer declares, per step it can classify, one transformation kind with stated inputs. *Restored (row 33): the list is widened from three to every kind sympy can check with a negative control:*
- **equivalence** of two consecutive expressions or of both sides of an equation step (including expansion, factoring and simplification);
- **differentiation or integration** of a stated expression with respect to a stated variable, including definite integrals with stated bounds;
- **substitution** of a stated value or expression;
- **solving** a stated equation for a stated variable;
- **limits** and **sums or series** with stated bounds;
- **inequality steps**, checked symbolically where sympy can and numerically on sampled points otherwise (labelled "checked numerically");
- **boundary and normalization conditions** (F(0) = 0, a density integrating to one) on a stated expression.

**Hints are verified separately from how they are written** (round 2 §4: "treat hint generation and hint verification separately"; restored). When a hint at level 2 or higher asserts something checkable about the learner's work ("your derivative in step 3 matches the PDF"), the same check path verifies that claim before the hint is shown; a failed claim is removed and the hint re-asked once, and an uncheckable claim is labelled the model's reading.

How: the check writer (route `workbench_check`) proposes the transformation and a sympy check; PF's sandbox runs it with a negative control, as PF's equivalence checks do (§3.4 item 6). Without bubblewrap the sandbox fails closed and the verdict reads "model's reading". Steps of other kinds — prose reasoning, proof moves, a choice of method — stay `unchecked` or model-read, and the report counts them by kind (§15). Formal verification of proof steps is deferred (§17).

### 5.4 Source visibility and what an attempt records

**Source visibility.**
- **In the Reader the source is visible by default.** The submission goes through `submit_attempt` with `entry_surface = 'desktop_reader_workbench'` in a practice session the workbench opens (`start_session`). **The visible source counts as one hint-equivalent**, the same rule `submit_reading_question` already records (`hint_equivalent: true`). The attempt is therefore assisted: graded blind, diagnosed, FSRS-fed, and never certifying (W7). Exposures are recorded on the anchored spans as `OpenInSource` does today.
- **"Hide the source"** before the first step makes it an ordinary attempt. Opening the source afterwards records the exposure, and the hint-equivalent applies from then on.
- **On the practice screen and from the plan,** the source is hidden. "Original page" behaves like any other source opening during practice.

**No new evidence semantics.** Every assist maps onto fields that already have meaning:

| Assist | Recorded as | Existing meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Source visible in the Reader | `hints_used` += 1 | Assisted: schedules, certifies nothing (`causal_activity_policy.py:182`); the field's `+hints` arm reads it |
| Check my work, and each hint shown at level 1–3, whatever its kind (§5.2's table: a discriminating question, a waypoint, a plan and an item's own hint included) | `hints_used` += 1 for each one shown | The same |
| A hint at level 4 or 5 (`continuation`, `worked_chunk`, `walkthrough`) | `primed = true` (not added to `hints_used`; §20.11 C4) | `repair_activity`: no FSRS feed and no certification, like a guided redo |
| Hint text overlapping the key | A `reveal_events` row of the existing kind `tutor_answer`, `basis = 'workbench_hint:<kind>:L<n>'`, with its overlap amount | Production admissibility and the reveal-aware cold lane |
| Answer confidence (optional, before Submit) | `answer_confidence` (1–5) | Logged only; never grading, certification or scheduling |
| Stuck text, then Submit | The steps are an ordinary answer; `declared_stuck` stays false | — |
| "Compare methods" after a correct Submit (RW2c, §5.10) | Nothing on the submitted attempt; a `reveal_events` row of the existing kind `answer_display` (or `source_review` for a source solution passage) on the item | The reveal-aware cold lane and RW6b's family exclusion treat the family as revealed |

Note the existing cap: `hints_used` is clamped to the item's `max_useful_hints` when that is set (`handlers/practice.py:561`). Any value above zero is assisted, so the cap changes no classification; the assist rows keep the true count for reports.

**Counted on the server** (adaptive-hints §5, with this table's rule). Every check and hint is written to `help_assists` when it is shown (§5.7). At Submit the handler derives the workbench attempt's assistance from the session: one for a visible source, one per shown check and per shown hint at level 1–3, and `primed` when any shown hint was at level 4 or 5. The client sends no hint count of its own for a workbench attempt, so nothing is counted twice, and a hint cancelled or withheld before it showed counts nothing (adaptive-hints G-H3). The level alone decides the mapping; the kind and horizon change nothing here.

**On Submit:**
- the blind grader grades the composed answer with no knowledge of the steps' history or the hints (W8);
- the diagnoser's context gains, for this attempt only, the step trace with the assists in order (each hint with its kind and level) and the work before each assist, under a prompt-version suffix (`+workbench-v1`) used only when a trace exists. The trace includes the step graph, the check's error-kind guesses (labelled as guesses) and any discriminating question with the learner's answer, so the diagnoser can use the separation the question bought. Every other attempt's context hashes as before (the stuck-reading precedent);
- the feedback screen is today's, including guided redo.

**Abandoned sessions** write no attempt. Their steps and assists stay as learner work and appear in RW6a's recap.

The RW2a agent confirms this table against `resolve_attempt_activity_policy`. **If any mapping needs a new discount or eligibility rule, stop and make it a successor** (the next free `mvp-0.N` tag) rather than bending an existing field.

### 5.5 RW2b: the first offer, a judgment

**Presentation:** UX §5.5 (the suggested rung, its kind in plain words, *why?*), §5.11 (help style).

When the learner opens Help, the **hint chooser** picks what to offer first: a level, the kind of help at that level and, for a waypoint, a horizon. The chooser is the adaptive-hints spec's (its §4.5; route `hint_chooser`, the first draft's `workbench_level_chooser`). The workbench supplies its state and options and presents the result; it does not redesign the chooser.

- **How it is asked.** One Decider request (in `DECIDER_TASKS`; never a chat fallback) with two named questions over one typed state: `kind_level`, a choice over the (kind, level) options this step allows, and `horizon`, a score over *next step · two steps · the end of the current subgoal · the goal*, asked only when a waypoint is among the options. 3 s time box. **The workbench's options** are §5.2's kinds whose preconditions hold (adaptive-hints §4.5: no waypoint or plan without a map, no `discriminating` without two rivals, no `worked_parallel` without a usable example or instance), constrained by the step: with two or more rivals open on the about-step, level 1's option is the discriminating question rung 1 shows (§5.2), and `discriminating` leaves the options once one has been answered there; after a waypoint on this step, every option at level 3 or above aims at the same target and `next_step` toward it is among them; after a "doesn't connect" reply, the level stays and the shortcut's constraint applies (§5.2). These constrain what can be offered; they are not rules about what helps.
- **Sampled from the Decider's own probabilities** (the adaptive-hints spec's D4). The suggested rung, its kind and its horizon are drawn from the probabilities the Decider returned, and the probability of the draw is logged as the propensity, as the router's mixture does ("a propensity only has to be the distribution actually sampled from", router §0). **This replaces the first draft's "there is no randomization of help quality".** Sampling within the judge's own uncertainty is not a hint-quality experiment: every option passed its preconditions and faces the same contracts (§5.2), nothing is withheld, and every rung stays takeable (W11). When the Decider is confident it puts nearly all the probability on one option, which is then offered nearly always; when it spreads probability it is unsure which kind is best, and offering in proportion to that uncertainty is probability matching. It is also what lets the report compare kinds for this learner (§15; adaptive-hints §6.3). §17's row against deliberately worse help stands. With `hints.choice_policy = "argmax"` the top option is offered and logged with propensity 1, and no off-policy comparison is then possible.
- **Taking a different rung.** The learner can take any rung (W11). Taking the suggested one serves the drawn option. Taking another makes the level the learner's (`level_source = learner_pick`), and the kind at that level is drawn from the Decider's probabilities renormalized over that level's options, with that conditional probability logged (1 where the level has one option, as rung 5 does, and rung 1 when rivals are open). "Show me the book's way" (rule 4) and "a smaller step toward this goal" (§5.2) are the learner's picks of route and kind, logged as `learner_pick` with no propensity, as in the router.
- **Fallback.** On the time box, an unavailable or spent-out Decider, or `hints.chooser_enabled = false`: rung 1 is suggested, then, on each further Help press on the same step, one rung above the last level offered or taken there (up to 5). The offered rung's *why?* then says *no judgment available right now — the next rung up* (UX §5.5 says the same). A rung taken under fallback has a fixed kind: level 1 the approach question (the discriminating question when rivals are open), level 2 `localize`, level 3 the item's next authored hint while one remains and then `next_step`, level 4 `continuation`, level 5 `walkthrough`. Logged as `chooser = 'fallback'`, propensity 1. The adaptive-hints spec's own fallback ("authored hints, then the next step") is its practice screen's; the ladder keeps this progression (§20.11 C6).
- **The state it gets** (typed, short). Adaptive-hints §4.5's list, which already covers the first draft's: the item's LO status (untested / assumed / learning / demonstrated) and placement intent; demonstrated vs assisted-only evidence on the item's facets (capability ledger, facet timeline); the active predictor's P(success) and interval for the item; the open hypotheses and misconceptions on the item's facets, read from the diagnoser's overlay, and a closed probe episode's recommended tutor move; the help style. The workbench adds:
  - **the approach-map placement from the last check:** the route, the node the `continue_from` step reaches and the about-step (§5.2);
  - **the learner's route preference** across the item's family (adaptive-hints §4.3);
  - **history by kind on these facets:** kinds and levels taken, whether the learner continued unaided afterwards, "doesn't connect" replies by shortcut, and later independent outcomes — read from `help_outcomes` once RW2c lands (§5.10), from the session's own rows before then;
  - this session's failed checks, hints, rivals and named gaps;
  - **time since the last hint on this step, and whether the work changed since it.**
  - **The help style** (**let me struggle · balanced · guide me**, default balanced; global and remembered, with a per-session override in the workbench header, D31; one preference shared with the practice screen's hints, §20.11 C7). It is a preference the Decider weighs, never a block: "let me struggle" leans toward the least specific help that could work (questions and goals to aim for, in line with the finding that trying first helps novices), "guide me" toward more specific help sooner (next steps, then worked steps), and every rung stays available.
- **What it aims for.** The lightest help likely to let the learner continue on their own, not the help most likely to make the next step correct. The dialogue's warning (round 2 §7: "a full solution reveal may maximize immediate correctness while undermining independent problem-solving"; round 1 §2's study of AI assistance and later unassisted exams) is in the chooser's instructions. **In kind as well as level** (adaptive-hints §1.5): less specific forms (approach questions, waypoints, abstract subgoal labels) tend to suit higher prior knowledge and steady progress, more specific forms (next steps, concrete labels, error-specific hints, worked pieces) lower prior knowledge or a learner who is stuck; a waypoint is for a learner whose state says they can bridge to it. As the learner demonstrates a facet, the help offered on it falls: this is the adaptively faded help the dialogue cites (round 2 §4), and the report's help-drift line shows it (§15).
- **Why a judgment.** The expertise-reversal finding the dialogue cites: novices benefit from more guidance and experts from less, per learner × facet × task, with no cut line (adaptive-hints §1.5). The chooser applies it from evidence (W4). Learner history reaches the chooser, never the grader (W8).
- **What the learner sees.** The offered rung is marked *suggested (why?)* when the chooser returns, naming its kind in plain words and, for a waypoint, how far ahead it points (UX §5.5); the ladder works without a suggestion until then. "Why?" gives the chooser's inputs in plain words ("a goal to aim for: you've demonstrated conditioning on your own twice and your steps so far stand; help style: balanced · LearnLoop's judgment"), and says so when the judgment was split ("it leaned between a goal and the next step").
- **Logging.** On the `help_assists` row (§5.7), as adaptive-hints §6.2 lists: the options offered, the Decider's full probability vectors, the drawn option, its propensity and seed, the chooser (`decider` \| `fallback` \| `learner_pick`), the policy and question-set versions and the state hash; and the workbench's offered level, the level taken and `level_source`.

### 5.6 Reuse, learner text and signals

- **Grading and diagnosis:** the blind grader, the diagnoser, PF-c and guided redo, unchanged.
- **Retrieval:** `TutorSpanProvider` (practice context) for bridging; nothing new.
- **Learner text (RW2b):** a non-empty stuck step, a "this doesn't connect" reply with text, and an answer to a discriminating question are learner texts. Learner-text gaps gains one source kind, `workbench_text` (beside `tutor_question`, `diagnosis` and `stuck_answer`), whose adapter reads the text with its item, step, anchors and role (`stuck` \| `hint_feedback` \| `discriminate_answer`). It names gaps exactly as the pipeline does for other sources; nothing else in the pipeline changes. The kind needs the widening migration (§3.4 item 13). Until `learner/text-gaps` merges, the adapter is not built and the texts stay learner work.
- **Field signals: RW2d.** `stuck_confusion` and "help before work" are derived signals that Phase 6 serving reads (fast-track blocking), so a new source changes serving and needs a successor (§3.4; `field/signals.py:327`). A submitted workbench attempt already carries its assistance in `hints_used` and `primed`. Signals from mid-work stuck steps and help before the first step are restored as RW2d, an algorithm successor with a kill switch (§5.11).

### 5.7 Data

| Table | Phase | Role | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| `workbench_sessions` | RW2a | WORKFLOW | `id`, `practice_item_id` (null until the import binds, UX S2), `stem_md` (the verbatim stem shown before binding), `import_status` (`none` \| `pending` \| `bound` \| `failed`), `origin` (`reader` \| `practice` \| `plan`), `entry` (`work_through` \| `resume` \| `check_me` \| `practice_this` \| `path_exercise` \| `discriminating_problem` \| `practice_screen` \| `plan`; it sets the default book visibility, UX S7), `parent_session_id` (a "short problem instead" session's original problem), `extraction_id`, `span_ids_json`, `source_visible`, `source_hidden_at`, `practice_session_id`, `help_style`, `status` (`open` \| `submitted` \| `abandoned`; `abandoned` → `open` on reopen, UX S13), `attempt_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `closed_at`. Every CHECK'd column is created with all its values |
| `workbench_steps` | RW2a | RAW_LEDGER | `id`, `session_id`, `ordinal`, `kind` (`step` \| `stuck`), `body_md`, `supersedes_id`, `deleted` (tombstone), `client_idempotency_key`, `created_at` |
| `help_assists` (shared with the adaptive-hints spec; the first draft's `workbench_assists`) | RW2b, or that spec's H0, whichever lands first | RAW_LEDGER | **One table for every surface's assists** (adaptive-hints §9), with `surface` (`practice` \| `workbench` \| `guided_redo` \| `tutor`). Its columns are that spec's §9 row — the item and its hash, the practice session, `attempt_id` (filled at Submit), `hint_kind` (its `kind`, renamed so it cannot be confused with the assist kind, §18), `level`, `horizon`, `route_id`, `from_node_id`, `target_node_id`, `map_id`, `strategy`, `body_md`, `authored_index`, `work_hash`, `work_chars`, `placement_json`, `chooser` (`decider` \| `fallback` \| `learner_pick` \| `none`), `options_json`, `probabilities_json`, `propensity`, `seed`, `policy_version`, `request`, `feedback_text`, `feedback_shortcut`, `contract_failures_json`, `leak_flags_json`, `shown`, `counted_by`, `model`, `prompt_version`, `input_hash`, `latency_ms`, `requested_at`, `shown_at` — plus the workbench's columns, null on other surfaces: `workbench_session_id`; `assist_kind` (`hint` \| `check` \| `discriminate_answer` \| `bridge` \| `example` \| `compare_methods` \| `feedback`; every other surface writes `hint`); `offered_level`; `level_source` (`offered` \| `learner_pick`); `step_snapshot_json` (step ids and content hashes at request time); `step_verdicts_json` (verdict, method, `depends_on`, `assumption`, and the map node and route reached, per step); `first_questionable_step_id`; `error_kind_guess`; `about_step_id`; `continue_from_step_id`; `rivals_json` (the open explanations with their weights and source); `predicted_answers_json` and `eig_nats` (for a discriminating question); `feedback_step_id`; `cited_span_ids_json`; `retrieval_manifest_json`; `alternatives_json`. A discriminating question is a `hint` row with `hint_kind = 'discriminating'` (the first draft's separate `discriminate` kind is folded in). On a hint row `propensity` and `seed` are the chooser's draw (§5.5); on a bridge or example row they are RW2c's passage draw. `hint_kind` takes every kind in §5.2's table (the adaptive-hints spec's eleven plus `continuation`); `feedback_shortcut` takes `not_where_stuck` \| `too_big` \| `learner_route` \| `notation` (the first draft's `different_approach` is renamed `learner_route`, because the adaptive-hints spec uses that word for the opposite request, §20.11 C2). **Every CHECK'd column is created with every value either spec writes**, so neither spec's later phase rebuilds it |

No reveal-table rebuild (§3.4 item 10). Replay never reads the workbench tables or `help_assists`: the attempt row carries `hints_used` and `primed`, and the reveal ledger carries the reveals. The approach maps hints are placed on are the adaptive-hints spec's `approach_maps` (WORKFLOW, a paid output never rebuilt; its H1). The workbench reads them through that spec's service and writes none.

### 5.8 Routes, spend, privacy

- **Routes (RW2b).** The Reader's own: `workbench_check` (chat: step verdicts, the step graph, the map node each valid step reaches, the error-kind guess, localization and the sympy check text). **Hints use the adaptive-hints spec's shared routes** (its §7.2): `hint_writer` (chat, structured output: hints of every kind at levels 1–5, their placement, and the candidate discriminating questions with their predicted answers; the first draft's `workbench_hint`) and `hint_chooser` (decider; the first draft's `workbench_level_chooser`), with that spec's `approach_map` building and merging maps in the background. One writer, one chooser and one set of receipts serve every surface. Unset chat routes fall back to the active provider.
- **Latency.** Codex blind grading took 29–37 s on the stuck-prompt copy, too slow for "Help". The targets are check p50 ≤ 10 s and the hint service's own writer target (p50 ≤ 8 s, adaptive-hints §7.1), all **reported, not enforced**; the chooser's 3 s time box falls back rather than delaying a hint. The recommended route for both is a `chatgpt_plan` profile (the provider merged on `main` at `57d31c63`; the owner's sign-in is pending); until then a fast OpenRouter chat profile (D7; the adaptive-hints spec's D9 recommends the same for `hint_writer`). `learnloop ai latency` compares candidates before the switch. Ranking discriminating questions adds no model call: the EIG is computed.
- **Caps.** `workbench.max_calls_per_session` (24, a money cap counted in calls) and `workbench.max_daily_cost_usd` ($1.00 for metered routes) bound the workbench's own calls: checks and the compare line. **Hint calls are bounded by the hint service's money caps, shared with every surface, with no count of hints** (adaptive-hints §7.3: `hints.max_daily_cost_usd`, $1.00 a day for writer and chooser; `hints.maps.max_daily_cost_usd`, $0.50 for maps; §20.11 C8). A capped session says so and keeps the authored hints.
- **Context per call:** for checks, the item stem, reference solution and rubric, the session's steps, and the approach map when one exists; for hints, the hint service's context (adaptive-hints §4.6: the item, its authored hints, the map, earlier hints and feedback, the chosen kind, level and horizon, the route preference), with the workbench's steps, verdicts, step graph, about-step and `continue_from` as the current work, the rivals as typed records (statement and status, no prose; Slice 1 S3) and, after RW2c, the help-outcome lines. About 4–12K input tokens.
- **Privacy:** the learner's work and that slice of their state go to the configured provider, as grading and diagnosis already do, and the chooser's typed state to the Decider on OpenRouter, which router D6 already discloses. No new disclosure.

### 5.9 Successor

None (§5.4; the adaptive-hints spec's §10.1 rules the same for its service). Kill switches:
- `workbench.enabled` (off: no workbench entry anywhere; the practice screen is byte-identical);
- `workbench.help_enabled` (off: RW2a only);
- `workbench.symbolic_checks_enabled`;
- `workbench.discriminate_enabled` (off: `discriminating` is never among the workbench's options and rung 1 is an ordinary question, as before the restoration);
- `workbench.error_kind_enabled` (off: no error-kind guess is shown or traced);
- **the hint service's, shared with every surface** (adaptive-hints §9, §10): `hints.chooser_enabled` (off: the §5.5 fallback progression; it replaces the first draft's `workbench.level_chooser_enabled`, since there is one chooser), `hints.choice_policy` (`sample` \| `argmax`), and `hints.maps.enabled` (off: no map is built or read; the check names no nodes, and waypoints and plans are not offered).

### 5.10 RW2c: what helps this learner (restored)

**Presentation:** UX §5.6 (the route-matched example under its hint), §5.9 (compare methods after a correct answer); the help-outcome lines are invisible context, shown only through the chooser's "why?".

The dialogue's deepest aim (round 2 §9, round 3 §7): "a memory not only of what a learner knows, but of how that learner arrives at understanding, where their reasoning tends to diverge, and which forms of assistance help them recover." RW2c builds the part of it that n=1 data can support now.

**Help outcomes, a replayable read.** `help_outcomes(repository, facet_ids, as_of) -> HelpOutcomesV1` is a pure function over rows that already exist: the workbench's `help_assists` rows (kind, strategy, level, horizon, the passages and their roles from the retrieval manifests), RW1c's tutor exchanges and offers, `library_asks`, figure views, and the outcomes that followed. Once the sources spec's S5 merges, it reads that spec's `retrieval_intervention_outcomes` (its §11.2: `R_next`, `R_cold`, with censoring) for every retrieval-supported help instead of re-deriving them, and keeps its own derivation only for what S5 does not record. **For hints it reads the hint service's per-hint outcomes** (adaptive-hints §6.1: unaided next progress, more help needed, time to the next valid step, "doesn't connect", grade, later independent success with how many are pending) instead of deriving them again, and keeps its own derivation only for help that service does not own (figures, comparisons, passages outside S5). For each help kind (a hint kind and strategy, such as a waypoint, a plan or a `boundary_check` error-specific hint; a passage role such as worked example, figure or counterexample; a comparison; a figure) on each facet, it returns:
- **the immediate continuation:** whether the next check found a valid next step without further help;
- **the attempt's grade** when the session was submitted;
- **the later independent outcome** on the same facets (the next unassisted attempt, or a RW6b follow-up), and how many have **no later observation yet**.
- **Where the work tends to go off** (round 2 §9, "where their reasoning tends to diverge"): per facet, the kinds of the first questionable steps (the check's labelled guesses) and the diagnoser's causes at Submit, with counts and dates. The diagnoser's causes are the authority; the guesses are shown as guesses. Durable misconceptions and recurrence groups (T1) already carry the long-run pattern, and the hint writer already receives them (§5.8); this line adds the workbench's own recent record beside them.
- It is deterministic from the ledgers, computed twice in tests, and needs no table (so no replayer). Associations only: it is labelled "what preceded recovery for you", never "what works".

**Who reads it** (tier 2, W1):
- **the hint writer** gets the top few lines for the step's facets in the request ("on boundary conditions, a worked parallel preceded recovery 3 of 4 times; a goal two steps ahead 1 of 3"). This is the dialogue's data-driven next-step hints (row 61), seeded from the learner's own history, since at n=1 there is no cross-learner data (§17);
- **the hint chooser** gets the same lines (§5.5);
- **the report's "Interventions → outcomes" block** reads it instead of recomputing (§15).

**A logged choice where it costs nothing** (round 3 §7: "causal claims need randomized variation"). When bridging, or a worked-example search, finds two or more verified passages that both pass the judge, one is shown by a logged draw (propensity and seed on the assist row) and the others stay one click away ("another passage"). Every option passed the same judge, so no learner gets worse help on purpose: this is the cue holdout's pattern (D10), not a hint-quality experiment (§17 keeps those unplanned). The hint chooser's sampled kinds (§5.5) are the other comparison that withholds nothing. Over months the draws give the report an unconfounded comparison between, say, a figure and a worked example for this learner. The sources spec defers registered randomization designs *between interventions* (its §19) and reserves `design`, `seed` and `propensity` on its intervention records; this draw is a narrower thing — which of several verified passages a Reader surface shows first — and §18 asks that spec to copy the assist row's propensity and seed into those reserved fields for drawn passages. Switch: `workbench.passage_draw_enabled`.

**A worked example that takes your route** (round 3: "a worked example using CDF-first derivation… the learner should not have to restart"; rows 89, 97). The retrieval is the sources spec's: `find_sources(intent="worked_example", item=…, learner_work_md=<the learner's steps>)` with the caller context of a workbench session, so its policy is `assisted_practice` for the item (no exercise or solution block of the item, nothing linked to it, nothing reproducing its answer). Its verification keeps passages judged `follows_learner_route` first (its §9.2). RW2c adds only the Reader's part:
- the query, written by the hint writer from the learner's own route and the questionable step ("CDF-first derivation of a mixture; boundary condition at the edge of the support");
- when it runs: in the background after a level-3 or higher hint, or on "Show me a similar example";
- what the learner sees: beside the learner's step, "A worked example that takes your route: Book A §3.4 · compare its step 2 with your step 4". It is the third explanation mode (a personalized bridge) inside the workbench.
- Before the sources spec's S3 merges, the workbench shows RW2b's bridge only, and "Show me a similar example" is absent (W14's switched-off state), not approximated.

**"Compare methods" after a correct answer** (round 1's "compare alternative methods"; rows 6, 105). After a correct Submit, the feedback screen offers "Compare with the book's method": the learner's route beside the reference solution, with one AI-written line per difference in assumptions or steps (labelled), the learner's steps in the learner's voice and the reference labelled by source or model. The reference is read by id, not retrieved (§3.4 item 17): the item's own key and solution, which feedback already shows after grading (`FeedbackScreen.tsx:2139`, an `answer_display` reveal), and, for an imported textbook exercise whose key the sources spec's ladder labelled `book_solution`, the book's solution passage through the item's `canonical_exercise` (its §7.3–7.4), recorded as a `source_review` reveal (§5.4). A valid different route is named valid (PF-c's rule).

**Data.** No new table: `help_assists` already holds the kinds, draws and manifests (§5.7). **Routes:** none of its own: the worked-example search uses the sources spec's `source_rerank`; the compare line uses `workbench_check`'s route (it is a Reader call, not a hint, so it stays off the hint service's receipts). **Caps:** inside the workbench's per-session and daily caps, and the sources spec's for its calls. **Successor:** none; everything is context, display or a learner pick. Kill switches `workbench.help_outcomes_enabled` (off: the writer and chooser lose the lines), `workbench.examples_enabled`, `workbench.compare_methods_enabled`, `workbench.passage_draw_enabled`.

### 5.11 RW2d: workbench signals in the learner field (restored; algorithm successor)

Restores the deferred "interaction signals from abandoned workbench sessions and mid-work stuck steps" (round 2 §6's formative tier, the S5 placement signal). The owner's preference is live behind kill switches, so it is built as a successor rather than waiting on a usage trigger. **Presentation:** none; nothing new is shown (UX §11.1).
- **What changes.** The field's signal derivation (`field/signals.py:327`) gains two sources: a mid-work stuck step (as `stuck_confusion`, like an "I'm stuck" answer, tied to the item's facets and LOs), and help requested before the first step (as "help before work"). Abandoned sessions contribute only through these two, never as a failure. **Asking is never penalized** (field §3.1's guard): the signals steer routing for new material (taught first, never fast-tracked on early answers alone), never the record.
- **Why a successor.** Phase 6's fast-track rule reads these signals, so what is served changes (F3). RW2d takes the next free `mvp-0.N` at its merge, with receipts that record what the previous derivation would have produced.
- **Kill switch** `workbench.field_signals_enabled` (or the field's own namespace, as the RW2d agent and reviewer decide): off, the field derivation and every plan build are byte-identical to the predecessor on fixture copies.
- **Depends on** RW2b (the stuck and help rows) and the field's Phase 6 serving on `main`. **Gate** G41.
- **As built** (`docs/reviews/reader-workbench-RW2d.md`): the switch is `workbench.field_signals_enabled` (default on), the tag one constant `WORKBENCH_FIELD_SIGNALS_ALGORITHM_VERSION` (built as `mvp-0.31` after AD2+AD3's `mvp-0.30`). A stuck step counts when it has text (an empty one says nothing, the owner's ruling for an empty stuck answer), once per step chain from when it first held text, and kept whatever the submit later says (a session submitted as a stuck answer keeps it beside the stuck answer's own signal). Help before the first step is a hint, bridge or worked-example request whose logged step snapshot holds no ordinary step with text (no snapshot, no signal; an identity-only entry is read through the step row it names, and one not found leaves the snapshot unknown). Both are zero-mass rows linked to no attempt (so never the record, an attempt's assistance or the report's question strata) on the submitted attempt's LO, else the LO of the assessment contract the session froze (never today's item; none on record: unmapped), with the subject recorded with that contract (`workbench_sessions.contract_subject_id`; none recorded: the explicit `workbench:subject-unrecorded`, never today's label). Receipts: `field_signal_receipts` (migration 219, RECEIPT) for a fast-track or quick-check verdict the workbench rows changed, with the predecessor's verdict on the predecessor's blocking signals, and the slate component `taught_first_predecessor` for the new-material order; the migration was needed because a withheld fast-track writes no decision row of its own.

---

## 6. RW3 — Passage cues (misconception-aware reading)

### 6.1 What the learner sees

**Presentation:** UX §4.4 (marks, popover, actions, density), §3.4 (the margin marks and their legend), §4.12 (the attention budget); wireframe W1.

A thin mark in a gutter left of the text (`◌┆`, `●┆`, `▹┆`), and a one-line note on hover or focus. Marks are focusable in reading order (`Alt+↓` and `Alt+↑` move between them) and are labelled for screen readers with the full sentence. Three kinds, each saying what connects or what is on offer, never what the learner lacks:
- **Revisit a distinction** (`◌┆`): "Connects to a distinction you worked on: density vs cumulative probability. Not yet checked on your own since."
- **A new application** (RW3b, `●┆`): "A new use of something you've shown: conditioning on a component." The Reader shows what the learner can do, not only what went wrong (round 2 §3); a week of only `◌` marks is reported (§15).

Every note has a second line that says **why it is there and how the link was found** (round 2 §3: "subtle, contextual, and explainable"): "Because: Exercise 4.3 (2 Oct) and your open question about interval probability · found by LearnLoop's model, not by a test" — or "· from the Guide's citations" for RW3a's marks. A semantic match is never presented as evidence that the gap is still there.
- **Try this step first** (RW3b, restored; round 2 §7's "request a prediction", round 1's "an explanation, or try the derivation yourself?"): on a worked example or derivation in a section whose LOs carry a claim needing an independent check (§9.1) or an open hypothesis, "Try the next step before reading it?". Pressing it opens RW1b's "Let me try the next step" at that line. It is an offer in the margin, never a gate on the text.

**Actions on a cue** (a small popover, `Enter` on the mark):
- **Open** shows the earlier work: the cited attempt, its diagnosis, the inquiry, the named gap.
- **Check me** offers one short item, as a learner pick: an ordinary attempt on your own, ordinary evidence. It calls `find_practice` constrained to the cue's facets; once the sources spec merges, the proposals include textbook exercises and, when the cue's target has rival explanations open, the call uses `intent="discriminate"` with those rivals as `target_hypotheses`, so the item offered is the one that best tells them apart. **It stays in the Reader** (D40): the item opens in the working posture with the book hidden, because a check beside the book would be assisted (UX S7), and `Esc` after feedback returns to the passage. It is recorded as a learner pick through DL1's `learner_pick` (`daily_plan/engine.py:1914`) when the plan is on; before DL1 or with the plan off, it opens the item directly.
- **Not relevant** shows an optional text box, "why? (optional)" (W5).
- **Fewer marks like this** steps the density down one notch.
- **Cues off** works for this source or everywhere, from the mark itself (W11); both undo until the learner leaves the source, and "everywhere" also from Settings.

**Restraint (W12):** an attention budget of visible LearnLoop marks per section, set by the learner's density control "off · fewer · normal · more" (off is "cues off"; the others are 1, 2 or 4; default normal, remembered in vault config; `cues.density`) — the dialogue's "optional, collapsible and density-limited", and a display limit the learner owns, not a count that decides learning. On top of it, at every setting:
- **at most one LearnLoop mark per screenful** (UX S8; `cues.one_per_viewport`): sections run from half a page to twelve, so a per-section budget alone can stack two marks in one view. Marks are spaced when a section renders: a lower-ranked mark within one viewport height of a higher-ranked one is not shown, and its impression records why (§6.3). Nothing appears or disappears while scrolling;
- **never on the passage the learner was restored to** (it would greet them with a judgment), never in the workbench's source pane while working, never in skim mode, never animated, never a pop-up.
- The learner's own marks — an open question (`?`), unfinished work (`✎`), a kept comparison (`⇄`), an interactive version (`◇▸`) — are theirs, not LearnLoop's, and never count against the budget.

### 6.2 How a passage gets a cue

**RW3a: the Guide's own passages.** The Guide already computes, per section, passages cited by items whose LO has an active error (`_passages_by_section`, `reader_guidance.py:390`). RW3a renders those with `learner_signal = 'recent_misunderstanding'` as margin marks:
- **The statement is already the diagnoser's:** since `fix/guide-overlay` (merged at `f2f02e32`), `_learner_signal` reads `active_errors_by_learning_object_overlaid` (§3.4 item 1). RW3a reads the same plan and adds no fix.
- The section's existing ranking picks which cues show, within the learner's density budget and the one-per-screenful spacing; the margin and the rail always agree.
- Wording comes from fixed templates (G16); the Guide's "Revisit this to resolve: …" stays in the rail only.
- Taps are logged as `reader_action_invoked` events (`event`: `cue_opened`, `cue_check_me`, `cue_not_relevant`) and as `learner_card` verdicts (task `passage_cue`).

**RW3b: discovered links.** For targets the Guide's provenance links miss:
1. **Targets.** Recurrence groups and open hypotheses (T1, Slice 1), durable misconceptions and holding-pen candidates, named gaps (`learner_text_gaps`, once merged), and facets with demonstrated capabilities (for "new application" cues). Each is rendered as its LLM-written statement (R3).
2. **Shortlist.** `search_spans` with the target's statement as the query, over the open source's extraction (T6: BM25, dense and the provenance list fused by RRF; top-k, no cutoff, W6). *Restored (rows 101, 102):* two more lists join the fusion through `extra_lists` — passages with a RW4e `contrast` or `counterexample` role on the target's LOs or their `confusables`, and the misconception's `correction_source_span_ids` (the sources spec's `remediate` intent reads the same; RW3b uses the list directly). Source-authored contrasts ("independence vs zero covariance") are what the dialogue expected to work best for misconceptions.
3. **Judge.** One listwise call per target through `judge_listwise` (route `passage_cue_judge`, ledger task `passage_cue`): it judges every shortlisted passage `clarifies_distinction` | `new_application` | `prerequisite_for` | `try_first` | `none`, picks at most two, and quotes what it relies on (quote-verified against the block). `try_first` applies only to worked-example or derivation passages on a target with an unchecked claim or an open hypothesis. The judge also proposes the passage's instructional roles for RW4e at no extra call. The ledger cache makes a repeat free.
4. **Rank by value, then the budget** (restored, rows 25 and 51). The dialogue's cue value was P(relevant gap) × gain − interruption − false warning. v1 uses the one term LearnLoop already measures: **the probability that the target is still open**, read from existing state — the open episode's posterior or the facet's `hypothesis_marginal` for a hypothesis, the misconception's lifecycle (active or not), a named gap's status, and for "new application" and "try first" the active predictor's P(failure) on the passage's LO. Within a section, judged links and the Guide's passages are ordered by that probability, then by the judge's pick rank, Guide passages first on ties, and shown until the density budget is used. **A target already shown during this visit ranks after every target not yet shown** (round 1 G: LearnLoop "shouldn't interrupt every time the concept appears"): most of a clarification's gain is spent once the learner has seen it, so the order says so without a count. The gain and the two costs are not hand-set: they are what the holdout (item 6) and the "not relevant" rate measure, and learning them is the deferred learned cue policy (§17).
5. **When state changes,** the cache key is the target's statement and status; a new or resolved hypothesis recomputes only its own links. Links are computed in the background when a source opens (W12).
6. **Holdout.** With `cues.holdout_rate` (0.1), an eligible **discovered** cue is withheld at random, with a logged seed and propensity, so its effect on the next attempt on those facets can be estimated without asking anything of the learner (§15; D10). Guide cues (RW3a) are not held out: they show what the rail already shows. A cue suppressed by the density budget, the spacing rule or the restored-line rule is not a holdout: its impression records the reason, and the estimate leaves it out.

**Learner taps** are judge feedback: "not relevant" appends `learner_card` `different`, "open" and "check me" append `same`, on the judge's ledger task. Judge precision is measurable with no review chore. "Not relevant, because…" text stays judge feedback; it is not a learner-text gap source (it says something about the cue, not about the subject).

**The in-session Reader policy, in one place** (restored, row 75). The dialogue proposed a lightweight, mostly rule-based policy over a small set of actions, with "help always helps" as its one hard rule. It is spread across the phases, so here is the map:

| Dialogue action | Where | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| Continue without intervention | Everywhere | The default: no cue clears the budget, no offer is pending |
| Show a source relationship | Cues (§6.1); RW4d's `⇄` on a comparison the learner kept | Cues: value order, then the density budget and spacing. `⇄` is the learner's own mark, outside the budget |
| A short clarification | Cue "Open"; RW1c's "From your books" excerpt | On the learner's tap or question |
| Request a prediction | "Try this step first" cues; RW5's predictions | An offer in the margin; predictions only before a figure's first change |
| A targeted hint | RW2b's ladder: kinds and horizons from the adaptive-hints spec's service; a discriminating question on rung 1 when rivals are open (§5.2) | Help always helps (W11); the suggestion is sampled from the chooser's judgment and every rung is takeable (§5.5) |
| A partial worked example | Ladder levels 4–5; RW2c's example in your route | On request |
| Return to the original task | RW1a's reconnect and "back to where I was" | When a branch resolves |

### 6.3 Data

| Table | Phase | Role | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| — | RW3a | — | No table: cues are computed from the Guide plan; taps are events and ledger rows |
| `passage_links` | RW3b | WORKFLOW (paid outputs; never re-run on rebuild) | `id`, `extraction_id`, `span_id`, `target_kind` (`recurrence_group` \| `hypothesis` \| `misconception` \| `learner_text_gap` \| `demonstrated_facet`), `target_id`, `relation`, `quote`, `ledger_id`, `state_key`, `model`, `prompt_version`, `created_at` |
| `reader_cue_impressions` | RW3b | RECEIPT | `id`, `link_id` (null for a Guide cue), `visit_id`, `shown`, `suppressed_reason` (`holdout` \| `density` \| `spacing` \| `restored_line` \| null), `propensity`, `seed`, `p_open` and its source (the value used to order the cue), `density`, `response` (`opened` \| `check_me` \| `try_first` \| `not_relevant` \| `fewer` \| `none`), `response_text_hash`, `created_at`, `responded_at` |

### 6.4 Routes, spend, privacy

- **Route:** `passage_cue_judge` (RW3b; listwise; Codex by default, since it is background work).
- **Caps:** one call per target per source open; `cues.max_calls_per_source_open` (12); `cues.max_daily_cost_usd` ($0.50 metered).
- **Privacy:** hypothesis and gap statements plus source passages go to the provider, the same class as diagnosis.

### 6.5 Successor

None: display only, and nothing it writes enters evidence or serving. "Check me" and "Try this step first" are learner picks. Kill switches `cues.enabled` and `cues.discovery_enabled` (RW3b); `cues.try_first_enabled`; `cues.value_order_enabled` (off: the judge's order, as before the restoration); `cues.one_per_viewport` (off: the per-section budget alone); `cues.holdout_rate = 0` turns the holdout off.

---

## 7. RW4 — Library questions, source comparison and exercises from sources

### 7.1 Roles: ingest decides exclusions, proposals add detail

Ingest's deterministic `source_blocks.role_hint` stays the authority for **exclusion** (§3.4 item 2): T6's item-bound scope keeps `exercise` and `solution` blocks out of practice and feedback contexts, and nothing in this spec changes that. Roles shown or filtered come from `role_hint` and, after RW4e, from role proposals in `source_objects` (§7.9):
- **grouping** a library answer ("Definition", "Worked example", "Exercise", "Explanation" for `ordinary_prose`, and RW4e's finer roles where proposed, labelled "model's label");
- **positive filters**: the sources spec's `find_sources(roles=…)` (its §9.2), which this spec asks to match RW4e's proposals as well as `role_hint` (§18);
- **RW4b's exercise view** and RW5's candidates (`equation`, `figure`).

Where a passage has no finer role yet, the organizer (§7.3) may add one ("Geometric view", "Application") in its contribution line, labelled as the model's; RW4e persists it as a proposal.

### 7.2 `find_sources` and its policies: the sources spec's, consumed

`find_sources` is designed by `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md` (its §9.1, `source_retrieval/find_sources.py`), which kept this spec's first signature as a subset and owns its intents, its policies and its manifest. This spec uses it as designed there:

```python
find_sources(
    vault, repository, query, *,
    intent="explain",                 # | "remediate" | "alternative" | "worked_example" | "compare"
    policy,                           # derived on the server from the caller (its §5.2), never a model argument
    scope="teaching",                 # | "library"
    learning_object_ids=(), facet_ids=(), item=None,
    target_hypotheses=(), exclude_span_keys=(), roles=(),
    learner_work_md=None, personalized=True,
    verify=True, limit=8, ...,
) -> SourceHits                       # verbatim spans with source, section, page, revision + manifest
```

**The policies the Reader relies on** (the sources spec's §5.1; P3 invariant 10):

| Policy | When the Reader's call gets it | Learner-facing material |
|---|---|---|
| `reading` | The Reader context, library asks, compare, "another way" | Everything in scope; each span shown is recorded as exposure |
| `assisted_practice` | Inside a workbench session, with its item (bridging, the worked example in your route, the tutor while working) | No `exercise` or `solution` block, nothing linked to the item, nothing reproducing its key |
| `cold_attempt` | During a cold attempt | **Nothing**, by construction; every tool is refused |

`authoring` is internal to the sources spec's verifier and PF and never reaches a Reader surface. **This spec no longer defines a `post_attempt` policy** (§3.4 item 17): the sources spec narrowed post-attempt retrieval to "assisted for the attempted item, reading otherwise" for T6's reason (the next attempt's window spans feedback), and the one Reader feature that needed an item's own solution after a correct answer, "Compare methods", reads it by id instead (§5.10).

**The concept-graph expansion** (restored, row 65: "a concept graph surfaces prerequisites, applications and connected learning objects") is composed by RW4a as the caller, so the sources spec's function stays as designed. For the question's LOs, RW4a reads `LearningObject.prerequisites`, the LOs that list them as a prerequisite, and `.confusables` (`vault/models.py:330`), and makes one more `find_sources(intent="explain", learning_object_ids=<the expanded LOs>, verify=False)` call per expansion it asks for. The organizer's listwise call then judges the union, so the expansions are verified once, together, and each hit keeps its label ("Prerequisite", "Application"). Similarity never creates an edge (W6); the graph is read as it is. §18 asks the sources spec for an `expand` argument that would fold these into one call; when it lands, RW4a switches to it.

**Built once.** If RW4a lands before the sources spec's S3, RW4a creates `source_retrieval/find_sources.py` with that spec's §9.1 signature, the `explain` intent and the three learner-facing policies (derived in the sources spec's `instructional/policy.py`), and S3 extends it with its other intents. It never creates a second function (W13, G53).

**Receipts:** each call returns a manifest (query hash, effective policy and how it was derived, extraction and revision ids, returned keys, verification statuses; the sources spec's §5.5) that the consumer stores on its own row, as T6's tutor manifest does. Exposure is recorded where the consumer shows text, unchanged.

### 7.3 RW4a: ask your library

**Presentation:** UX §4.5 (the comparing posture: the library answer and the compare table beside the source), §4.6 (exercises from your books); wireframes W9, W10.

The library search box (T6: type for keyword, Enter for meaning plus rewrite) gains **"Ask"** (Shift+Enter). In the Reader the same ask is `Ctrl+Shift+F`, or "where else is this explained?" from a selection, and its answer opens in the **comparing posture** beside the open source, pushing a crumb, so nothing is lost.
- **The answer is sources first** (D12): no AI essay. Up to eight passages from `find_sources(intent="explain", scope="library")` plus the prerequisite and application expansions (§7.2), grouped by source and then by role, with the dialogue's own groups where they apply: **Foundation** (definitions and theorems), **Explanation** and finer views ("Geometric view"), **Prerequisite** (from the expansion), **Application** (from the expansion). The header says how they were found ("found by keyword + meaning · notes by LearnLoop's model"). Each hit shows its verbatim excerpt first; its line, "what this passage contributes", arrives when ready from the organizer: one listwise call through `judge_listwise` (route `library_organizer`, ledger task `library_contribution`) that judges each hit, picks and orders them, writes a line that must cite exactly that hit's key (validated in code), and proposes each hit's instructional roles for RW4e at no extra call. Each hit has *peek* (its neighbours, in the right side, with a crumb), *pin* and *open* (the left pane goes to that source, with a crumb).
- **"Make this explorable"** appears on a hit that is an `equation` or `figure` block when the organizer marks it feasible (RW5b).
- **"Ask the tutor about these"** opens a tutor exchange in the `reader` context whose citation allowlist is the pinned hits, across sources, through a pinned-span provider passed as `source_span_provider` (§3.4 item 11), and says so ("answers only from these 3 passages"). It is a `question_events` row, so it joins the outstanding-question queue, the field's question signals and learner-text gaps with no new adapter.
- **"Personalized" is a visible chip,** default on. When on, the organizer also sees the learner's open gaps and hypotheses on the hits' LOs and may mark one passage "speaks to your open question about …". Off, the order is plain retrieval rank (the dialogue's guard against confirmation bubbles), and the sources spec's `personalized = False` is passed through.
- **"Practice this question"** calls `find_practice(query)`, the same call as DL3's "Practice on…" box and RW1c's "Practice this": one RPC, which the sources spec counts among `find_practice`'s real callers (its S0). The learner picks from its proposals: a learner pick under DL1 (`daily_plan/engine.py:1914`), else the item opens directly, in the Reader's working posture with the book hidden (§5.1). **Once the sources spec merges,** the proposals include textbook exercises as their own candidate kind (shown as RW4b shows them, with source, page and key label), the call passes the question's LOs and facets, and a chosen textbook exercise goes through that spec's import and verifier ladder before it is served. Until then, when nothing fits, RW4b's fallback search is offered. Nothing is generated live.

**Compare sources** ("How do my sources treat this?"), from a selection, a library answer or pinned hits, calls the sources spec's `compare_sources` (its §9.4) with the learner as caller. That spec designs the counterparts, the comparison call, quote verification and storage (`source_comparisons`); it has no source cap, only a prompt-size bound, and lists the sources it could not fit as "also discussed in: …". It runs when the learner presses Compare (that spec's D5). The Reader shows:
- **a table**, one row per source (the learner's own passage first when compare started from a selection), with columns *contributes · assumes · differs from the others*. Each cell is a model line that quotes its own source's passage; a line that failed verification was already dropped (that spec's G12). The header says "differences kept · each line quotes its own source". **There is never a merged "consensus" answer.**
- **three voices apart** (W2): what each source says (quoted, `§`), what LearnLoop infers about the relation (`◇`, labelled), and the learner's own take (`›`, RW4d);
- **requested perspectives** ("a geometric view", "an application"), typed into a small field above the table and passed as `requested_perspectives`; a perspective no source covers says "not found in your library";
- "Ask the tutor about these" on the compared passages.

Until RW4d lands, the comparison is display only and the Reader stores nothing beyond the sources spec's comparison row; RW4d adds "Keep this comparison". **Built once:** if RW4a lands before the sources spec's S3, it builds `compare_sources` to that spec's §9.4 design, in `source_retrieval/compare.py` with its `source_comparisons` migration, and S3 takes it over.

### 7.4 RW4b: exercises from my sources

**Presentation:** UX §4.6.

**The Reader's view of textbook-exercise candidates.** `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md` makes textbook exercises practice candidates (its S1): `find_practice` returns them as their own kind, with source, page and span; choosing one imports it, checks its completed key with a verifier ladder built from PF-b and labels the key honestly (`book_solution`, `checked`, `panel_agrees`, `model_answer`, `disputed`), maps its competencies and attaches it to a matching family as a surface ("don't eliminate cards — change what they represent"). RW4b designs none of that. It shows the candidates wherever the Reader shows practice proposals (RW4a's "Practice this question", RW3's "Check me", RW1c's buttons):
- each candidate shows its passage, source and page, "open in the book" and "use this one", with the label *from your book · verbatim · answer completed by LearnLoop's model* and the key's status;
- "use this one" calls the sources spec's import path; the workbench opens at once with the verbatim stem while the import runs (§5.1), and the item becomes a learner pick when it is admitted;
- a candidate the sources spec's exposure rule marks (its page read, its solution or a revealed sibling seen, or an equivalent worked example studied, its §5.4) carries that label, and is never offered as a check on your own.

**Before the sources spec merges,** a learner-initiated fallback: when `find_practice` finds nothing, "Find an exercise in my sources" runs `find_sources(policy="reading", roles=["exercise"], scope="teaching")` over the query's LOs, and "use this one" runs the existing `import_exercises` (verbatim stem, AI-completed answer, rubric, facets, hints). Worked examples are not offered as exercises: their solution sits beside them, so they are passages, not practice (the sources spec's §7.1, D14). The fallback is retired when the sources spec's candidates are live (its switch, `library_exercises.fallback_enabled`, then defaults off). Automatic sourcing of RW6b's follow-ups from textbook exercises comes through the same `find_practice` call (§9.3).

### 7.5 Data

| Table | Phase | Role | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| `library_asks` | RW4a | WORKFLOW (paid outputs; never re-run on rebuild) | `id`, `caller` (`learner` \| `tutor`), `query_md`, `query_hash`, `personalized`, `requested_perspectives_json`, `manifest_json` (the `find_sources` manifests, including the expansion calls), `organizer_json` (lines with their hit keys and ledger ids), `comparison_id` (the sources spec's `source_comparisons.id`, when compared), `kept_json` (RW4d: the kept object and relation ids, each with its fine relation label), `kept_at`, `question_event_id` (when the tutor was asked), `model`, `prompt_version`, `created_at`. RW4d's columns are in RW4a's migration, so RW4d adds none |

RW4a writes nothing to `source_object_relations` or `source_objects`; RW4d and RW4e write proposals there (§7.8, §7.9). The comparison itself is the sources spec's row.

### 7.6 Routes, spend, privacy

- **Routes:** `library_organizer` (listwise; serves live requests, so the fast route is recommended, as for RW2b). T6's routes and the sources spec's (`source_rerank`, `source_compare`) do the rest.
- **Caps:** library ask: one retrieval plus one per expansion (T6's own caps; expansion calls skip verification), one organizer call; compare: the sources spec's `source_service.compare_max_cost_usd` and prompt bound; `library.max_daily_cost_usd` ($1.00 metered) for the organizer.
- **Privacy:** source text and the question go to the provider. The personalized chip adds gap statements; it can be turned off.

### 7.7 Successor

None. Retrieval is context (T6's ruling) and the organizer's lines are display. Kill switches: `library_ask.enabled`, `library_ask.expand_graph`, `reader_compare.enabled` (the Reader's compare UI; the sources spec's `source_service.compare` gates the function), `library_exercises.enabled`, `library_exercises.fallback_enabled` (all off: T6's library search exactly as merged).

### 7.8 RW4d: syntopic reading, kept (restored)

**Presentation:** UX §4.5 (compare, perspectives, the three bands, keep), §3.4 (the `⇄` mark); wireframe W10.

The dialogue (round 1 E, round 2 §5, round 3 §5C) asked for syntopic reading as a first-class operation — "`compare_sources(query, facets, requested_perspectives)` … returning source-local claims; equivalent claims, different assumptions and real disagreement kept apart" — and for claims to be kept "with their source context" while "model-proposed relations are reviewed before entering a trusted graph". The operation is now the sources spec's `compare_sources` (its §9.4), callable by the learner, by the tutor through that spec's tool requests (its S4), and later by the router. That spec defers keeping a comparison as relation proposals until "the Reader's compare UI ships a 'keep this comparison' action" (its §19): RW4d is that action. P3 §7.1–7.2 already provides the layer: per-source objects and relations that stay proposals.

**What RW4d adds to the Reader:**
- **The tutor as a caller.** When the tutor's answer carries a `compare_sources` request, RW1c renders it as "How do your books treat this?"; pressing it opens the same table (§4.6).
- **Keep this comparison.** On the learner's "Keep", and only then, from the stored comparison row:
  - each shown claim becomes a `source_objects` row with a version (`object_type = 'claim'`, or `definition` for a definition; `authorship = 'ai'`, `status = 'proposed'`, `revision_id` of its source; one citation with span and quote);
  - each relation becomes a `source_object_relations` row, `authorship = 'ai'`, `review_status = 'proposed'`, typed by §3.4 item 15's mapping, with its fine label kept in `library_asks.kept_json`;
  - **"My take"** (the band under the table, *your take (optional)*) adds the learner's own line on any pair or claim: a `learner_connects` relation with `learner_text`, `authorship = 'learner'` (P3 invariant 8's connect-it semantics). The three voices — the source, LearnLoop's inference, the learner's belief — stay separate rows with separate authorship (W2, P3 invariant 9).
  - *Remove* undoes a keep: the proposals are marked `superseded`, never deleted.
- **Kept comparisons come back.** A passage with a kept comparison shows `⇄` in the margin; it is the learner's own mark, outside the cue budget (§6.1), and focusing it reopens the table. A later library ask on the same claims says "you compared these on 3 Oct". Reviewing a proposed relation uses P3's existing source-object review path; nothing in this spec promotes one.

**What it never does.** No canonical concept edge, facet merge, prerequisite or learning-object change (P3 §7.3; G18). No claim extraction over passages the learner did not compare: that is the deferred full-library claim index (§17).

**Data.** No new table: `library_asks` links the comparison (`comparison_id`) and the kept ids (`kept_json`), and kept claims and relations live in `source_objects` and `source_object_relations`. **Routes:** none of its own (the comparison is the sources spec's `source_compare`). **Caps:** that spec's. **Successor:** none; proposals and display.
