Kill switch `reader_compare.keep_enabled`.

### 7.9 RW4e: instructional roles (restored)

**Presentation:** UX §4.5 (role labels on hits, "model's label" for a proposal).

Round 3 §5A: "index instructional roles, not just passages" — definition or theorem, explanation or intuition, worked example, exercise, figure, counterexample, prerequisite passage, alternative treatment — "several roles per passage… with confidence and provenance… anchored to immutable source versions". Ingest's single deterministic `role_hint` covers four of these; the rest need judgment. The sources spec defers its own labeller, with a trigger that includes this spec's (its §19); RW4e builds it here, on the Reader's side of the source-object layer, and the sources spec's retrieval reads the result.

**Roles accumulate from judgments already being made.** The Reader's own judges — the library organizer (RW4a) and the cue judge (RW3b) — already read each passage they judge. Their outputs gain one field per passage: `roles`, one or more of `definition`, `theorem`, `explanation`, `intuition`, `worked_example`, `exercise`, `figure`, `counterexample`, `contrast` (a passage that distinguishes two easily confused ideas), `prerequisite`, `alternative_treatment`, `application`, each with a confidence (`high` \| `medium` \| `low`). No extra call. §18 asks the sources spec's `source_rerank` and `source_compare` to return the same optional field, so their judgments add roles too.
- **Stored** as a `source_objects` proposal per span and role: `object_type` mapped by §3.4 item 14, `authorial_role` the role, `content_json` holding the confidence, the judge, its ledger id and the prompt version, `authorship = 'ai'`, `status = 'proposed'`, one citation with the span, its block hash and the revision. A second judge proposing the same role on the same span adds a version, so agreement across judges is visible.
- **An owner-run batch labeller** for when the full index is wanted now: `learnloop reader label-roles --source <id> [--dry-run]` walks the source's blocks in reading order in listwise batches (route `role_labeller`, spend cap `roles.batch_max_cost_usd`), writing the same proposals. It never runs on the reading hot path.

**Used positively, never to exclude.** Through the sources spec's `roles` filter, once it matches `authorial_role` proposals as well as `role_hint` (`low` confidence counts only when nothing better exists; §18). RW3b prefers `contrast` and `counterexample` passages for distinction cues (§6.2); the sources spec's `remediate` and `alternative` intents can read the same proposals. **Exclusions stay with ingest's `role_hint` and item links:** an AI proposal never admits a block into an item-bound scope and never removes one from an exclusion (G44).

**Data.** No new table. **Routes:** `role_labeller` (listwise, batch only). **Successor:** none; labels on sources. Kill switches `roles.on_demand_enabled`, `roles.positive_filter_enabled`.

---

## 8. RW5 — Explorable figures

RW5a is the first figure phase, now including the dialogue's flagship example (restored: shaded areas, integral readouts, PMF bars, animation and region selection were cut from the pilot). RW5b adds panel kinds for the dialogue's other domains and figures offered by retrieval and the tutor (§8.7).

**RW5d** — see `spec_reader_show_me_figures_v1.md`: "Show me" from a question, a passage or both (`FigureRequestV1`, grounded in the learner's books), a sketch tier for what the grammar can't express, and figures as a help kind.

### 8.1 What the learner sees

**Presentation:** UX §4.7 (the inline figure, labels, states), §7.2–7.3 (sliders, readouts, the values table, reduced motion); wireframe W11.

"Make this explorable" on a selected equation, a figure block, a short passage or, *restored* (row 46), **a region of a figure**: on the original-page view the learner drags a rectangle over part of a figure (`Ctrl`+drag), and only that crop (`build_block_region`, `span_view.py:266`, with the selection's bounding box) plus the caption and the surrounding paragraphs go to the figure author.
- **While it builds,** one dim line under the passage, "building an interactive version — keep reading" (with "about N s" once the report has measured it); reading continues.
- **The result sits inline under its passage** (UX S9), at the full width of the text column, not in the 360 px rail, which is too narrow for a plot with sliders; a figure belongs to its passage. Once built it collapses to one line (*▸ interactive version · AI-built · matches the book's equation (checked)*) *(RW5a: "· AI-built illustration · not checked against the book"; the checked line is deferred to RW5c)* and expands in place; one figure is expanded at a time. A built figure leaves the learner's own `◇▸` mark in the margin. Open, it shows sliders with numeric fields for the parameters, linked plots or readouts, a *values as a table* toggle (`t`) that gives every plot a data-table equivalent for screen readers, and, by default, one **prediction** before the first change (for the dialogue's two-sided exponential, "If λ doubles, what happens to P(X ≤ 0)?" — it stays at p, which is worth discovering — or "Which shaded area is P(X < 0)?"); the learner answers in a word, a choice or a click on an area, then sees the result.
- **What it shows and assumes** (round 2 §2's mechanism extraction: "variables, relationships, valid parameter ranges, assumptions, and what the figure is supposed to demonstrate"): one line above the controls says what the figure demonstrates, and its assumptions are listed beside it, each quoting the source where the source states it, all labelled AI-written.
- **"What changed, and why?"** (round 2 §2's "an explanation of what changes and why", restored): after the result, an optional one-line box. A missed prediction with its answer becomes a `reader_formative` learner text (tier 2, W1).
- **Animation** (restored): a parameter marked `animate` gets a play button that sweeps it across its range ("animate the associated CDF"). It is the same evaluation as dragging the slider. Nothing moves until the learner presses play (D39), and under reduced motion play becomes step buttons.
- **The label is always visible:** *(RW5a: every figure carries the illustration label; the "(checked)" variant is deferred to RW5c, §8.3.)* "AI-built interactive · matches the source's equation (checked)" for a verified reconstruction; "AI-built illustration of an interpretation (not checked against the source)" otherwise.
- **Unsupported requests** say what was asked for and why it can't be drawn yet ("can't draw this yet — needs a simulation the figure language can't express · noted"); the report counts them by kind, which informs RW5b's order and the deferred generated-code figures (§17).
- **"Not right?"** (round 3 §5D: figures can be "reused, versioned, and improved instead of regenerating"): an optional line of text ("the slider for λ should start at 0.1") asks the figure author for a new version with that note; the new version is verified like the first, the earlier one is kept, and the request counts against the figure spend cap.
- The same figure reopens instantly from the cache.

### 8.2 The spec, not code

The model writes an **`InteractiveFigureSpecV1`** (JSON, strict schema). A trusted React renderer draws it. **No generated code runs anywhere** (generated-code figures are deferred, §17).

```text
spec_version    "ifs-v1"
kind            reconstructed | illustrative
source_claims   [{span_id, quote}]                     # quote-verified
region          {span_id, bbox}?                        # restored: a crop of a figure
demonstrates    text                                    # re-review: what the figure is meant to show; AI-written, labelled
assumptions     [{text, span_id?, quote?}]              # re-review: quoted where the source states them
parameters      [{name, label, min, max, step, default, animate?}]
definitions     [{name, expr}]                          # the grammar below
panels          function_plot {x:{var,min,max}, series:[{expr,label}], markers?,
                               shade?: [{series, from, to, label}]}      # restored: areas under a series
                pmf_bars      {k:{var,min,max}, series:[{expr,label}],
                               highlight?: {from, to, label}}           # restored: discrete distributions
                linear_map_2d {matrix:[[expr,expr],[expr,expr]]}   # unit square and grid under the map
                readout       {label, expr}                         # may use integral() and sum()
                # RW5b: heatmap_2d, vector_field_2d, trajectory_2d (§8.7)
invariants      [{kind: finite | bounded | nonnegative | integrates_to_one | sums_to_one
                       | monotone_nondecreasing | equals_source, ...}]
predictions     [{prompt, change:{param:value}, observe: expr | choices | area_label}]
annotations     [{target, text}]                        # restored: the dialogue's "pedagogical annotations"; AI-written, labelled
```

**The grammar:** numbers; parameter and variable names; `+ - * / ^`; unary minus; `exp log sqrt abs sin cos tan atan erf Phi min max floor`; `piecewise((cond, expr)…, else)` with comparisons; `pi`, `e`; and, *restored*, `integral(expr, var, a, b)` and `sum(expr, k, a, b)` with finite numeric or parameter bounds. Caps on length, node count and nesting (no integral inside an integral).
- **Two evaluators, one grammar:** a hand-written TypeScript parser and evaluator (no `eval`, no `Function`), and a Python twin in the sidecar. Both implement `integral` with the same fixed adaptive Simpson rule (same depth and tolerance) and `sum` exactly over integers. One golden corpus, run in both vitest and pytest, keeps them identical within 1e-9 relative error, and within 1e-6 for integrals.
- **A shaded area** is drawn under its series between `from` and `to` (expressions, clipped to the plot range) and its value is a linked `integral` readout, so moving `p` or `λ` moves the area and its number together.

### 8.3 Verification (before a figure shows)

Run in the sidecar:
1. **Structural.** Every expression parses; values are finite on a parameter grid (three points per parameter, at most 81 combinations); panels reference defined names.
2. **Invariants.** Each declared invariant holds on the grid, numerically (a density integrates to 1 within 1e-3 by Simpson in Python; a PMF sums to 1 within 1e-9 over its support; a CDF is non-decreasing from 0 to 1; values stay bounded). A shaded area's readout is checked against an independent high-resolution integration in Python.
3. **Source match** (`kind = reconstructed` only). *(Deferred to RW5c; see the amendment below.)* PF's pattern: the verifier (route `figure_verifier`) writes a sympy check, with a negative control, that the spec's defining expression equals the source equation quoted in `source_claims`; the sandbox runs it.

**Outcomes:** passing 1–3 makes the figure `verified`; failing 3 alone downgrades it to `illustrative` with its label; failing 1 or 2 means it is not shown ("couldn't build a reliable version of this", with the reason). Verdicts go to `judgment_ledger` (task `figure_verified`, judge `verifier` or `llm`).

**Amendment (owner decision, 2026-10-21; RW5a as built).** RW5a ships **without** step 3. Every figure is labelled an illustration ("AI-built illustration of an interpretation · not checked against the book"); steps 1–2 still decide whether a figure is shown, and the `figure_verifier` route is removed. Step 3 and the "matches the book's equation (checked)" label are **deferred to RW5c**, which designs code-owned, element-scoped labels and has that design reviewed before any code (`docs/reviews/reader-workbench-RW5a.md`, "RW5c"). **Standing scope note for RW5c:** "checked" certifies that the figure's *definition* equals the source equation, symbolically and exactly, and that the displayed source-labelled content *is* that definition; it never certifies float values at points the check did not sample (rendering resolution, rounding at a reachable value off the sample), and such divergences are Low. G23 holds vacuously until RW5c.


### 8.4 Predictions

A prediction is formative (W1, tier 2): stored on `figure_predictions`, never as an attempt or an evidence observation. The dialogue called a recorded prediction "a formative observation" (round 2 §2); restored, that means a missed prediction, its answer and its "what changed?" text become a `reader_formative` learner text, so learner-text gaps can name the belief behind it, and RW6b counts it among the session's open questions (§9.3). RW6a's recap lists predictions that missed as "worth a second look". Numeric use as a weak observation stays deferred (§17).

### 8.5 Data, routes, spend, privacy

| Table | Role | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| `interactive_figures` | WORKFLOW (paid outputs; never re-run on rebuild) | `id`, `extraction_id`, `span_ids_json`, `region_json`, `asset_id`, `origin` (`selection` \| `library` \| `tutor`), `supersedes_id` and `revision_note` (a "not right?" version), `spec_json`, `spec_version`, `kind`, `verification` (`verified` \| `illustrative` \| `failed` \| `unsupported`), `verification_report_json`, `unsupported_kind`, `model`, `prompt_version`, `input_hash` (reuse key), `created_at` |
| `figure_predictions` | RAW_LEDGER | `id`, `figure_id`, `prediction_index`, `learner_answer`, `observed_json`, `matched`, `explanation_text`, `created_at` |

- **Routes:** `figure_author` (chat; image input when a figure asset or crop is included: an OpenRouter vision model or Codex's image input); `figure_verifier` (chat). *(RW5a has no `figure_verifier`; RW5c re-adds what it needs.)*
- **Caps (money):** two author attempts and one verifier call per request; `figures.max_daily_cost_usd` ($1.00 metered). The first draft's `figures.max_per_day` (20) count is replaced by the spend cap.
- **Privacy:** source text and, for figures, the figure image or crop. No learner data.

### 8.6 Successor

None: content and formative. Kill switches `figures.enabled`, `figures.predictions_enabled`, `figures.region_enabled`, `figures.revisions_enabled`.

### 8.7 RW5b: more figure kinds, and figures offered by retrieval (restored)

The dialogue named the domains (round 2 §2): "probability distributions, optimization objectives, matrix transformations, gradients, attention mechanisms, and dynamical systems". RW5a covers distributions and 2-D linear maps. RW5b adds three panel kinds to the same grammar and renderer, and lets retrieval offer figures (round 3 §5D).

**Panels** (both evaluators, golden-corpus parity, the same verification):
- `heatmap_2d {x:{var,min,max}, y:{var,min,max}, expr, contours?}`: objective landscapes, joint densities, and attention-like weight matrices written as expressions over indices (`exp(s(i,j)) / sum(exp(s(i,k)), k, 1, n)`);
- `vector_field_2d {x, y, u: expr, v: expr}`: gradients and flows, with optional overlaid `heatmap_2d`;
- `trajectory_2d {x0, y0, dx: expr, dy: expr, t_max, steps}` or `iterate_2d {x0, y0, fx: expr, fy: expr, n}`: a dynamical system by fixed-step RK4, or a discrete map such as gradient descent with a learning-rate slider. The step count is a parameter cap on computation, and the integrator is identical in both evaluators so their trajectories agree within the corpus tolerance.

**Presentation:** UX §4.7 (offered figures), as RW5a.

**Figures offered, not only requested** (row 104; the sources spec defers tutor-initiated figures until RW5 merges, its §19, so this is where they are built). When the library organizer (RW4a) or the Reader-context tutor (RW1c) cites an `equation` or `figure` block, its judgment includes `explorable: yes | no` (can the block support a parameterized, verifiable figure?) at no extra call. A `yes` adds "Make this explorable" to that hit or answer; pressing it runs RW5a's pipeline with `origin = library | tutor`. Retrieval supplies the equation, figure and assumptions; the figure pipeline decides feasibility and verifies, as the dialogue kept them separate. Built figures are reused by input hash and versioned by spec version.

**What stays out:** anything the grammar cannot express (general simulations, agent-based models, arbitrary code) — the generated-code figures deferred in §17, with the unsupported-kind counts as their evidence.

**Data:** RW5a's tables (`origin` already has the values). **Routes:** RW5a's. **Successor:** none. Kill switches `figures.advanced_panels_enabled`, `figures.offers_enabled`.

---

## 9. RW6 — "Last time here", the digest, the router card and cold follow-ups

### 9.1 RW6a: "Last time here" (deterministic, no model)

**Presentation:** UX §4.8 (the card, wrap up, the narrative), §2 moments 0, 10 and 11 (the felt sequence); wireframe W12.

When the learner opens a source they have read before, the rail shows a compact card at the head of the Guide (the returning posture). Headings carry no counts ("Still open", never "2 open questions") and nothing is presented as overdue (UX U12):
- **the last passage** and "continue there" (P3 restoration, as today);
- **Still open:** inquiries on this source, each with "open" (to its crumb) and status chips (resolve / revisit once / keep open);
- **Unfinished:** problems from RW2a (`✎`), each with its step count, the help used so far and "Resume this problem";
- **Worth a second look:** predictions that missed (RW5a), next-step, why-step and assumption answers self-checked "partly" or "no" (RW1b), and reconnect "still unclear" answers (RW1a);
- **Since last time** (UX S10; the dialogue's "visible capability gains", round 1 §2, and "reinforce what the learner is capable of doing", round 2 §3): the LOs this source teaches that became demonstrated since the previous session ended — `lo_certification(...).demonstrated` now (`goals/goal_certification.py:275`), with the date of the certifying attempt after that session's end — shown as *● piecewise CDF — demonstrated on your own (Mon) · on the map*, and checks retired since as no longer needed. It uses the well's markers (W2), never a percentage;
- after RW6b: **Coming up**, the scheduled checks as a dated timeline (UX S15), each with its date, purpose, "why" and undo, the next three by date and then "later ▸" with no number (D36); and the narrative, collapsed (*▸ summary written by LearnLoop's model from what you did*), never the first thing shown.

The card collapses after the first scroll or "continue"; *last time ▸* in the Guide header reopens it; *dismiss* hides it for this source until the next session. Today's re-entry panel (DL1, goal-optional re-entry) can show the same card for the most recent source. The card is a pure read over `question_events`, `reader_section_progress`, `reader_authored_questions`, `workbench_sessions`, `figure_predictions`, `practice_attempts`, commitments and the reader envelope; nothing new is stored.

A **reader session** is one visit on the reader envelope (`visit_id`), or several visits less than 30 minutes apart. RW6a's typed summary is computed per session:
- sections read (from `reader_section_progress`);
- inquiries opened, resolved and still open, with their roots;
- workbench sessions: item, highest help level, submitted or abandoned, grade;
- cues opened or marked not relevant; predictions missed;
- gaps named by learner-text gaps from this session's texts;
- facets touched;
- **"claims needing an independent check":** facets where the session's work was assisted only (help level 3 or more, or a source-visible attempt) and no unassisted success exists since;
- *restored (rows 58, 68):* **the session's formative records**, `ReaderObservationV1`, one per tier-2 signal (W1). Each has the shape the dialogue sketched (round 2 §6), restricted to routing: `kind` (`still_unclear`, `next_step_missed`, `why_step_missed`, `assumption_missed`, `prediction_missed`, `check_questionable`, `hint_taken`, `doesnt_connect`, `discriminate_answer`), `source_event_ids`, `span_ids`, `item_id` and `step_id` where they apply, `facet_candidates` (the item's facets, or the LOs of the spans), `observed` (a short typed field, never prose: the verdict, the predicted and observed values, the help level), `hypotheses` (gap ids named from it, rival causes it was asked against), `assistance` (level, reveal), and `authority: "routing_only"`. They are not compiled by a second model: learner text goes through learner-text gaps (the compiler, W13), verdicts and levels are copied from their rows. The numbers stay with the estimator, which never reads these records (W1).

**Wrap up** (restored, row 23: "an optional session-ending synthesis under a minute"). The rail's footer shows "Wrap up (under a minute)", and `w` opens it, once a session has **activity**: at least one inquiry, workbench session, formative answer (a next step, why step, assumption or prediction) or kept comparison. Scrolling, highlights and cue taps alone don't count, because a pure reading session has nothing to wrap (D34). **After the learner leaves a source with activity, the offer appears where they land** — Today or the library — as one dim, self-dismissing line for one view (*Reading wrap-up ready — under a minute*), never in the Reader being closed, where a note would vanish before it could be read (UX S11), and never blocking (P3 invariant 1, W12). Whether to show it is a pure read: the last reader session had activity and no `wrap_up_opened` event followed it. Wrap up shows three short lists from the typed summary — **resolved**, **still open**, **worth reinforcing** (work done with help `◌`, missed predictions and next steps, the claims needing an independent check, and after RW6b the checks coming up with their dates) — each line with one action (resolve, keep open, revisit once, undo). The narrative sits collapsed at its foot when ready. One key closes it. Nothing new is stored: actions write what the same chips write elsewhere. Kill switch `recap.wrap_up_enabled`.

### 9.2 RW6b: the narrative and the router card

**Presentation:** UX §4.8 (the narrative, collapsed), §4.9 (checks on your own and the way back); wireframes W12, W13, W16.

**The narrative** is optional (`digest.narrative_enabled`, default on): at most 120 words, written by route `reader_digest` from the typed summary only, citing event ids (validated against the summary), labelled "summary written by LearnLoop's model". It is generated once per session in the background and shown collapsed in the "Last time here" card and in Wrap up. It is never a source of a `●` (round 2 §1: a model's guess written into a summary must never become evidence).

**Reader activity in the router's learner-state card.** `reader_activity_for_card(lo_ids, as_of) -> ReaderActivityV1` is a pure read, like `named_gaps_for_card()` on the text-gaps branch. For the candidates' LOs it returns: open inquiries (root question excerpt, age, depth); assisted-only work (facet, highest help level, date); pending reader follow-ups (each with its purpose, date and valuation); missed predictions; *restored (rows 59, 72):* **open hypotheses** from the session (gaps named from its text, rival causes still open, with their source rows), **the session's formative records** (`ReaderObservationV1`, typed, no prose), **kept comparisons** on these LOs (RW4d: the source relationships the dialogue asked the card to carry), and **candidate actions** — the dialogue's "possible next actions" — as a typed list with where each came from (a pending follow-up with its valuation; continue reading at the next unfinished section, or the next core section of a reading path; near-transfer practice proposed by `find_practice`), never a choice; the latest narrative, labelled model-written, with its cited event ids. Router R2 renders it as a "Reader activity" section of `LearnerStateCardV1` (router §4). The card stays deterministic from the ledger, because the narrative is a persisted, cited row (the teach-back summary pattern, router §15.1). **Nothing in it is evidence** (W1). Before R2 the function exists and has tests, but nothing renders it.

### 9.3 RW6b: cold follow-ups (the owner's "short unfamiliar problem")

**No count cap.** The owner's decision of 2026-10-12 (§20.7): value of information chooses, numbers and dates the checks, and the learner's real limits — the daily plan's minutes and the spend caps — bound them. Several follow-ups may be worth it, to find the true misconception, to decide between rival explanations, or to decide between actions. Follow-ups are served through DL2's "Check once". **RW6b builds no valuation of its own** (W13): it assembles the questions and candidates and asks the machinery that already values probes (§3.2).

1. **The open questions.** From the session's typed summary (§9.1), RW6b lists what a later independent attempt could tell LearnLoop about this learner. Each is read where it already lives; no new learner state is written:
   - **claims needing an independent check** (assisted-only work on a facet): the hypotheses are "holds unaided" and "doesn't", with the active predictor's P(success) and interval as the prior (field §4);
   - **rival explanations** still open on the session's facets: the locked hypothesis set and replayed posterior of an open probe episode (`episode_posterior`); a diagnosed attempt's rival causes (`unresolved_cause_factors.candidate_causes_json`); open `causal_hypotheses`; the facet's `hypothesis_marginal`; and, once learner-text gaps has merged, gaps named from this session's text;
   - *restored (RS3; rows 45, 52, 70):* **the session's formative records** (§9.1): a missed prediction, a "still unclear", a missed next-step or assumption answer, a questionable check never resolved, a discriminating answer that left two causes standing. Each adds the facets it touched as a question ("does the learner now hold this unaided?") and, where learner-text gaps named a gap from it, that gap as a rival explanation. They change which questions are asked, never a prior's number (W1, tier 2).
2. **Candidate checks.**
   - For each facet with an open question, `find_practice(query, constraints)` proposes unfamiliar items with the sources spec's exact filters (§3.4 item 7; its §8.1): `unattempted_only` (no attempt on the item), `exclude_family_ids` (the families of every item whose solution the session revealed, from the reveal ledger) and `exclude_revealed_families`. Admission already applies the scheduler's reserves.
   - *Consumed from `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md` once its S1 and S2a merge:* the call passes that spec's `intent` — `retention` for a claim needing an independent check, `discriminate` with the rivals as `target_hypotheses` for rival explanations — and its proposals include **textbook exercises** as their own candidate kind. A chosen textbook exercise goes through that spec's import, verifier ladder and family attachment before its commitment is created; only a key labelled `book_solution`, `checked` or `panel_agrees` can stand as a check on your own (its §7.3).
   - Open probe episodes on those facets add their eligible instruments (`probe_episodes.eligible_instruments`).
   - **Relevance and exposure are the sources spec's steps.** RW6b hands the candidates and the open questions to `value_candidates` (its §8.3). Its first step is the only model judgment: one listwise call keeps the candidates that test the capability or bear on the rival hypotheses, quoting what in the candidate does so, and never picks a winner or a number. Its exposure rule (its §5.4) keeps out of `discriminate` and `retention` any candidate whose span, solution or family was exposed, or whose **equivalent worked example the learner studied** within the cold delay (RS8, row 106: "a textbook exercise from an unread page is a poor cold test if the learner has seen an equivalent worked example"), and discounts a `related` one with `familiarity_discount`. **Built once:** if RW6b lands before S2a, RW6b builds `value_candidates`, `open_hypotheses` and the exposure rule to that spec's design, in its `instructional/` package, and S2a takes them over (W13, G35, G53).
   - **Novelty labels** (row 79: "unseen wording, unseen surface and genuinely unfamiliar application; new constants are not transfer"). From exact facts, a candidate in the family of an item the session worked is `same_surface` ("same idea, new numbers") and is never labelled transfer. Telling `new_surface` from `new_application` takes a judgment, so §18 asks the sources spec's relevance step to return it relative to the session's worked items; until then such a candidate is labelled "a new problem", and "a new application" is never claimed. The label sets the purpose wording and is recorded on the receipt.
3. **Value each candidate with the existing machinery,** through `value_candidates` (the sources spec's §8.3, which composes exactly these functions):
   - **Information about the hypotheses (EIG).** Instruments use `instrument_expected_information_gain`, or the predictive variant when its target set is adequate; the two are never added together (probe EIG spec §7.4). Ordinary items use the per-facet EIG that the post-attempt follow-up slate already computes (`facet_expected_information_gain` over `hypothesis_marginal`), with `_misconception_discrimination` where rival misconceptions are open. Familiar surfaces are discounted with `familiarity_discount` (`learner/recall_coverage.py:293`), as the scheduler does for probes, so a near sibling of a problem just worked counts for less than a genuinely unfamiliar one. A candidate the exposure rule judged `related` to a worked example the learner studied is discounted the same way; one judged `equivalent` is never valued as a check on your own at all (it stays available as ordinary practice). A `same_surface` candidate is never labelled transfer.
   - **Value for the next decision (EVSI).** Where the session left a decision pending between actions (which repair to try; repair or move on), the loss table is `action_loss.build_loss_table`'s wasted learner-minutes and the value is `evsi.robust_evsi` (point, LCB, UCB). Where no loss table exists, `robust_composition.evaluate_selection` converts the robust EIG to minutes, as the robust probe path already does.
   - **Rank and stop with `evsi.rank_feasible`.** Rank is LCB / (expected minutes + burden); expected minutes come from DL1's estimator (`daily_plan/estimates.py:138`). Checks are added greedily, each valued conditionally on those already chosen (the rule `probe_episodes.plan_precommitted_block` uses for a block), so two checks that would tell LearnLoop the same thing are not both scheduled. Adding stops when the best remaining candidate's LCB ≤ λ·minutes + burden. An `abstain` verdict falls back to the EIG-per-minute order with the same stop, labelled "value uncertain".
   - **The number follows.** A session gets whatever clears the stop: often none, sometimes one, sometimes several, for example to separate two misconceptions and then to check retention.
   - **No new constants.** λ (1, minutes as numeraire), the burden (0) and the nat-to-minutes scale (60) are the existing decision parameters, labelled heuristic in `diagnosis/robust_composition.py:44`–`:55`, and read from there so a calibration lands everywhere at once. Fitting them is the `evsi/calibration` track's job (the sources spec's §11.4, the decision-value spec's §9); RW6b's receipts and the learner's undo reasons give it data (§15). Until then every valuation says "LearnLoop's estimate; its scale is not yet calibrated".
4. **Date each check by what it is for.**
   - A **discriminating check**, valued for separating rival explanations before a repair decision, is dated for the next day's plan. DL1 never grows today's plan (§6.1), and the check is worth less once the repair is chosen without it.
   - A **retention check**, valued for whether an assisted claim holds unaided, is dated at the practiced item's effective due (DL2 §5.3), when forgetting is expected and a success says most (router §15.2). Without FSRS state the date is `followups.fallback_days` (3) away, labelled as a default.
   - **`followups.max_wait_days` (14) is a time cap:** no claim waits longer than two weeks for its check.
   - The date goes in `due_hint`. **Cross-spec request:** DL2's dated branch reads `due_hint` when present and falls back to `capture.test_me_later_days` otherwise (§18).
5. **Schedule as content, not as a new serving rule.** Each follow-up is a `test_me_later` commitment (disposition `one_check_pending`) on its item, with `provenance = {"via": "reader_followup", "summary_id": …, "purpose": "discriminating" | "retention", "novelty": …, "valuation_ref": …, "inquiry_root_event_ids": […], "anchor_span_ids": […]}`. DL2 serves each exactly like any other "Check once": a dated learner request among the maintain lane's scheduled checks, cold (W7), with restoration after the measurement closes.
6. **Time and money are the only limits.** There is no count cap per session or per day.
   - **Minutes.** The plan's finite minutes bound what is served on any day. Scheduled checks sit in the maintain lane, which takes at most `plan.maintain_max_share` of the minutes (DL1 §6.2); what doesn't fit stays in the full list as "later", never as debt (DL1 §6.1). Dating by purpose spreads checks across days.
   - **Money.** `followups.max_daily_cost_usd` ($0.50 metered) covers the relevance and exposure calls made for RW6b; they also count against the sources spec's `source_service.max_daily_cost_usd`, and whichever is reached first stops them. The valuation itself makes no model call.
   - **From router R2 on,** each pending follow-up is also a block candidate that the router values (planner value with robust EVSI, router §3). On a crowded day it then competes with reviews for the minutes instead of always going first.
7. **Re-valuation.** Before each day's plan is built, RW6b re-values its pending follow-ups with the same machinery against the current state. A follow-up whose question has been answered since — the facet was demonstrated unaided, or the rival explanations resolved — no longer clears the stop and is retired with its reason shown ("No longer needed: you solved a problem like this on Tuesday · undo").
8. **Nothing fits → generate in the background** (restored, rows 5, 80, 92: "retrieval first; generate on demand… with the practice-family and task-blueprint machinery, freshness checks and source-grounded verification"). The summary records `no_item` for that question, the card says "No unfamiliar problem on this yet — LearnLoop is writing one", and RW6b queues one generation request through PF Phase 5a's source-anchored path, grounded in the session's spans and the question's facets (the same path the sources spec names for "when nothing fits"; if it builds a request API, RW6b calls that). Generation is CPU-slow batch work and never runs on the session's request path. A generated item is verified by PF-b before it exists; the next daily re-valuation (item 7) sees it as a candidate like any other. Money bounds it: `followups.generation_max_daily_cost_usd` ($1.00 metered); a capped request waits for the next day. Kill switch `followups.generate_when_nothing_fits` (off: `no_item` stands, as before the restoration).
9. **Back to where it was studied** (restored, RS9, row 83: "after the cold attempt, reopen the Reader at the most relevant passage or inquiry branch if repair is warranted"). Each follow-up's provenance carries the session's inquiry roots and anchor spans. When a follow-up is answered wrong, or with help, DL2's restoration after the measurement shows "Back to where you studied this: Mixture CDF → integration bounds", which opens the Reader at that inquiry branch or passage. It is a link, never an automatic jump; it opens the Reader in the returning posture at that place.
10. **How a served check looks** (DL2's surface, specified there; UX §4.9, wireframes W13 and W16; the requests are in §18). The plan line says *check · on your own · about 6 min · from your reading of §5.4 (why)*. A cold banner says *"A check on your own. Help is still here — using it makes this practice with help."* Help, ask and "show the book" stay live and are never blocked (U11, P3 §11): pressing help opens the ladder first, whose header says that taking any rung makes the attempt practice with help, and Enter on a rung takes it. That is the ladder's ordinary choose-then-take step, so a stray key reveals nothing and no confirmation dialog is added (W3; D42). During the check the header names the book and nothing that locates the exercise in it — no exercise number, page, section or link — and carries no *why* or purpose text: P3's invariant 10 withholds source cues and purpose text from a cold administration, and an exercise number is enough to find the exercise or its worked solution (D46; the UX wireframes W13 and W16 used to show an exercise number there, and W13 also "from your reading of §5.4" and *why*). After feedback the header gains the exercise number, page, *original page* link and *why this check*. The plan line before the check keeps its purpose and *why*, as this item says. On the phone, a check that is an ordinary item is served through the existing allowlist with the same banner; a probe-type check says "continue it on the desktop", as today.

**Labelled, with undo** (UX §4.8–4.9, S15; wireframes W12, W13).
- The card's "Coming up" shows every scheduled check as a dated timeline, never a list of counts: "Wed · a question that tells apart two readings of your step 4 · about 6 min · why · undo"; "Mon · a new mixture problem, on your own · new setup · about 8 min · why · undo". Learner-facing wording never says "error" or "mistake" about the learner's work (G16).
- "Why" shows the purpose and the valuation as LearnLoop's estimate ("expected to settle which repair to try · about 6 min · LearnLoop's estimate"), never as a verified fact (W2).
- **Undo takes an optional reason**, "why? (optional)" (W5). An undo says that, to this learner, the check was worth less than its minutes and burden, so it is the direct evidence about λ and the burden that the calibration track needs (§15). The reason is stored on the `learner_card` verdict that undo appends, never sent to learner-text gaps (it is about the check, not the subject).
- "Undo all" retires every follow-up from the session. Undo and re-valuation retire the commitment (`commitments.retire`).
- Retirements read as good news: "No longer needed: you solved one like it on Wednesday · undo".
- The relevance and exposure verdicts are the sources spec's `judgment_ledger` rows.

**The valuation receipt** is stored on the digest row (§9.4): the questions (with the formative records that raised them), every candidate with its kind (item, instrument, textbook exercise, generated), its novelty label and exposure verdicts and the discount they caused, its EIG, EVSI point, LCB and UCB, expected minutes, the **predicted outcome distribution** P(E) for each chosen check (the field the sources spec's S5 records for calibration, its §11.4 item 1), the verdict (`measure`, `stop`, `abstain`) and the stop reason, the chosen ids in order, and the module versions. Fields that R1 also logs for scheduler candidates use R1's names (`probe_eig`, `evsi_assignment`), so router R3's replay can read both; when the sources spec's receipt type (`ValuationReceipt`) exists, RW6b stores that type. Replay never re-values (W9).

**Why no successor.** The serving algorithm is unchanged: it serves commitments as DL2 defines them. RW6b adds content (commitments) that the learner can undo, behind a kill switch, which is the precedent learner-text gaps set for its live prerequisite creation and seed check. This is the first live use of `evsi.rank_feasible`, but it decides only which commitments to create, never what the scheduler serves or how evidence counts. The RW6b agent confirms this with the reviewer. If they rule otherwise (for example because uncapped checks change the maintain lane's composition), RW6b takes the next free `mvp-0.N` tag with plan parity under `followups.enabled = false`.

**The outcome** joins later in the report: cold success after assisted reader work, and whether each check told LearnLoop what its valuation predicted (§15).

### 9.4 Data

| Table | Phase | Role | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| — | RW6a | — | No table: the card and summary are pure reads |
| `reader_session_digests` | RW6b | WORKFLOW (the narrative is a paid output; never re-run on rebuild) | `id`, `visit_ids_json`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `summary_json` (typed, including each question's outcome or `no_item`), `narrative_md`, `cited_event_ids_json`, `followup_commitment_ids_json`, `valuation_json` (the receipt, §9.3), `model`, `prompt_version`, `dismissed_at`, `created_at` |

Follow-ups live in `commitments`; re-valuation retirements are commitment events with their reason. Nothing else is added.

### 9.5 Routes, spend, privacy

- **Routes:** `reader_digest` (chat; Codex by default, since it is background work). Relevance and exposure use the sources spec's routes (`practice_rerank`, `worked_example_equivalence`). The valuation makes no model call. Background generation uses PF's routes.
- **Caps (money only):** one narrative per session; `followups.max_daily_cost_usd` ($0.50 metered) for the relevance and exposure calls made for RW6b; `followups.generation_max_daily_cost_usd` ($1.00 metered) for generation. Nothing caps the number of follow-ups (§9.3).
- **Privacy:** the typed summary goes to the provider, the same class as the router card.

### 9.6 Successor

None (§9.3, with the stated fallback). Kill switches: `recap.enabled` (RW6a), `recap.wrap_up_enabled`, `digest.enabled`, `digest.narrative_enabled`, `followups.enabled` (off: no follow-up is created and no re-valuation runs; existing ones stay as the learner's commitments and can be undone), `followups.formative_questions_enabled`, `followups.generate_when_nothing_fits`. The exposure rule and the relevance step switch with the sources spec's valuation (`source_service.discriminating_search`); when RW6b built them first, they ship under that switch name.

## 10. RW7 — Goal-aware reading paths (restored)

Deferred in the first revision; restored by the owner's decision (§20.8) as two phases. The dialogue (round 1 D, after Matuschak's "routes of different depths through the canonical text"): the path should "explain why material is included, what can be skipped based on existing evidence, and whether it is a prerequisite or enrichment", and "let the learner override the recommendation". The first revision's two corrections stand: roles come from `role_hint` and RW4e's proposals plus the path judge, not a separate index; and changing DL1's reading block is a serving change.

### 10.1 RW7a: the path in the Guide (display only)

**Presentation:** UX §4.11 (the path card, role chips, "why", overrides); §2 moment 0; wireframe W1.

- **The goal it serves** (round 1 D: "connecting its recommendations to a specific capability the learner wants to acquire"). When an active goal's facet scope covers the source's LOs (the Guide's existing `goal_for_item`, `reader_guidance.py:116`), the path names it at the top ("toward your goal: derive mixture distributions on your own"); otherwise the path serves the depth alone and says so. RW7a creates no goals; goals stay where they are made today.
- **The depth is the learner's.** The Guide asks once per source, "How deep do you want to go?": **get the idea** (definitions, examples, visualizations), **learn to use it** (explanations, worked examples, practice), **fluency** (novel problems, changed assumptions, delayed checks). Remembered per source; changeable any time. When DL2's control lands, its intents map onto these (Master: fluency; Learn: learn to use it; Keep and Light touch: get the idea), and the Guide's choice and DL2's stay one setting.
- **What the learner sees.** Per section: **core**, **prerequisite**, **enrichment**, **already shown** or **assumed**, each with a one-line "why" ("needed for the mixture CDF you're working toward"; "you showed this on 2 Oct, unaided"), and "read it anyway" / "skip it". The "Orient" intention's "What matters here?" opens this view (RW1b).
- **One exercise worth trying** (row 34: "the guide identifies a relevant section and one representative exercise"). Each core section can name one exercise to try after it: an `exercise` block of that section, or, once the sources spec merges, its best textbook-exercise candidate for the section's LOs. "Try it" opens it in the workbench at once (§5.1), imported through the usual path, with the book open.
- **The suggested next activity** (round 1 D's mock-up: "Read the mixture distribution section, then derive the CDF without looking at the answer"). One line at the top of the path, composed without a model from the path itself: the next unfinished core or prerequisite section and, at the "learn to use it" and "fluency" depths, its exercise ("Next: read §5.4, then try 5.14 · about 15 min"). It is an offer; the learner's position and choices win.
- **How labels are decided.** One LLM call per source and depth (route `reading_path_judge`) over the section list, the sections' roles, the learner's status on the linked LOs, the depth and the goal. `already shown` stands only if every LO the section teaches is demonstrated (`lo_certification(...).demonstrated`), and `assumed` only with an open `assumption_decisions` row; otherwise the label falls back to `core`. Verdicts append `judgment_ledger` (task `reading_path_role`). A learner override appends a `learner_card` verdict and is kept; "skip it" marks nothing as known (W1).
- **Display only.** RW7a changes no plan, no evidence and nothing served. Re-judged when the depth changes or an LO's status changes (cache key: depth, section list hash, LO statuses).
- **Data:** `reader_path_sections` (WORKFLOW: paid outputs; never re-run on rebuild): `id`, `extraction_id`, `depth`, `section_id`, `label`, `reason`, `exercise_span_id`, `learner_override`, `ledger_id`, `state_key`, `model`, `prompt_version`, `created_at`.
- **Successor:** none. Kill switch `reading_paths.enabled` (off: today's Guide).

### 10.2 RW7b: the plan follows the path (algorithm successor)

- **What changes.** DL1's reading block (§6.10 there) opens the next unfinished **core** or **prerequisite** section of the source's path instead of the next section in book order; enrichment is never planned; a learner's "read it anyway" makes a section core for planning.
- **Why a successor.** This changes what is served (F3). RW7b takes the next free `mvp-0.N` at its merge, with receipts recording the section DL1's order would have chosen.
- **Kill switch** `reading_paths.plan_uses_path` (off: DL1's order, byte-identical).
- **Depends on** RW7a and DL1 (merged); not on DL2.
- **Gates** (the first draft's G28–G29, renumbered G47): honest path labels; `reading_paths.plan_uses_path = false` leaves DL1's reading blocks byte-identical, with a rebuild showing zero projection deltas.
- **As built (2026-10-25, `docs/reviews/reader-workbench-RW7b.md`).** Tag `mvp-0.32` after RW2d's `mvp-0.31` (one constant, `READING_PATH_PLAN_ALGORITHM_VERSION`; renumbered at landing if another successor lands first); no migration. The path narrows the predecessor's own order (U4's "continue from the furthest section" or DL1's book order, with the exam filter) on each reading that has a judged path at the learner's depth; the labels are the Guide's (`reading_paths.plan_path`, the same `effective_label` rule) from a judgment for the learner's current state, honest labels re-applied when the plan is built. A reading with no depth, nothing judged or only a stale judgment (an older state; recorded) keeps the predecessor's order; a withheld (exam) reading plans nothing through its path, and a withheld section is never the path's step even in DL1's exact selection. The plan never calls the judge and reads the path once, when it is built. The receipt is `split_json["reading_path"]` (the predecessor's section, who chose, each path consulted), written only when a path was in play, so a vault without paths plans byte for byte as the predecessor. The block's why names the path's choice (UX §4.11). The switch acts only with the Guide's `reading_paths.enabled` on too (the plan never follows a path the learner cannot see). Turned off after a build, it puts the predecessor's reading back while the plan's reading is unstarted (an unstarted path block ended unread, minutes recomputed, the transition on the receipt). The receipt names the input and judged state keys and the judge and override rows read. §15's row "misses on LOs whose core section was skipped" is `learnloop reader report`'s `reading_paths.skipped_core` (Codex review fixes, `docs/reviews/reader-rw7b-codex-review.md`).

## 11. Persistence summary

**Migrations.** One per phase that needs one, numbered when it lands (`ls migrations/`; head 180 on `main` at `f2f02e32`, with R1, facet identity and learner-text gaps claiming numbers first in the merge chain, and the sources spec's track from 184). Follow `CLAUDE.md`'s head-fixture procedure. Every CHECK'd column is created with all the values any phase here will write, so no later phase rebuilds a table this spec created.

| Table | Role (`db/table_roles.py`) | Phase |
|---|---|---|
| `reader_authored_questions` (existing): `ADD COLUMN scope_kind`, `question_style` (five values), `anchor_span_ids_json`, `inquiry_root_event_id`, `self_check` | unchanged | RW1a |
| `learner_text_gaps`, `learner_text_gap_jobs` (existing, from learner-text gaps): widen the `source_kind` CHECK with `workbench_text` and `reader_formative` (rebuild, FK-safe; §3.4 item 13) | unchanged | The first adapter phase (RW1b or RW2b) |
| `workbench_sessions` (with `stem_md`, `import_status`, `entry`, `parent_session_id`; `practice_item_id` nullable) | WORKFLOW | RW2a |
| `workbench_steps` | RAW_LEDGER | RW2a |
| `help_assists`, shared with the adaptive-hints spec (with RW2c's kinds and columns and both specs' CHECK values; §5.7) | RAW_LEDGER | RW2b, or that spec's H0, whichever lands first |
| `passage_links` | WORKFLOW | RW3b |
| `reader_cue_impressions` (with `suppressed_reason`) | RECEIPT | RW3b |
| `library_asks` (with `comparison_id` and RW4d's `kept_json`) | WORKFLOW | RW4a |
| `interactive_figures` (with RW5b's `origin` values and `supersedes_id`) | WORKFLOW | RW5a |
| `figure_predictions` | RAW_LEDGER | RW5a |
| `reader_session_digests` | WORKFLOW | RW6b |
| `reader_path_sections` | WORKFLOW | RW7a |

RW1b (unless it carries the widening migration), RW1c, RW2c, RW3a, RW4b, RW4d, RW4e, RW5b, RW6a and RW7b need no migration; RW2d, as built, adds one RECEIPT table (`field_signal_receipts`, migration 219, §5.11). **Tables the adaptive-hints spec owns are not counted here** (`approach_maps`, its H1; `help_assists` is counted above because RW2b may create it), **nor those the sources spec owns** (`source_comparisons`, `source_exercise_imports`, `source_tool_calls`, the intervention records, the proposal columns): a Reader phase that lands first and builds a shared piece once (§7.2, §7.3, §9.3) carries that spec's migration for it, numbered when it lands. RW4d and RW4e write proposals into the existing `source_objects`, `source_object_versions`, `source_object_citations` and `source_object_relations` tables (P3 §7). **No DERIVED table, so no new replayer:** RW2c's help outcomes and RW6a's formative records are pure reads. Replay never reads these tables; the evidence they lead to lives in `practice_attempts` (`hints_used`, `primed`) and `reveal_events`, which replay already reads. **One table rebuild, and only of the learner-text tables:** no new `interaction_events` kind and no new reveal kind.

**Events.** Existing reader kinds with a payload `event` field (§4.3): RW1 adds `intent_routed`, `intent_corrected`, `reconnect_answered`, `next_step_requested`, `another_way_opened`, `reconstruct_scheduled`, `tutor_offer_pressed`, `detour_opened`, `detour_returned`; RW2 adds `workbench_opened`, `workbench_resumed`, `workbench_left`, `workbench_closed`, `workbench_reopened`, `workbench_bound`, `book_visibility_changed`; RW3 `cue_shown` (RW3a only; RW3b's impressions table is authoritative), `cue_*` taps, `cue_density_changed`; RW4 `library_ask`, `compare_opened`, `comparison_kept`, `comparison_removed`, `my_take_added`, `exercise_chosen`; RW5 `figure_opened`, `figure_animated`, `figure_region_selected`, `figure_revision_requested`; RW6 `recap_shown`, `wrap_up_offered`, `wrap_up_opened`, `followup_undone`, `back_to_study_opened`; RW7 `path_depth_set`, `path_override`. The salience-firewall test gains each.

**Config** (modelled defaults, out of the decision-only template unless it has room). Each phase recomputes the latest `DEFAULTS_SNAPSHOT_BY_ALGORITHM` entry with a "no scoring, scheduling, projection or serving change" note (§3.4 item 9); RW2d and RW7b record their successor instead:
- `[reader_inquiry]`: `enabled`. `[reader_intent]`: `enabled`, `speculative_enabled` (true), `speculate_after_ms` (600, a latency setting), `timeout_seconds` (4), `max_calls_per_day` (200). `[reader_active_reading]`: `enabled`. `[reader_formative]`: `enabled`. `[reader_tutor]`: `retrieval_enabled`, `offers_enabled`.
- `[workbench]`: `enabled`, `help_enabled`, `symbolic_checks_enabled`, `discriminate_enabled`, `error_kind_enabled`, `help_outcomes_enabled`, `examples_enabled`, `compare_methods_enabled`, `passage_draw_enabled`, `field_signals_enabled` (RW2d, a successor's switch), `open_session_days` (30), `max_calls_per_session` (24, a money cap counted in calls, on the workbench's own calls), `max_daily_cost_usd` (1.00), `price_per_million`. The hint service's `[hints]` and `[hints.maps]` (adaptive-hints §9: `chooser_enabled`, `choice_policy`, `chooser_timeout_seconds`, the money caps, `maps.enabled`, `maps.build_on_serve`) govern the chooser, its policy, the maps and hint spend on every surface, the workbench included; they are that spec's keys, not added here.
- `[cues]`: `enabled`, `discovery_enabled`, `try_first_enabled`, `value_order_enabled`, `one_per_viewport` (true, a display limit), `density` (`fewer` \| `normal` \| `more`, i.e. 1, 2 or 4 per section; default `normal`; "off" in the UI is `enabled = false` for the vault or a source), `holdout_rate` (0.1), `max_calls_per_source_open` (12), `max_daily_cost_usd` (0.50).
- `[library_ask]`: `enabled`, `expand_graph` (true), `personalized_default` (true), `max_daily_cost_usd` (1.00). `[reader_compare]`: `enabled` (the Reader's compare UI), `keep_enabled`. The comparison's own bounds are the sources spec's (`source_service.compare_max_prompt_tokens`, `compare_max_cost_usd`). `[library_exercises]`: `enabled`, `fallback_enabled`. `[roles]`: `on_demand_enabled`, `positive_filter_enabled`, `batch_max_cost_usd` (5.00, per batch run).
- `[figures]`: `enabled`, `predictions_enabled`, `region_enabled`, `revisions_enabled`, `advanced_panels_enabled`, `offers_enabled`, `max_daily_cost_usd` (1.00).
- `[recap]`: `enabled`, `wrap_up_enabled`. `[digest]`: `enabled`, `narrative_enabled`. `[followups]`: `enabled`, `formative_questions_enabled`, `generate_when_nothing_fits`, `fallback_days` (3, a dating default without FSRS state), `max_wait_days` (14, a time cap), `max_daily_cost_usd` (0.50), `generation_max_daily_cost_usd` (1.00). No count cap (§9.3, §20.7).
- `[reading_paths]`: `enabled`, `plan_uses_path` (RW7b, a successor's switch).

**Routes** (each its own `ai.routing.*`, added to the `AITask` literal, `ROUTE_FOR_OPERATION` and the config schema):
- **decider tasks** (added to `DECIDER_TASKS`; never a chat fallback, never overridden by `LEARNLOOP_AI_PROVIDER`): `reader_intent`; and the adaptive-hints spec's `hint_chooser`, shared (the first draft's `workbench_level_chooser` is withdrawn);
- **listwise tasks** (through `judge_listwise`): `passage_cue_judge`, `library_organizer`, `role_labeller` (batch only);
- **chat tasks:** `workbench_check`, `figure_author`, `figure_verifier` *(not in RW5a; RW5c)*, `reader_digest`, `reading_path_judge`; and the adaptive-hints spec's `hint_writer`, shared (the first draft's `workbench_hint` is withdrawn), with its background `approach_map` and `hint_outcome`.

Unset chat routes fall back to the active provider, and receipts record the resolved model. RW1c uses the tutor's existing route; background generation uses PF's. **The sources spec's routes are its own** (`practice_rerank`, `source_rerank`, `source_compare`, `exercise_discrimination`, `worked_example_equivalence`, `source_solution_match`): RW1c, RW2c, RW4a, RW4d and RW6b call them through its functions and add none. The first revision's `followup_relevance` and `example_fit` routes are withdrawn (§20.9).

**`judgment_ledger` tasks:** `reader_intent`, `passage_cue`, `library_contribution`, `source_role`, `figure_verified`, `reading_path_role`, plus `learner_card` rows on the same tasks for corrections, taps, feedback, overrides and undos (an undo of a scheduled check is a `learner_card` row on the sources spec's valuation task, with the learner's optional reason). The sources spec's tasks (`source_intent_fit`, `source_tool_request` and its relevance and exposure tasks) are its own. **The hint service's tasks are shared** (adaptive-hints §9): `hint_choice` (judge `decider`), `hint_fit` (the "doesn't connect" replies, judge `learner_card`; the first draft's `workbench_hint_fit`) and `approach_map_valid` (judge `verifier`); a verdict's subject is the `help_assists` row, which carries the surface. The ledger's judge CHECK (migration 175) already admits `llm`, `verifier`, `decider` and `learner_card`; `task` is free text. No change.

**Kill-switch parity is a gate** for every row: switch off, the RPCs it touches return byte-identical results on fixture copies.

The fix to `_learner_signal` that this spec first gave RW3a has merged on `main` as `fix/guide-overlay` (`f2f02e32`), a display-only change with no successor; its tests hold G30.

## 13. Rollout

Every phase goes through `implementation_goal.md` §2's loop: implement, adversarial review (Codex, or Opus 5.5 when Codex is out), fix, gate, integrate. Phases merge one at a time. Keep at most two Reader phases open at once; all of them touch the Reader's frontend. The Reader workbench is not part of the goal's definition of done (`implementation_goal.md` §1), so it yields the merge queue and Codex capacity to the goal's tracks (DL2, DL3, router R2) when they compete.
