**Size key:** S ≈ one agent session plus review (about a day); M ≈ two to four days of implement, review and fix; L ≈ one to two weeks. *Italics* in "Builds" mark what the restoration added; **[UX]** and **[re-review]** mark what the UX integration and the brainstorm re-review added (§20.9); **[hints]** marks what the adaptive-hints fold-in added (§20.11).

**Recommended order** (dependencies in the table):
1. **RW1a** first (it ports finished work and splits the rail).
2. **RW3a** and **RW6a** (small, immediate learner value) alongside **RW2a**.
3. **RW1b** and **RW4a**.
4. **RW2b** once the ChatGPT-plan sign-in and learner-text gaps are in. RW2 is the core differentiator and the owner's own pain point; RW2a delivers its non-AI half early, and the discriminating question is RW2b's most valuable addition.
5. **RW1c** and **RW4b**, which gain the sources spec's backend as it merges; then **RW3b** and **RW4d**.
6. **RW5a**, **RW4e** and **RW7a** (independent of each other; two at a time).
7. **RW2c**, **RW5b** and **RW2d**; then **RW7b**.
8. **RW6b** whenever DL2 lands (it may move earlier in this list: it depends on DL2, RW2a and `main`, not on phases 3–7).

The sources spec's track runs in parallel throughout: it does not touch the Reader's frontend, so it does not count toward the two-open-phases limit.

---

## 14. Gates and tests

| # | Gate |
|---|---|
| G1 | **Branch port parity.** The branch's 8 backend and 9 frontend tests pass on `main`. A follow-up's prompt holds only its ancestors. T6's retrieval for a follow-up uses the follow-up's own question. Legacy flat histories read as roots |
| G2 | **Inquiry status is not evidence.** Resolving, reopening, "revisit once" and reconnect answers write no attempt, observation, mastery or FSRS row. The salience firewall covers every new payload `event` |
| G3 | **Intent routing.** A routed action has exactly the effect of the same palette action (the same RPC and payload). An empty Enter makes no model call. An unavailable, timed-out or spent-out Decider routes to Ask. "change" records a `learner_card` verdict. With `reader_intent.enabled = false`, the selection UI is today's |
| G4 | **Scoped questions are formative and keyed.** Reconnect, next-step, why-step, assumption and reconstruct questions write no attempt (a reconstruct item is an ordinary practice item only after escalation, and its commitment is an ordinary `test_me_later`). A passage-scoped row never shadows its section's quick check (lookup filtered by `scope_kind`). Reuse keys hold. Section quick checks are byte-identical |
| G5 | **Assistance mapping.** For the source visible or hidden, each help level (0–5), each hint kind at its level (§5.2) and combination, the submitted attempt's `hints_used`, `primed` and reveal rows match §5.4, and `resolve_attempt_activity_policy` gives the stated FSRS and certification eligibility (any source visibility, check or hint earns zero certification credit in both projections). The workbench's count comes from the server's fold over its `help_assists` rows, never the client. A rebuild reproduces evidence identically. An abandoned session writes no attempt |
| G6 | **The grader stays blind.** The rendered blind-grading prompt for a workbench attempt contains the composed answer only: no step history, assist text, hint, feedback or learner state |
| G7 | **Reconciliation contract.** A fake hint writer that cites no learner step, continues from the first questionable step or a step that depends on it (or, without a graph, from any step at or after it), or is shown under a step it does not quote, or repeats its kind and strategy after a "doesn't connect" reply (other than "the notation"), or, with a map, names no route or target node or misses the chosen horizon (rule 7), or breaks its kind's contract (rule 8: a waypoint naming a transformation, a plan label carrying a later node's expression, a discriminating question whose rivals predict the same answer, a worked chunk leaving no gap) is caught, re-asked once, then downgraded as §5.2 says |
| G8 | **Leak control.** A level-1–3 hint containing the expected answer (including short numeric answers) is never shown, and no waypoint or subgoal label carries the state of a map node marked `leak`. Bridging never returns the item's exercise, solution or linked blocks, and never delays the hint |
| G9 | **Symbolic labels.** "checked" appears only when the sandbox ran the check and its negative control behaved. Without bubblewrap every verdict reads "model's reading". Only declared kinds (§5.3) are attempted; a numerically checked inequality is labelled "checked numerically" |
| G10 | **Source visibility.** A Reader workbench attempt with the source visible goes through `submit_attempt`, is graded and diagnosed, and earns zero certification credit in both projections. "Hide the source" makes an ordinary attempt. Opening the source afterwards records the exposure and the hint-equivalent |
| G11 | **Workbench kill switch.** With `workbench.enabled = false`, practice RPCs and screens are byte-identical to `main` on fixture copies |
| G12 | **Spend.** Per-session and daily caps hold. A capped session keeps authored hints. Codex receipts are `estimated_only`. The chooser's time box falls back to rung 1 and the §5.5 progression. Hint calls count against the hint service's money caps (`hints.max_daily_cost_usd`), never against a count of hints |
| G13 | **Cues are display only.** With cues on or off, evidence, mastery, projections, FSRS and the plan are byte-identical. "Check me" produces an ordinary learner-pick attempt |
| G14 | **Restraint and holdout.** No section shows more cues than the learner's density budget. No cue in skim mode. Guide cues are never held out. For discovered cues the logged propensity equals the draw probability, and seeds reproduce draws |
| G15 | **Judges decide.** With the cue judge stubbed to `none`, no discovered cue appears, whatever retrieval ranks (R1). Every discovered cue has a ledger verdict and a verified quote |
| G16 | **Wording.** Cue copy comes from fixed templates. A lint test over the copy of every new learner-facing surface rejects trait words ("weak", "you don't understand", "you struggle", "you always") and, about the learner's work, "wrong", "error" and "mistake" (UX U6). Headings carry no counts of open or unfinished things and never say "overdue" (UX U12) |
| G17 | **Sources first.** A library ask returns passages and organizer lines, never a tutor essay, until the learner asks the tutor. Each organizer line cites exactly one returned hit. The tutor's citations stay inside the pinned hits |
| G18 | **Compare keeps sources apart.** The Reader calls the sources spec's `compare_sources` and shows only lines that passed its quote verification (its G12); the table has no consensus row or heading, and each cell names its source. Without "Keep", the Reader writes nothing to `source_objects` or relations (table diff); with it, only RW4d's proposals (G43). No concept edge, facet merge, prerequisite or LO change is ever written |
| G19 | **Retrieval policies.** Every Reader call to `find_sources` gets its policy from the caller context, never from an argument (the sources spec's G2): the Reader context gives `reading`, a workbench session `assisted_practice` for its item, a cold attempt `cold_attempt` and an empty result. With the sources spec faked, the Reader's calls and contexts are asserted; with its build present, `assisted_practice` equals `TutorSpanProvider`'s scope and filters for the same item. No Reader surface ever receives `authoring` material. Policies and the expansion calls are recorded on every manifest. An RW4e role proposal never changes what an item-bound scope excludes |
| G20 | **RW4 kill switches** restore T6's library search, tutor spans and Quick Add behaviour byte-identically |
| G21 | **No generated code runs.** The grammar parser rejects everything outside the grammar (fuzzed). The renderer has no `eval` or `Function`, enforced by a lint rule |
| G22 | **Two evaluators agree** on the golden corpus within 1e-9 relative error (1e-6 for `integral`), and the corpus covers every grammar construct and panel kind |
| G23 | **Labels follow verification.** "matches the source's equation" appears only after step 3 passed. *(Holds vacuously in RW5a: step 3 and the label are deferred to RW5c, §8.3.)* A figure failing steps 1 or 2 is never shown. Unsupported requests are recorded with their kind |
| G24 | **Predictions are formative.** They write no attempt, no evidence observation and no field signal; a missed one queues a `reader_formative` learner text (G36) |
| G25 | **Recap, digest and card.** The typed summary is deterministic from the ledgers (computed twice, identical). The narrative cites only ids in the summary. `ReaderActivityV1` contains no cold-answer or reserved-surface material (router G5) |
| G26 | **Follow-ups are unfamiliar and valued, not counted.** Never an attempted item; never an item in a family whose solution the session revealed; never a candidate the exposure rule excludes from `retention` or `discriminate`; `no_item` when nothing fits, with a background generation request (never served unverified, never on the request path). With a fake valuation where every candidate's LCB is below its minutes, none is created; where several clear it, all of them are created in rank order, with no count cap, and conditional valuation drops a second check that duplicates the first. Every created follow-up has a receipt whose chosen value cleared the stop rule. Re-valuation retires a follow-up whose facet was since demonstrated unaided, with its reason |
| G27 | **Follow-ups are ordinary "Check once" commitments.** Served by DL2's dated branch on `due_hint`, cold, with restoration after; discriminating checks dated for the next day's plan, retention checks at effective due, none later than `followups.max_wait_days`. A day with more due follow-ups than its maintain minutes serves what fits and leaves the rest as "later" (plan minutes never exceeded). Undo and "undo all" retire the commitments. With `followups.enabled = false` no commitment is created and plan builds equal DL2's |
| G35 | **Reuse, not a new chooser.** The valuation calls `evsi.robust_evsi` / `evsi.rank_feasible`, the probe-EIG functions and `familiarity_discount` (asserted by monkeypatching them in the test); RW6b defines no EIG, EVSI or stop rule of its own. The valuation makes no model call; replay never re-values |
| G30 | **The Guide reads the overlay.** On a split fixture with a live diagnosis, the Guide's passage reason names the diagnoser's statement; on a joint-graded fixture it is unchanged. **Held on `main`** by `fix/guide-overlay`'s tests (`tests/test_reader_guidance.py`, `tests/test_active_errors_overlay.py`); RW3a adds a margin-mark case |
| G31 | **The recap is a pure read.** Opening a source with the recap on or off writes nothing but the `recap_shown` event |
| G32 | **Resume.** Steps survive navigation, sidecar restart and app restart (flush-on-leave tested); "Leave for now" keeps the session open; "Abandon" closes it; a resumed session shows its full history |
| G33 | **Hint feedback.** A "doesn't connect" reply reaches the next hint's request at once and appends a `learner_card` verdict (task `hint_fit`). The next hint stays at the same level and follows its shortcut (§5.2): "that's not where I'm stuck" moves `about_step`; "too big a jump" gives a shorter horizon toward the same target or a finer kind; "I'm using a different approach" stays on the learner's route (an existing route or `new_route`) and names the step it builds on; "the notation" restates the same idea quoting the learner's symbols. Except after "the notation", its kind or strategy differs from the earlier ones on that step |
| G34 | **Exercises from sources.** Before the sources spec merges: fallback candidates are `exercise` blocks from the teaching scope (never worked examples, whose solutions sit beside them), and choosing one runs `import_exercises` unchanged. After: candidates are the sources spec's, and "use this one" calls its import path. Either way nothing is imported without the learner's pick |
| | *Added by the restoration (§20.8):* |
| G36 | **W1's tiers hold.** For every tier-2 path (`reader_formative`, `workbench_text`, check verdicts, hints taken, discriminating answers, predictions), a table diff shows no write to `grading_evidence`, `facet_recall_state`, `facet_capability_evidence`, certification, coldness, FSRS state, `probe_observations` or any mastery projection; the only writes are learner-text gap rows and their jobs, workflow rows and events. The salience firewall covers each new `event` and source kind. The widening migration preserves every existing learner-text row and passes `foreign_key_check` (durability lane) |
| G37 | **Ask before repairing.** With two or more rivals open on a questionable step, rung 1 is a `discriminating` question and level 1's only option in the chooser's request; with one, rung 1 is an ordinary question and `discriminating` is not offered. Which rung is suggested is the sampled draw (G63), never a fixed rule; a fake Decider that puts its probability on rung 1 gets it suggested, and one that puts it elsewhere leaves it takeable on rung 1. A taken rung 1 whose candidates all have zero EIG serves an approach question and says why. Candidates are ranked by `instrument_expected_information_gain` (asserted by monkeypatch); a candidate whose rivals all predict the same answer is never offered. The question passes leak control and states neither rival. Its answer reaches the diagnoser trace and the next hint's context, and writes no posterior or probe observation |
| G38 | **The step graph and the guess.** A questionable step marks only its dependents as not standing; `continue_from` may be a later independent valid step. The error-kind guess is labelled "the model's guess", appears in the diagnoser trace, and is never written as a diagnosis or error event. Each declared check kind has a negative-control test |
| G39 | **Help outcomes are a replayable read; draws are honest.** `help_outcomes` is deterministic (computed twice, identical) and reads only existing rows. A passage draw happens only between options that all passed the judge; the logged propensity equals the draw probability, seeds reproduce draws, and the other options are one click away |
| G40 | **Examples and methods respect scopes.** The worked-example search runs under the workbench session's caller context, so it never returns the item's exercise, solution or linked blocks or a span reproducing its answer; before the sources spec's S3 it is absent, not approximated. "Compare methods" appears only after a correct Submit, reads the reference by id (the item's key; a book solution only when the key is labelled `book_solution`), retrieves nothing, and records a reveal before showing a source solution |
| G41 | **RW2d is a proper successor.** With `workbench.field_signals_enabled = false`, the field derivation, Phase 6 serving and plan builds are byte-identical to the predecessor on fixture copies; on, the receipts record what the old derivation would have produced. A stuck step or early help never lowers the record (F2) |
| G42 | **Cue value and offers.** Cues are ordered by the stored `p_open`, then the judge's rank, Guide first on ties; the order uses no hand-set weight. The density control changes only how many show. A try-first cue appears only on a worked example or derivation with an unchecked claim or open hypothesis, and pressing it opens RW1b's question as a learner pick. Contrast and correction-span lists appear in the manifest |
| G43 | **Kept comparisons are proposals.** "Keep" writes `source_objects` (claim or definition) and `source_object_relations` rows with `status` / `review_status = 'proposed'` and the right authorship; "my take" writes a `learner_connects` relation with `authorship = 'learner'`. No keep, no write. No concept edge, facet merge, prerequisite or LO change. The tutor's `compare` offer runs only when the learner presses it |
| G44 | **Roles add, never exclude.** Role proposals carry confidence, judge, ledger id, revision and citation. A span with an AI role but an ingest `exercise` or `solution` hint stays excluded from item-bound scopes, and an item's linked blocks stay excluded whatever their proposed role. `roles=…` filters match ingest or proposed roles. The batch labeller honours `--dry-run` and its spend cap |
| G45 | **Flagship figures.** The two-sided exponential golden case (f(x) = p·λe^{λx} for x < 0 and (1−p)·λe^{−λx} for x ≥ 0, so P(X ≤ 0) = p: 30% at p = 0.30, as the dialogue's illustration shows, and unchanged by λ) renders shaded areas whose readouts match Python's integration within 1e-6; the same density with an extra ½ in each component (total mass ½, the UX document's first W11) is a negative control that `integrates_to_one` rejects, so the figure is not shown; PMF bars and `sum` readouts satisfy `sums_to_one`; animation calls the same evaluator as the slider; a region selection sends only its crop. A missed prediction stores its explanation text |
| G46 | **More panels and offers.** Heat map, vector field, trajectory and iterated-map panels agree across evaluators on the corpus (RK4 identical step for step). An `explorable` offer appears only when the judge said `yes`, and building runs RW5a's verification unchanged |
| G47 | **Reading paths.** `already shown` only when every LO the section teaches is demonstrated; `assumed` only with an assumption row; otherwise `core`. With RW7a on and RW7b off, plan builds are byte-identical (display only). With `reading_paths.plan_uses_path = false`, DL1's reading blocks are byte-identical and a rebuild shows zero projection deltas; on, receipts record DL1's choice |
| G48 | **The tutor looks things up within policy.** In the Reader context the tutor's retrieval uses `reading`, `assisted_practice` inside a workbench, nothing during a cold attempt. "Another way" never repeats a passage already shown in the inquiry. A "From your books" excerpt whose text is not in its passage is dropped; when verification keeps no passage, the answer opens with the abstention line and claims nothing from the books. The sources spec's requests and the two Reader buttons render as buttons; a comparison runs only when pressed; the tutor never serves, schedules or imports |
| G49 | **Follow-up restorations.** A candidate the exposure rule judged `equivalent` to a studied worked example is never created as a check on your own; a `same_surface` candidate is never labelled transfer, and "a new application" appears only on the sources spec's `new_application` label; formative records add questions but never change a prior's value; a generation request is queued at most once per `no_item` question and day, respects its spend cap, and its item is valued only after PF-b verification; "back to where you studied this" opens the provenance's inquiry or passage and never jumps on its own |
| G50 | **Formative records and Wrap up are pure.** `ReaderObservationV1` records are deterministic from the ledgers, carry `authority: "routing_only"`, and are read by nothing that writes evidence (import-linter contract). Wrap up never blocks closing a source and writes only what the same chips write elsewhere |
| G51 | **Intentions, new actions, every source kind.** Grouping by intention changes no action's RPC or payload. "Another way" excludes the open passage. "Reconstruct this later" creates exactly one escalated item and one `test_me_later` commitment, both undoable. Selection, intent box, inquiry and recap work on a transcript fixture; the notes pane offers only anchor-free actions |
| G52 | **The friction audit exists.** RW1a's review file holds the six-workflow audit before its UI changes, and each later phase's review file repeats it for the workflows it touched |
| G53 | **Sources-spec consumption degrades cleanly.** Each consuming phase (RW1c, RW2b's "a short problem instead", RW2c's worked example, RW3's "Check me", RW4a/b/d/e, RW6b) passes its gates with the sources spec absent and with a fake of its interfaces present (the names at `ae82eb08`: `find_sources`, `compare_sources`, `value_candidates`, `PracticeConstraints.intent`, `tool_requests`). The Reader never re-implements its candidate kinds, intents, ranking, exposure rule or comparison; a shared piece a Reader phase builds first lives in that spec's module with that spec's signature, and an import-linter contract keeps the Reader's packages from defining a second one |
| | *Added by the UX integration (§20.9):* |
| G54 | **Attention.** In the reading and working postures no LearnLoop-started element moves focus, opens a modal or a toast, or animates: vitest asserts that `document.activeElement` is unchanged when a selection is made and when a model result lands; a CSS lint finds no `animation` or `transition` on Reader components outside a figure's play; the quick-check marker is static. Skim mode renders no LearnLoop-started mark, reconnect line or offer |
| G55 | **Voices and contrast.** Every component that renders model text renders it through the model-voice component, with its label and screen-reader prefix (a lint over Reader components); source excerpts and learner text use theirs. Meaning-bearing labels ("model's reading", "counts as one hint", "not checked", "model's guess") use `--dim`, never `--faint` (a lint over the label components), and every new token pairing is checked in the five palettes |
| G56 | **Model-action states.** Each model-backed action renders pending, landed, failed (input kept), model unreachable, sidecar down, capped (naming the cap and what still works) and switched off (absent, no disabled control), each tested with a fake; "usually about N s" appears only when the report has a measured p50 for the route |
| G57 | **Return and keys.** Every detour pushes a return-stack entry and `Alt+←` restores the exact passage and selection; a restart restores only the root. Selecting text never changes the rail tab while `reader_intent.enabled`; the intent box never takes focus until a printable key. Every new learner action has the key the UX key map gives it, and the friction audit records keyboard reachability |
| G58 | **The workbench opens at once.** On a selected problem the stem and the first step save before the import binds; Check, Help and Submit wait for the bound item and say so; a failed import keeps the steps and writes no attempt; an abandoned session reopens to `open`; the default book visibility follows `entry`; back from feedback on a Reader workbench attempt opens that problem, and every other attempt goes back as today |
| | *Added by the spec–UX consistency review (§20.10):* |
| G59 | **Offers fetch early and show on press.** In the Reader, a tutor answer's practice and passage lookups start when the answer lands and render nothing until pressed; `record_proposal_impressions` and span exposures are written only on the press; `compare_sources` makes no call until pressed; a lookup with no result renders its plain line, and a failed one runs again on the press (D44) |
| G60 | **A hint sits where it is about and builds on what stands.** `about_step_id` names an existing step the hint quotes and is where it renders; `continue_from_step_id` is never the first questionable step or a step depending on it; the label names both; "that's not where I'm stuck" moves `about_step` to the picked step (D45) |
| G61 | **A cold header locates nothing.** During a cold administration the item header renders the book's name and no exercise number, page, section, link or *why*; after submission they appear (D46; asserted where DL2 renders the header, with RW6b's served checks as its source-exercise case) |
| G62 | **Labels match the record.** The workbench footer and the submit line count every assist that `hints_used` counts, a check included; each box's *where this goes* text matches where that box's text is routed (a table-driven test over the boxes against the learner-text source kinds); the chooser's fallback offers one level higher on each further Help press on the same step |
| | *Added by the adaptive-hints fold-in (§20.11):* |
| G63 | **Kinds, horizons and the draw.** Every served hint has a kind of its level (§5.2's table) and its plain-words label; no waypoint or plan is offered without a map; a waypoint's target lies `horizon` edges ahead, on the learner's route, of the node `continue_from` reaches (or is the last node short of the goal); after a waypoint, Help on that step offers `next_step` toward the same target, and "a smaller step toward this goal" serves it. The logged propensity equals the probability the option was drawn with — the joint probability for the suggested rung, the probability conditional on the level for a rung the learner chose — seeds reproduce draws, `argmax` and fallback log 1, and "show me the book's way" and "a smaller step toward this goal" log `learner_pick` with none. A hint after a "doesn't connect" reply has the level of the hint it answers. No hint is shown unless the learner opened Help or replied to a hint |
| G64 | **One hint service.** The workbench gets every hint, its kind and the suggestion from `learnloop.hints` (`request_hint`, asserted by monkeypatch); an import-linter contract keeps the Reader's packages from defining a hint writer, chooser or map builder; its rows are `help_assists` rows with `surface = 'workbench'`, and its hint calls use `hint_writer` and `hint_chooser` only. The server's fold at Submit follows §5.4 (a check and each level-1–3 hint once, levels 4–5 as `primed`), with no double count and no lost hint (adaptive-hints G-H3). If RW2b lands before that spec's H2, the core it builds lives in `learnloop/hints/` with that spec's signatures, tests and gates G-H5 to G-H8 |

**Every phase also holds:** replay and rebuild make zero model and embedding calls with its tables populated; every new RPC is pinned desktop-only in `tests/test_mobile_allowlist.py`; every new table is classified in `db/table_roles.py`; the defaults snapshot is recomputed with its note.

**Every phase runs** `uv run lint-imports --no-cache`, `uv run pytest -n auto`, `uv run pytest -m durability`, `npm run typecheck && npm test` (the frontend changes in every phase), and the DB reference generate and validate scripts when the schema changed. **B3:** while the Rust toolchain on carson is broken, `cargo check` fails and the desktop shell can't be rebuilt; phases are verified with vitest and the sidecar tests, and the owner's in-app check waits for the toolchain fix.

---

## 15. Evaluation: learning, not engagement

The dialogue's warning applies: a better Reader may produce *fewer* interactions, because the learner gets unstuck sooner. So `learnloop reader report` reports outcomes and completed workflows first. As everywhere at n=1, these are **reports with noise floors, not thresholds** (R6).

| Block | Measures | Phase |
|---|---|---|
| Funnel | Selection → action → usable result → back to reading, with denominators: shown or available, requested, completed, failed (reason), cancelled; latency p50/p95 with model wait separated from learner and idle time; per route, model and prompt version | RW1a, extended by every phase |
| Experience (UX §9.2) | **Return rate** (detours that end back at the origin passage within the visit) and **detour cost**; **proactive load** (LearnLoop-started elements per reading hour against the density setting); **waiting behaviour** (the share of model waits during which the learner kept reading, selecting or typing); time from opening the workbench to the first step (seconds, after UX S2); the share of `●` against `◌` cues | RW1a, RW2a, RW3 |
| Inquiry | Inquiries opened, branch depth, resolved, dismissed, reopened, open after 7 days; reconnect answers; intent-box vs palette use and routing corrections (RW1b); next-step attempts and self-check results | RW1a/b |
| Workbench | Sessions opened, resumed, submitted, abandoned, left open; help level taken vs offered, by facet over time (**hint dependence**); recovery: the share of checks followed by a valid next step without further help; time stuck (questionable verdict to next valid step); **unusable hints** ("doesn't connect" by shortcut); check coverage by transformation kind; grade by highest level used; check and hint latency. **By kind** (§20.11): for each hint kind and level, and each horizon for waypoints, the adaptive-hints spec's per-hint outcomes (its §6.1: unaided next progress — here the next check's valid step or a placement reaching the hint's target node —, more help needed, time to the next valid step, "doesn't connect" by shortcut, grade, later independent success with how many are pending), split by the LO's placement intent and the predictor's P(success) at the request (its expertise-reversal check); **goals reached after a waypoint** without more help, and how often "a smaller step toward this goal" was taken; suggested against taken kinds by help style; and, once a month of rows exists, its off-policy estimates per kind with their propensities, effective sample sizes and intervals (its §6.3, H5). These rows are read from that spec's `learnloop hints report` for `surface = 'workbench'`, not recomputed | RW2a/b |
| Cues | Impressions, open, check-me and not-relevant rates; cues per reading hour (**interruption burden**); for discovered cues, next attempt on the cue's facets for shown vs held-out, IPS-weighted by the logged propensity, with an interval and its sample size | RW3a/b |
| Library | Asks; organizer-line validation failures; tutor follow-up rate; compare usage; practice-this pick rate; "nothing fits" rate and exercise-from-sources picks | RW4a/b |
| Figures | Requests; verified / illustrative / failed / unsupported shares (by kind); predictions made and missed | RW5a |
| Calibration | Confidence before submission (optional) and reconnect "yes" against the next independent outcome on the same facets: counts of **confident misses** and **unconfident successes**, never a score | RW2a, RW1a, RW6b |
| Interventions → outcomes | For each shown hint, bridge, comparison, figure and cue: the next attempt on the same item or facets (immediate continuation) and the later cold outcome; how many have **no follow-up observation yet**. Associations, never causal credit | RW2b, RW6b |
| Loop closure | Follow-ups per session (the distribution, not a cap), by purpose; scheduled, undone, retired by re-valuation, served, answered and left as "later"; `no_item` rate; minutes spent on follow-ups as a share of plan minutes; **cold success after assisted reader work** (the dialogue's primary outcome), beside cold success on comparable items without reader help, and per hour of active reading and workbench time (the dialogue's "per unit of learner effort"); recap and narrative dismissal | RW6a/b |
| Valuation calibration | For each answered follow-up: the predicted outcome distribution P(E) against the observed outcome (log loss and a reliability table, prequential by date), predicted EIG against the realized change in the hypothesis posterior, and whether a pending decision flipped as its EVSI predicted; the `abstain` rate. **From the learner's side, at no extra cost** (UX §9.3): realized minutes (`practice_attempts.latency_seconds`) against expected minutes; **undo of a scheduled check, with its optional reason, by purpose and by predicted value** — direct evidence that the check was worth less than its minutes and burden to this learner, so evidence about λ and the burden; skips of a scheduled check in the plan, a weaker form of the same signal. The learner is never asked to rate usefulness. These are the same fields as the sources spec's §11.4, and they feed the `evsi/calibration` track and the decision-value spec's readiness audit (`diagnosis/causal_selection_audit.py`) as the first live EVSI data. Reports, never thresholds (R6) | RW6b |
| *Added by the restoration:* | | |
| Intentions | Actions by intention (orient, understand, apply, consolidate, transfer) per reading hour; routing corrections by intention | RW1b |
| Formative signals | Tier-2 records per session by kind; gaps named per source kind; **precision of reader-named gaps**: the share later matched by a diagnosed misconception or a missed follow-up on the same facet, and the share contradicted by an unassisted success | RW1b, RW2b, RW5a, RW6a |
| Discrimination in the workbench | Discriminating questions offered and answered; how often the answer agreed with the diagnoser's cause at Submit; predicted EIG against how far the answer narrowed the rivals in the diagnoser's verdict; error-kind guesses against the diagnosis | RW2b |
| Tutor and sources | Answers by mode (excerpt, grounded, bridge); **abstentions** ("your books don't cover this directly") and excerpts dropped by quote verification, a proxy for unsupported content (round 3 §4); "another way" and "that didn't help" rates, and whether the next exchange resolved the inquiry; buttons shown and pressed by kind | RW1c |
| What helps | `help_outcomes` by help kind and passage role; for passage draws, the next-step continuation and later cold outcome by option, IPS-weighted, with interval and sample size | RW2c |
| Comparisons and roles | Compares, keeps, my-takes; kept relations by type; role proposals by role and judge, and agreement between judges on the same span | RW4d, RW4e |
| Reading paths | Labels by kind, overrides ("read anyway" on enrichment, "skip" on core), and, after RW7b, misses on LOs whose core section was skipped | RW7a/b |
| Figures (extended) | Requests by panel kind; animation and region use; offered vs requested figures; "what changed?" answers; "not right?" revisions and whether the new version verified | RW5a/b |
| Follow-up restorations | Candidates by kind (item, instrument, textbook exercise, generated); exposure-rule exclusions and same-surface rates; generation requests, verified items produced and their use; "back to where you studied this" opens | RW6b |

The three experiments the dialogue proposes map as follows:
- **Generic vs work-aligned hints** is *not* run: it would deliberately give worse help. The reconciliation contract is the design; recovery, dependence and unusable-hint rates are tracked. The hint chooser's sampled kinds (§5.5) and RW2c's passage draws are the randomized comparisons of help that withhold nothing: every option passed the same checks, and the learner can take any rung.
- **Cues vs no cues** is the RW3b holdout.
- **Summaries alone vs typed evidence plus summaries for routing** belongs to router R3, whose replay can ablate the card's reader section.

**One math-heavy textbook first** (round 2 §9). The first evaluation of each phase runs on a `fixtures-local/` copy of the probability vault (derivations give the most concrete chances to check intermediate reasoning, and it holds the dialogue's own mixture and two-sided exponential examples), then on linear algebra. A randomized whole-workflow comparison against a plain PDF-plus-chat reader (round 1's "first experiment") is deferred and labelled (§17): at n=1 it means withholding the workbench from some sections on purpose.

**Usability walkthrough.** At the RW1a and RW2a checkpoints the owner may do one optional, timed task walkthrough (ask → branch → return; open → solve → leave → resume), recorded in the phase's review file. It is never required (R1). The friction audit (§4.1, G52) is the required, agent-run counterpart. The UX document's owner checkpoints (its §9.1) and its first-week checklist (its §9.4; thirteen questions since §20.11 added one about goals to aim for) are the optional owner-side companions; a "no" names the screen and moment, and the phase's review file records it.

## 16. Decisions: recommended defaults (each default; owner may override)

| # | Decision | Recommended default |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Phase order | RW1a → RW3a, RW6a, RW2a → RW1b, RW4a → RW2b → RW1c, RW4b → RW3b, RW4d → RW5a, RW4e, RW7a → RW2c, RW5b, RW2d → RW7b; RW6b whenever DL2 lands (§13) |
| D2 | The selection's first interaction | The intent box, shown unfocused below the selection (typing focuses it; scroll and copy keep working), with the palette one key away (Tab). An empty Enter asks. Selecting never switches the rail tab. **UX integration (§20.9):** was "with the cursor in it" |
| D3 | Who routes the intent box | The Decider (one typed choice, about 2 s), started when typing pauses so Enter is usually instant; fallback Ask. Speculation stops at 80% of the daily call cap and can be switched off |
| D4 | Inquiry status | Reuse `question_events.resolution` (plus reopen), and the `test_me_later` / `help_me_remember` commitments. No new status words |
| D5 | Port or rebuild branching Ask | Port `origin/feat/reader-clarification-branches` |
| D6 | Workbench evidence mapping | A visible source, checks and levels 1–3 add to `hints_used` (assisted: schedules, never certifies); levels 4–5 make the attempt `primed`; hint text overlapping the key is a `tutor_answer` reveal with a workbench basis naming the kind. The level alone decides it, whatever the kind (§20.11); counted on the server from `help_assists`. No new rule, so no successor |
| D7 | Workbench model routes | The ChatGPT-plan provider (merged on `main` at `57d31c63`) once the owner has signed in; until then a fast OpenRouter chat profile. Codex only if the latency report is acceptable. Hints use the adaptive-hints spec's `hint_writer` and `hint_chooser`, with the same recommendation (its D9) |
| D8 | First offer | **Reworked (§20.11):** a (level, kind, horizon) choice — the adaptive-hints spec's chooser, the Decider over typed state plus the help style (default "balanced"), time-boxed at 3 s — **sampled from its own probabilities with the propensity logged** (that spec's D4; `argmax` is the alternative, with no off-policy comparison). Fallback: rung 1, then one rung up per further Help press on the same step, with the item's authored hints serving rung 3 while they last |
| D9 | Where the workbench is the default, and whether the book starts open | In the Reader, at half the window, opening at once with the verbatim stem. **The book starts open or hidden by the entry's purpose (UX S7; §20.9):** open for "work through this", resume and the Guide's exercise; hidden for "check me", "practice this question", "a short problem instead" and plan items; one key flips it, with the consequence stated. On the practice screen and in the plan, a remembered "Work in steps" button, not the default, until the RW2b checkpoint |
| D10 | Cue restraint | A density budget the learner sets (off · fewer · normal · more = 0, 1, 2, 4 per section; default normal), plus at most one LearnLoop mark per screenful at every setting (UX S8), none on the restored line or in skim mode; ordered by the probability the target is still open, with targets already shown this visit last; a 10% logged holdout on discovered cues only. The learner's own marks don't count |
| D11 | Cue kinds | "Revisit a distinction" from RW3a; "a new application of something you've shown" and "try this step first" from RW3b |
| D12 | Library answers | Sources first, grouped by source and role, with prerequisite and application passages from the LO graph; the tutor on request; personalization on, with a toggle |
| D13 | Compare sources | **Restored (§20.8), aligned (§20.9):** the comparison is the sources spec's `compare_sources` (quote-verified claims and typed relations, no source cap, a prompt bound, "also discussed in"); the Reader shows it as a table with three voices apart, and "Keep" stores proposals in `source_objects` / `source_object_relations`, with the learner's own take as `learner_connects`; the tutor requests it through that spec's tool requests. No claim extraction beyond what the learner compares |
| D14 | Roles | **Restored:** ingest's `role_hint` decides exclusions; finer roles (several per passage, with confidence) accumulate as `source_objects` proposals from judges already running; an owner-run batch labeller |
| D15 | Figures | Declarative only; RW5a: function plots with shaded areas, PMF bars, linear maps, readouts with integrals and sums, animation, region selection; RW5b: heat maps, vector fields, trajectories, offered figures. Unverified reconstructions downgraded to labelled illustrations; one prediction per figure by default |
| D16 | Recap and digest | "Last time here" on returning, deterministic, with **"Since last time"** (what the learner has since demonstrated, UX S10). **Restored:** an optional "Wrap up", offered in the rail and, after leaving, **at the destination** for one view (UX S11), never blocking. The narrative on, collapsed in the card and in Wrap up |
| D17 | Cold follow-ups | **Owner, 2026-10-12 (§20.7):** automatic, with **no count cap**. The existing value-of-information machinery chooses, numbers and dates them: probe EIG over the learner's open hypotheses, and EVSI (`scheduling/evsi.py`) for the next decision, stopping when a check's robust value no longer pays for its minutes. Discriminating checks next day; retention checks at effective due; `max_wait_days` 14 as a time cap; 3-day fallback without FSRS state. Bounded by the plan's minutes and a $0.50 daily spend cap. Labelled with purpose and valuation, undo each or all; DL2 "Check once" commitments dated by `due_hint`; `find_practice` (with the sources spec's textbook exercises and intents once merged); **restored:** formative questions, exposure and novelty labels, background generation when nothing fits, a way back to where it was studied |
| D18 | Reading paths | **Restored:** RW7a in the Guide (display only) now; RW7b, the plan following the path, as a successor with a kill switch |
| D19 | Spend | Metered caps of $1.00 a day each for the workbench and library, $0.50 for cues; hint calls under the hint service's own caps ($1.00 a day for writer and chooser, $0.50 for maps, the adaptive-hints spec's D10; §20.11 C8); Codex `estimated_only` |
| D20 | Phone | No new RPC is phone-allowed in v1 |
| D21 | Hint feedback | "This doesn't connect to my work" on every hint, with four shortcuts ("that's not where I'm stuck", added by UX S14; "too big a jump"; "I'm using a different approach", which keeps the learner's route; "the notation"), used immediately. **Defined effects (§20.11):** the next hint keeps the level; "not where I'm stuck" moves its step, "too big a jump" shortens the horizon or picks a finer kind, "a different approach" stays on the learner's route, "the notation" restates in the learner's symbols |
| D22 | Confidence | Optional before a workbench Submit, logged only; reported as calibration counts |
| D23 | W1's middle tier | **Owner, 2026-10-12:** formative signals steer hypotheses (through learner-text gaps), routing and follow-up valuation; never the mastery posterior, evidence, certification or FSRS. Changing serving takes a successor |
| D24 | Ask before repairing | With two or more rival explanations open, rung 1 is a discriminating question ranked by the existing EIG. **Reworked (§20.11 C1):** whether it is the suggested rung is the chooser's sampled judgment, whose instructions favour asking first; "guide me" leans away from it, and it stays on rung 1 for a learner who wants it. Never offered with one rival |
| D25 | What helps | A replayable read of help kinds and later outcomes, read by the hint writer and chooser as associations; logged draws only between options that all passed the judge |
| D26 | Workbench field signals | **Restored as RW2d:** a successor, live behind its kill switch, rather than waiting for a usage trigger |
| D27 | The source-practice backend | Built by `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md` as a separate track; every consuming Reader phase works without it and gains it when it merges |
| D28 | Tutor actions | The tutor requests, through the sources spec's tool requests (practice, a passage for an intent, a comparison); the Reader adds two buttons of its own (make this explorable, try the next step first); the learner presses. The tutor never serves, schedules or imports |
| | *Added by the UX integration (§20.9); D29–D41 are the UX document's open questions Q1–Q13, with its recommended defaults:* | |
| D29 | Reading font for the extracted-text view (Q1) | Offer *mono · proportional* in Settings; **default mono**, the owner's established look (the PDF surface, the default view, shows the book's own type). Revisit after RW1a's week |
| D30 | Rail in the reading posture (Q2) | Open by default at 360 px, collapsible with `\`, remembered per device |
| D31 | Help style scope (Q3) | Global and remembered, with a per-session override in the workbench header |
| D32 | "A short problem instead" mid-problem (Q4) | Opens in the same working posture as a second workbench session, book hidden, with a crumb back; the original problem stays open |
| D33 | Mark density default (Q5) | *Normal* (2 per section, and 1 per screenful); *fewer* one key away from every mark |
| D34 | What counts as activity for Wrap up (Q6) | At least one inquiry, workbench session, formative answer or kept comparison; scrolling, highlights and cue taps alone don't |
| D35 | Money on paid actions (Q7) | Not shown by default; a route's cost appears in the routed line only when its daily cap is within 20%. The reports show spend |
| D36 | Scheduled checks in the recap (Q8) | The next three by date, then "later ▸" with no number (the UX document's "later: 2 more" would be a count, against its own U12); retired checks show for one visit |
| D37 | "Not right?" on a gap named from the learner's text (Q9) | Appends a `learner_card` verdict `different` with optional text and hides the gap from cue targets for this learner. **Needs the learner-text-gaps owner's confirmation** that the pipeline can honour it (§18) |
| D38 | A read-only recap on the phone (Q10) | Not in v1 (D20); deferred with a trigger (§17) |
| D39 | Figure animation (Q11) | Off until the learner presses play; step buttons under reduced motion |
| D40 | "Check me" from a cue (Q12) | Opens in the Reader's working posture with the book hidden, so the learner returns to the passage; a learner pick under DL1 |
| D41 | Rail tab on a routed Ask (Q13) | The Ask tab opens, because the learner asked; answers arriving while the learner is in another posture only mark the tab |
| D42 | Help during a check on your own (the UX document's one confirmation) | **No confirmation dialog.** Help opens the ladder first, whose header says that taking any rung makes the attempt practice with help, and Enter takes a rung: the ladder's ordinary choose-then-take step stops a stray key from revealing anything, and the consequence is stated before it happens (U11) without adding a step (W3). A request to DL2, whose surface it is (§18) |
| D43 | "Check me" and "quick check" | Two names for two things: "check me" is always an ordinary attempt on your own (evidence); "quick check" is always a formative question that is never an attempt. The reconnect line's "check me" becomes "quick check" |
| D44 | When the tutor's offers run in the Reader (§20.10) | **Fetch early, show on press.** `find_practice` and `find_sources` start when the answer lands (the sources spec's D5, eager); their results, impressions and exposures wait for the learner's press; `compare_sources` runs only when pressed. Nothing is shown, served, scheduled or imported unasked (§4.6) |
| D45 | Where a hint sits, and what it continues from (§20.10) | Under its `about_step` (the step it addresses, usually the first questionable one), with a label naming its checkpoint (`continue_from`), which is never the questionable step or a step that depends on it (§5.2 rules 2–3) |
| D46 | What a check on your own shows before feedback (§20.10) | Nothing that locates the exercise beyond the book's name — no exercise number, page, section or link — and no *why*, until feedback (P3 invariant 10). The plan line before the check keeps its purpose and *why* (§9.3 item 10; requests to DL2 and the sources spec, §18) |
| | *Added by the adaptive-hints fold-in (§20.11):* | |
| D47 | Hints in the workbench | From the adaptive-hints spec's hint service (its phase H3): its kinds per level, waypoints with a horizon, labelled subgoal plans and approach maps; one writer (`hint_writer`), one chooser (`hint_chooser`) and one assists table (`help_assists`, with a `surface` column) for every surface. The Reader keeps the ladder and its levels, the reconciliation contract (with placement and kind rules added), the evidence mapping and the presentation. A waypoint is never shown unasked, and after one, "a smaller step toward this goal" is one key away |

**Owner confirmations, 2026-10-20** (queued at the RW1c landing, recorded in this spec at the AD2+AD3 landing, 2026-10-24; the owner: *"q1: no switch, q44: end it honestly with no replacement, q45: yes it ends the check. for rw6a yes to defaults"*):
- **Q1** (`docs/reviews/regrade-contract-replay.md`): no kill switch for the mvp-0.26 regrade; rollback means restoring the backup.
- **Q44** (`docs/reviews/serve-eligibility-decision.md`): a withdrawn cold check ends honestly, with no replacement surface.
- **Q45** (the same record): practice after a check is scheduled ends the check, as built.
- **RW6a** (`docs/reviews/reader-workbench-RW6a.md` §6): all six defaults confirmed, and abandoned problems stay on the recap card (`reader-rw6a-codex-review.md` F8).

## 17. Deferred, with triggers

After the restoration (§20.8), everything left here is either too ambitious to build usefully at n=1 today, blocked on something outside this spec, or would withhold help on purpose. Each item says what it would take. Items the first revision deferred and the restoration built are listed at the end, so nothing silently disappears.

| DEFERRED | What it would take | Revisit when |
|---|---|---|
| **Generated-code figures** in an isolated, unprivileged surface (round 2 §2's fallback "for unusual simulations"; the attention, agent-based and general-simulation cases RW5b's grammar can't express; and the dialogue's most ambitious form, turning a whole paper into an executable interactive system, after PaperVoyager) | A Tauri isolation design with no IPC, file or network access (Tauri warns about iframe isolation on Linux), a working Rust toolchain on carson (B3), a code-review and sandboxing story for model-written JavaScript, and its own verification. The unsupported-kind counts from RW5a/b are its evidence | ≥ 10 unsupported requests the grammar can't express, *and* the isolation design is reviewed, *and* B3 is fixed · *Superseded (2026-10-25): built as RW5d's sketch tier on the owner's direction; see `spec_reader_show_me_figures_v1.md` §9, P2 (the unsupported-kind counts remain its evidence)* |
| **Page-image (visual) retrieval** (ColQwen-style page embeddings; round 1 §7) | A page-image index beside T6's text index, a multimodal embedding route, and T6 benchmark B4's verdict that images beat Marker text on this corpus. Owned by the embedding-consumers spec | T6 open question 4's multimodal rematch shows page images beating Marker text |
| **OS-level learning service** (MCP, editor or browser capture; round 1 §10) | A capture endpoint outside the desktop app, consent and scoping for what an outside app may send, and an MCP server exposing read-only tools (`find_sources`, the sources spec's `find_practice`, capture). Belongs with the mobile/tailnet work, not the Reader | DL3's capture box is used most days *and* the owner reports capturing from another app as a gap; or the sources spec's tool interface is merged and the owner wants an outside agent to call it |
| **Local rerankers** (Qwen3-Reranker; round 1 §7) | A local model server on carson and a T6 benchmark showing parity with LLM verification | LLM verification latency is the measured bottleneck in the RW4, RW1c or RW2b latency blocks |
| **Learned cue policies** (the dialogue's "learn which cues are worth showing from later independent performance"). *The hint half moved (§20.11):* a learned hint chooser — a logged mixture of the Decider and a per-context Thompson-sampling model over kinds, rewarded by later independent success — is the adaptive-hints spec's (its §6.4 and §13, with its own trigger), and the workbench's sampled kinds feed it | The cue value's gain and cost terms fitted from holdout outcomes; a policy learner with logged propensities; router §13's learned-scorer conditions | ≥ 500 discovered-cue impressions with matured outcomes and propensities, *and* router §13's conditions |
| **Formative signals as numeric weak observations** (round 2 §6: "possibly low-weight evidence if independently validated") | An observation model for each tier-2 kind with its reliability, validated against later independent outcomes; a learner-field harness arm under its §7.4; an algorithm successor. Until then tier 2 steers hypotheses and routing only (W1) | The formative-signals report shows reader-named gaps or missed predictions precede later misses on the same LO, directionally, with an interval |
| **Full step-level solution graphs with every branch** (round 2 §4: "a partial solution graph… possible continuations"; RW2b builds the learner's own step graph). **Partly superseded (§20.11):** alternative routes now exist at subgoal level as the adaptive-hints spec's approach maps (its §4.3: the reference route, the source's worked example, the trace contract's recipes, distinct routes a capable student would take, and the learner's own successful routes), built by its H1; RW2b places hints on them, and a hint continues along the matched route (§5.2 rule 7). What stays deferred is the step level | Several verified full solutions per item (the book's, generated alternates) with every branch, step-level matching of the learner's step graph against each, and symbolic checks on map edges (the adaptive-hints spec's §13 deferral) | With approach maps live, "I'm using a different approach" replies still exceed 20% of hint sessions over a month, or "doesn't connect" replies are traced to maps too coarse to place the learner's steps |
| **Formal verification of proof steps** (row 33's remainder: "follows from" for proofs) | A proof assistant (Lean) and autoformalization of informal steps; sympy cannot check proof moves | The check-coverage report shows proof steps are most of the unchecked steps *and* an autoformalization route is reliable on the owner's sources |
| **Cross-learner data-driven hints** (Continuous Hint Factory, round 2 §4) | Partial-solution states from many learners on shared items. At n=1 there is no such data; RW2c seeds hints from the learner's own history instead | LearnLoop has more than one learner with workbench sessions on shared items |
| **A learned, causal model of which help works for this learner** (round 3 §7: "learn whether a particular learner benefits more from worked examples, visualizations, counterexamples or hints") | Enough randomized variation (RW2c's draws, and the hint chooser's sampled kinds with their propensities, §5.5) with matured cold outcomes to estimate per-kind effects with intervals; a policy that acts on them; router R3's off-policy machinery and the adaptive-hints spec's off-policy report (its §6.3, H5) | RW2c's draws reach ≥ 200 with matured cold outcomes across at least three help kinds, or the adaptive-hints spec's learned-chooser trigger holds (its §13) |
| **A randomized whole-workflow experiment** (round 1's "PDF-plus-chat vs LearnLoop with a delayed independent test") | An n=1 alternating-treatments design: sections randomized to a plain reader (every RW switch off) or the full Reader, both followed by RW6b's cold checks; the loop-closure block stratified by arm. It withholds the workbench from half the sections on purpose | The owner opts in |
| **Claim extraction over the whole library, with a claim matcher** (round 2 §5: "keep each author's claim"; RW4d keeps the claims the learner compares) | A batch claim extractor into `source_objects`, a cross-source claim matcher (a T1 match kind), and P3's review path for promoting relations | Kept comparisons exceed 10 a month *and* the owner asks to browse claims across sources |
| **A CAS calculator in the workbench** (the "calculators" in round 1's context-switching pain point) | Its own assistance mapping (a computed step is help) | The owner asks |
| **Photo of handwritten work as steps** (the phone as the camera, steps arriving in the desktop workbench in the learner's voice, labelled "transcribed by LearnLoop's model") | DL4's `image_transcription` route | DL4's route is merged |
| **A read-only "Last time here" on the phone** (UX Q10, D38) | A phone RPC for the recap read, pinned in the mobile allowlist with its own review | The mobile client is used most days |
| **Randomized hint-quality experiments** | — | Not planned: they would withhold better help on purpose. RW2c's draws compare options that all passed the judge instead, and the hint chooser samples within the Decider's own uncertainty (§5.5), which offers nearly always what the Decider is confident in, keeps every rung takeable and withholds nothing |

The UX document's §11.2 says where each deferred item would appear, so building one later needs no new posture.

**Restored from the first revision's deferrals (no longer deferred):** goal-aware reading paths (RW7a/b, §10); the router querying sources as a tool (now built by the sources spec); an LLM role labeller (RW4e, §7.9); source claims and stored cross-source relations, for the comparisons the learner keeps (RW4d, §7.8); generated follow-ups (RW6b item 8); follow-ups from textbook exercises (RW6b through the sources spec); field signals from mid-work stuck steps and early help (RW2d, §5.11); the general figure language (RW5a/b, §8); broader symbolic checks (§5.3); full solution graphs, as the learner's own step graph (§5.2) and, at subgoal level, the adaptive-hints spec's approach maps (§20.11); figure predictions and reconnect answers as signals (tier 2, W1); workbench and figures on video transcripts (§4.1).

### 17.1 Owned by other specs (not designed here)

So that nothing the dialogue raised is silently absent from this spec, these ideas are listed with their owners. The coverage audit's ELSEWHERE rows carry the detail.

## 18. Cross-spec notes (record when each phase merges)

- **Source practice and probes (`spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md`, branch `sources/canonical-practice`, committed at `ae82eb08`).** The owner's decision of 2026-10-12: "build the router and sources path as its own backend track now". That spec owns, and this spec only consumes (names as committed):
  - **S0, real callers for `find_practice`:** the tutor's deterministic practice request (`instructional/offers.py:practice_request_for_answer`), learner picks with a `proposal_id`. RW4a's "Practice this question", RW1c's "Practice this" and DL3's "Practice on…" are one RPC.
  - **S1, textbook exercises as practice candidates** (the audit's RW4c): a source-exercise arm in `find_practice`, import with a verifier ladder and honest key labels, family attachment, `PracticeItem.canonical_exercise`. Consumed by RW4a, RW4b (the view), RW3's "Check me", RW1c, RW7a (one exercise per core section) and RW6b (candidates).
  - **S2a, the discriminating search and the valuation:** `PracticeConstraints.intent` (`practice` \| `discriminate` \| `retention` \| `remediate`), `target_hypotheses`, the typed filters, `instructional/valuation.py:value_candidates` and `open_hypotheses`, and the exposure rule with its equivalent-worked-example judge (its §5.4). Consumed by RW2b ("a short problem instead"), RW3's "Check me" on a target with rivals, and RW6b.
  - **S3, `find_sources` with intents and `compare_sources`:** consumed by RW1b ("another way to see this", `alternative`), RW1c (`explain`, `alternative`, `remediate`), RW2b's bridge (through `TutorSpanProvider`'s intents), RW2c (`worked_example` with `learner_work_md`), RW4a and RW4d (`compare_sources`).
  - **S4, the tool interface:** the tutor's `tool_requests` (rendered in the Reader by RW1c, its SC11), `practice_availability` (the router's availability pass) and the router R2 wiring it defers.
  - **S5, the intervention records:** read by RW2c's help outcomes once merged; the calibration fields that RW6b's receipt matches.
  - **Built once, by whichever lands first, to that spec's design and in its modules:** the base `find_sources` (§7.2), `compare_sources` with `source_comparisons` (§7.3), and `value_candidates` with `open_hypotheses` and the exposure rule (§9.3). A Reader phase that builds one carries that spec's migration and tests for it; that spec's phase then extends it. G53 holds in both orders.
  - **Requests to that spec** (each small; each Reader feature works without it):
    1. an `expand` argument on `find_sources` (`prerequisites` \| `applications` \| `confusables`, read from the LO graph as labelled extra lists), so RW4a's caller-side expansion becomes one call (§7.2);
    2. the `roles` filter matches RW4e's `source_objects.authorial_role` proposals as well as `role_hint` (low confidence only when nothing better exists), and `source_rerank` and `source_compare` return an optional per-passage `roles` field, so their judgments add proposals (§7.9);
    3. the relevance step of `value_candidates` returns a novelty label (`same_surface` \| `new_surface` \| `new_application`) relative to caller-supplied recently worked item ids (§9.3);
    4. its §5.2 table gains "tutor, reader context inside a workbench session → `assisted_practice` with the session's item" (§4.6);
    5. S5 copies a drawn passage's `propensity` and `seed` from RW2c's assist row into its reserved fields, and records RW6b's served follow-up checks with their predicted P(E) (or reads RW6b's receipt), so the calibration track sees one record type (§5.10, §9.3);
    6. a request API for "when nothing fits" generation, which RW6b's item 8 would call (§9.3);
    7. its §19 dispositions now point here: stored comparison relations are RW4d's "Keep" (its own trigger), the instructional-role labeller is RW4e, tutor-initiated explorable figures are RW5b, and the narrow passage draw is RW2c's; the integrative problem this spec once requested is its `facet_match = "all"`;
    8. its practice-screen header ("From *Book*, Exercise 4.12 · open in source", its §7.4) hides the exercise number as well as the link during a cold administration, naming the book only, and shows both after submission: an exercise number locates the exercise and its solution as surely as a page does (P3 invariant 10; D46).
- **Adaptive hints (`spec_adaptive_hints_v1.md`, branch `docs/adaptive-hints`, committed at `67adef8a`).** The owner's request of 2026-10-13: hints written for the learner's current work, waypoints rather than only next steps. That spec owns, and this spec only consumes: the hint service (`learnloop/hints/`, `request_hint`, its §4.1), kinds, levels and horizons (§4.2), approach maps and their build triggers (§4.3), placement (§4.4), the chooser with sampled propensities (§4.5), the writer, its shared contract rules and leak control (§4.6), authored hints as seeds (§4.7), per-hint outcomes and the off-policy report (§6), the routes `hint_writer`, `hint_chooser`, `approach_map` and `hint_outcome` with their money caps (§7), and the `approach_maps` table. `help_assists` is shared and built once by whichever of RW2b and its H0 lands first; the service's core likewise, if RW2b is ready before its H2 (G64). RW2b is its phase H3. Its §8.2 proposals are ruled on in §20.11. **Requests to that spec** (each small; each workbench feature works without it):
  1. name its assists column `hint_kind` (its §9 `kind`) and add the workbench's columns and `assist_kind` with every value in §5.7, so the shared table has one migration (§20.11 C9);

  5. use §5.2's EIG ranking of candidate questions as the `discriminating` kind's selection step on every surface, and exempt "the notation" from its rule 5's change of strategy (C1, C17);
  6. write the reveal `basis` with a surface prefix (`workbench_hint:<kind>:L<n>` in the workbench; its `hint:<kind>:L<level>` elsewhere; C15);
  7. if its practice screen needs a learner-text source kind for "doesn't connect" text (its `hint_feedback`), add it to the one `source_kind` widening migration with `workbench_text` and `reader_formative` (§3.4 item 13), so `learner_text_gaps` is rebuilt once (C12); its §15 note names `workbench_stuck`, which is `workbench_text` here;
  8. read the help style as one stored preference on every surface, with the workbench header's per-session override on `workbench_sessions.help_style` (D31; C7);
  9. its §8.2 item 9 cites "§5.3 'Check my work'": that is §5.2 here (§5.3 is symbolic checks).
- **Owner decision, 2026-10-12: lift the other four count caps on value of information.** §20.7 removed the Reader's follow-up count caps. The owner has decided that the same basis applies to the four count caps that live elsewhere:
  - `probe.episode.session_qualifying_observation_cap` (4; with `maximum_observations`, 4) for probe episodes (`config/schema.py:224`, `:209`);
  - `scheduler.followup.max_interventions_per_lo_per_session` (1) for post-attempt follow-ups (`schema.py:40`, enforced at `diagnosis/followups.py:339`);
  - `teach_back.session_cap` (1) (`schema.py:625`, enforced at `scheduling/scheduler.py:568`);
  - `plan.project_probes_max_per_day` (4) in the daily plan (`schema.py:1885`, read at `daily_plan/engine.py:462`).

  **It is a separate serving stage, not part of this spec.** Each cap changes what is served, so the stage is an algorithm successor of its own (the next free `mvp-0.N` at its merge), with a kill switch per cap that restores the old count byte-identically, receipts that record what the count would have allowed, and plan parity under the switches. Each count becomes the stop rule RW6b uses: add another observation, follow-up, teach-back or project probe while the best remaining candidate's robust value (EIG converted to minutes, or EVSI where a loss table exists) exceeds λ·minutes + burden, valued conditionally on those already chosen; time (session and plan minutes) and money (spend caps) bound the rest. It reuses the shared candidate-valuation adapter and `evsi.rank_feasible`; it adds no decision parameter. Its calibration inherits RW6b's (§20.4 risk 9). The stage's owner is the orchestrator's next serving track; this spec only records the decision. Noticed while auditing and not part of the decision: `teach_back.max_followups` (4) and `plan.reentry_max_items` (8) are also counts.
- **P3 Reader spec.** §5.4's inquiry thread is implemented through `question_events.resolution` and branch parents, with no projection table. §1.3's "syntopic comparison UI" exclusion is lifted by RW4a under §7.1–7.2's source-local rules. **RW4d and RW4e now write proposals into P3 §7's source-object layer** (kept comparisons; instructional roles), with authorship and review status as P3 §7.1–7.2 require; nothing becomes canonical without P3's review path. Invariants 1, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10 are restated in W12, W1, G43, RW4d's "my take", W2 and W7. "Posterior" in invariant 6 is read as the mastery posterior (W1).
- **Quick-check producer.** Gains `scope_kind` (section, inquiry, selection) and `question_style` (recall, next step, why step, assumptions, reconstruct). Section behaviour is unchanged; the section lookup filters on scope.
- **Daily loop.**
  - **DL2 (requests from the UX integration, §20.9):** (a) help during a check on your own opens the ladder first, with "taking any rung makes this practice with help" in its header, and Enter takes a rung — choose-then-take, no confirmation dialog (D42); (b) a textbook exercise's header during a check on your own names the book and nothing that locates the exercise in it — no exercise number, page, section or link — and no *why*, until feedback (D46; the sources spec's item header, request 8 below); (c) the cold banner and the plan line follow §9.3 item 10.
  - **DL1 (request, UX S11):** Today renders the Reader's one-line wrap-up offer for one view after the learner leaves a source with activity, from a read RW6a provides (`reader.wrap_up_pending`); Today owns the placement.
  - **DL §6.10 (refinement, the UX document's resolved conflict):** inside the Reader, `Esc` peels one layer (intent box or palette, then the ladder or a popover, then a peek, then editor focus, then the posture). Only at the outermost layer during a plan reading block does `Esc` mean DL's "back to today's plan" (`left_reader`); elsewhere a stray `Esc` at the outermost layer does nothing. "Back to today's plan" stays one click.
  - **DL2 (request):** the dated "Check once" branch reads `commitment_versions.due_hint` when present and falls back to `capture.test_me_later_days`; RW6b's follow-ups depend on it (§9.3). DL2's provider fills DL1's existing `check_once` scheduled-check kind (`daily_plan/lanes.py:74`). Reader follow-ups can make several checks due on one day; the plan's minutes, not a count, bound how many are served (§9.3 item 6). **DL2 (request, restored):** its restoration after a measurement shows the follow-up's "back to where you studied this" link from the commitment's provenance (§9.3 item 9). DL2's depth intents map onto RW7a's depth choice as one setting.
  - DL1's learner pick (`daily_plan/engine.py:1914`) records RW3's "Check me", RW4a's "Practice this question", RW4b's chosen exercise and RW1c's practice button, which open in the Reader's working posture (D40); "Try this step first" is a formative question, not a pick.
  - DL3's "Practice on…" box and RW4a's "Practice this question" are the same `find_practice` call; build one RPC and reuse it.
  - DL4's pre-questions and RW1's scoped questions share the quick-check producer.
  - DL1's re-entry can show RW6a's card. **RW7b** changes DL1's reading block (a successor, §10.2).
- **Router.**
  - `LearnerStateCardV1` gains the "Reader activity" section from `reader_activity_for_card` (RW6b), now with open hypotheses, formative records, kept comparisons and candidate actions.
  - **§15.3's `search_sources(query)` is the sources spec's `find_sources`**, with its policies; the existing `reader.source_search.search_sources` is the legacy keyword search and keeps its name. §15.3 itself (the tool, its guardrails, "when nothing fits" and the availability pass) is now built by the sources spec, not deferred behind R2.
  - The Reader's in-session help (the hint chooser, discriminating questions, cues) is not a router decision (router §1); the hint chooser borrows the router's propensity convention and receipt fields (adaptive-hints §15).
  - **R2:** each pending reader follow-up becomes a block candidate valued with robust EVSI like any other (router §3), so the router, not the order of lanes, decides whether it is served on a crowded day. RW6b's valuation receipt uses R1's field names (`probe_eig`, `evsi_assignment`) so R3's replay reads both. RW2c's passage draws and the hint chooser's draws log propensities in the same style, for R3.
- **Embedding consumers.**
  - T6 gains consumers (workbench bridging and examples, discovered cues, the library organizer, compare, the Reader-context tutor, the exercise view) through the sources spec's `find_sources` (with the caller-side graph expansion here, until that spec takes the `expand` request). R1–R6 hold: the graph is read, never written by similarity.
  - `judge_listwise` gains the Reader's tasks `passage_cue`, `library_contribution` and `source_role`; the sources spec adds its own.
  - `PracticeConstraints` gains the sources spec's fields, including the `unattempted_only` and `exclude_family_ids` RW6b needs.
  - T1's matcher gains no kind. B4's text-only decision stands; page-image retrieval stays deferred (§17).
- **Blind grading and diagnosis.** The grader is unchanged (G6). The diagnoser's context gains the current attempt's workbench trace when one exists (`+workbench-v1`), including the step graph, the labelled error-kind guesses and any discriminating question with its answer; contexts without a trace hash as before. The Guide reads the diagnoser's overlay (RW3a).
- **Learner field.** Workbench assistance flows into `hints_used` and `primed`, so the `+hints` arm sees it. Formative reader signals reach the field only as question signals and named gaps, under F2 (W1). **RW2d adds two signal sources as an algorithm successor under F3** (§5.11). Numeric weak observations from tier 2 stay deferred (§17).
- **Decision value and probe EIG.** RW6b (or the sources spec's S2a, whichever merges first, through the shared `value_candidates`) is the first live consumer of `scheduling/evsi.py`'s `robust_evsi` and `rank_feasible`; RW2b's discriminating question is a new consumer of `instrument_expected_information_gain`. Both go through adapters that write no learner state. Neither adds a decision parameter; λ, the burden and the nat-to-minutes scale stay the registry's. RW6b's receipts and RW2b's discriminating answers (predicted EIG against what the diagnoser concluded) are inputs to the decision-value spec's readiness audit (§9 there) and to any EVSI calibration track.
- **Learner-text gaps.** Two new source kinds, `workbench_text` (RW2b) and `reader_formative` (RW1b, RW5a), with one migration widening the `source_kind` CHECK on `learner_text_gaps` and `learner_text_gap_jobs` (§3.4 item 13); if the adaptive-hints spec's practice-screen `hint_feedback` kind is ready by then, the same migration carries it (request 7 above). Reader questions, including "what's still unclear?", "that didn't help" and tutor questions from library asks, are already sources. Texts are queued when written, so gaps can be named mid-session (§5.2). Named gaps become RW3b cue targets and RW6b questions.
- **Knowledge model.** Cue links, organizer lines, role proposals and kept relations are labels and proposals. They never touch facet identity or the semantic graph.
- **Mobile.** No new phone RPC. Every new RPC is pinned desktop-only. Checks on your own that are ordinary items already reach the phone through the plan and the existing allowlist, with DL2's banner; a read-only recap on the phone is deferred (§17, D38).
- **Learner-text gaps (request, D37).** "Not right?" on a gap named from the learner's text appends a `learner_card` verdict `different` with optional text and hides that gap from the Reader's cue targets for this learner; the pipeline's owner confirms it can honour that (or names the existing retraction path to use) before RW3b reads named gaps.
- **Decision value and the `evsi/calibration` track.** The Reader's contributions to calibrating value of information, all at no extra cost to the learner (§15): RW6b's receipts with predicted EIG, EVSI and P(E) per chosen check; RW2b's discriminating questions (the predicted answer per rival against the answer given and the diagnoser's verdict at Submit); realized minutes; undo of a scheduled check with its optional reason, and skips in the plan, as revealed evidence about λ and the burden. The learner is never asked to rate usefulness.
- **Handoffs.** Add a "Reader workbench" row to `implementation_handoffs.md` §1 per phase, using §19.
