## 19. Agent prompt template

Fill in `<phase>` (RW1a … RW7b), the phase row from §13 and the base branch. Give it to a fresh agent in the repo. The review and fix steps follow `implementation_goal.md` §2.

```text
Implement LearnLoop reader-workbench phase <phase>, as specified in
docs/specs/spec_reader_workbench_v1.md (§13 row "<phase>", plus the sections that row
cites, §3.4 and §20).

Before coding, read: CLAUDE.md; docs/specs/implementation_handoffs.md §0 (standard rules
and invariants); spec_reader_workbench_v1.md in full; spec_p3_reader_integration.md §1.1;
docs/specs/daily_loop_frictionless_defaults.md; the sections of other specs the row names;
the UX sections named in the phase's "Presentation" line in docs/specs/ux_reader_workbench_v1.md,
with its §1 principles, §3.4 voices, §4.0 states and §4.12 attention budget (presentation; this
spec wins on behaviour, data and gates; record any disagreement in the review file);
spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md (at ae82eb08 or later) if the row consumes it;
spec_adaptive_hints_v1.md (at 67adef8a or later) for RW2b and RW2c;
db/table_roles.py; docs/algorithm-change-playbook.md. Then write a short plan (files,
migrations, successor and kill switches, spend caps and model routes, judgment_ledger
writes, tests) to docs/reviews/reader-workbench-<phase>.md and proceed.

Work in your own worktree on a new branch reader/<phase>, created from <base branch>.
Follow §0 of the handoffs and §2 of the spec, especially:
- reuse before building (W13): T6 for retrieval, practice_index/listwise.judge_listwise
  for every listwise judgment, find_practice for practice, the quick-check producer for
  questions, learner-text gaps for learner text, DL2's "Check once" for follow-ups,
  the existing probe-EIG and scheduling/evsi.py functions for valuing them (§9.3),
  source_objects for roles and kept comparisons, and spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md
  for find_practice's callers, textbook exercises, PracticeConstraints.intent,
  value_candidates and its exposure rule, find_sources and its intents and policies,
  compare_sources and the tutor's tool_requests: consume its interfaces, never
  re-implement them, and keep working when it has not merged (G53); if your phase must
  build one of them first, build it once to that spec's design, in its module;
- every hint, its kind and the first offer come from the adaptive-hints spec's hint
  service (learnloop/hints/: request_hint, its chooser, contracts and approach maps),
  with help_assists as the one assists table and hint_writer / hint_chooser as the
  routes; build its core once, to that spec's design, only if your phase lands first (G64);
- no new interaction_events kind and no new reveal kind (payload `event` fields and
  the tutor_answer kind with a workbench basis instead); the only table rebuild is the
  learner-text source_kind widening (§3.4 item 13);
- the attention budget and voices (W2, W12, W14; G54-G57): nothing LearnLoop starts takes
  focus, animates or opens a modal; every model text has its voice label; every model
  action has its seven states;
- W1's tiers: reading, cues, figures, recaps and inquiry status are salience only;
  formative signals (learner text, check verdicts, hints, predictions) may name gaps
  and steer routing and follow-up valuation, never the mastery posterior, evidence,
  certification or FSRS; the only evidence is a submitted attempt, with assistance
  recorded as §5.4 says;
- the grader stays blind (G6); retrieval follows find_sources' policies (§7.2);
- no algorithm successor unless §12 names one for this phase; if you find one is
  needed, stop the affected part, record why, and take the next free mvp-0.N tag only
  with the orchestrator's agreement; with each kill switch off, behaviour is
  byte-identical;
- recompute the latest DEFAULTS_SNAPSHOT_BY_ALGORITHM entry for new config keys, with a
  "no scoring, scheduling, projection or serving change" note;
- replay makes no model or embedding calls; paid outputs are persisted inputs;
- every paid step uses the spend framework with --dry-run; Codex is allowed for every
  LLM role and is recorded estimated_only; decider tasks never fall back to chat;
- no live or paid AI calls in tests (fakes only); paid runs only on learnloop.db.copies
  backups under fixtures-local/, dry-run first; never commit changes to
  fixtures/linear_algebra, fixtures/probability or fixtures/arxiv_v2;
- if the spec contradicts the code, record it in the review file, take the
  conservative option (switch off, no evidence writes) if work can continue, else stop.

Done means: the phase's gates (§14) pass; its listed tests exist and pass;
uv run lint-imports --no-cache, uv run pytest -n auto and uv run pytest -m durability
pass; npm run typecheck && npm test pass; the DB reference scripts pass if the schema
changed. The review file lists what was built, deviations, test results with pass
counts, files touched, migrations, owner-run commands (dry-run first) and open
questions. Commit with clear messages; do not merge or push.
```

## 20. Integration review and owner decisions (2026-10-12)

An Opus 5.5 reviewer checked every phase against the code on `main` at `68213d96`, the in-flight branches (`merge/dl1`, `router/r1`, `learner/text-gaps`, `providers/chatgpt-plan`, `facets/identity`, `origin/feat/reader-clarification-branches`) and the architecture specs. It then took a Codex reviewer's ROI list (job `20261012-222309-txa3sX`) and revised this spec in place.

### 20.1 What changed, and why

| Change | Why |
|---|---|
| Every phase split into a first slice (a) and a model slice (b); RW7 deferred | The first draft bundled cheap learner benefits with new infrastructure (Codex's main point). Each "a" slice now ships alone, mostly from existing parts. Reading paths weren't in the owner's reply, needed DL2 and a successor, and had the lowest ROI |
| No new `interaction_events` kinds; payload `event` fields on existing kinds | The kind CHECK means every new kind is a full rebuild of a large, FK-referenced table (§3.4 item 4) |
| Reconnect questions: `scope_kind` and `question_style` columns, section lookup filtered | `section_id` is `NOT NULL` and reuse was "newest row for the section", so passage rows would have shadowed section checks |
| The Reader workbench submits through `submit_attempt`, with the visible source as one hint-equivalent | The first draft's `administer_reading_question` path grades and diagnoses nothing (§3.4 item 3). The hint-equivalent rule reuses existing semantics (assisted: schedules, never certifies), so there is still no successor |
| `tutor_answer` reveal kind with a workbench basis, not a new kind | Avoids a `reveal_events` rebuild; a workbench hint is tutor prose |
| Symbolic checks narrowed to three transformations, via PF's LLM-written check with a negative control | No LaTeX parser is installed; "follows from" in general is large (Codex A12) |
| No new field signal from the workbench | Field signals feed Phase 6 serving, so a new source is a successor (§5.6) |
| Stuck steps reach learner-text gaps through one adapter | The pipeline is the observation compiler; reader questions already reach it |
| Bridging runs after the hint, never before | T6 retrieval alone measured about 7 s |
| Practice screen: "Work in steps" is opt-in, not the default | Changing the default answer surface of the daily plan before the RW2 checkpoint risks the daily loop's latency and flow |
| RW3a fixes the Guide's statement under the split | The Guide reads raw `error_events` and misses the diagnoser's overlay on split vaults (§3.4 item 1) |
| RW3b uses T6 retrieval plus `judge_listwise`, not new T1 match kinds | T1's judge is pairwise same/different; cue relevance is a listwise judgment, and that judge already exists with ledger caching |
| No LLM role index; roles from ingest's `role_hint` | Ingest already labels roles deterministically and T6 already excludes exercises and solutions (§3.4 item 2) |
| `find_sources` cut to three policies; `post_attempt` and `authoring` dropped | T6 keeps the feedback context item-bound on purpose; PF's source-anchored path has its own retrieval |
| Compare is display-only; no claims extraction, claim matcher or `source_object_relations` rebuild | Codex A9: test demand before the infrastructure |
| The cross-source tutor uses the `reader` context with a pinned-span provider | `library` questions require a note |
| Router §15.3's `search_sources` is named as `find_sources` | `reader.source_search.search_sources` already exists |
| RW6 follow-ups are DL2 "Check once" commitments with `due_hint`; generation deferred; `find_practice` gains two filters | Reuses DL2's scheduled-check mechanism instead of a new serving class, removes the `reader_followup_v1` successor (with a stated fallback), and keeps generation (CPU-slow, batch) off the session path |
| Successor tags: no named tags; "the next free `mvp-0.N` at merge" | The chain is linear (DL1 `mvp-0.14`, R1 `mvp-0.15`, then learner-text gaps); named tags conflicted with it |
| Defaults fingerprint recompute required per phase | Every new config key moves the pinned snapshot (§3.4 item 9) |
| Branch port facts corrected | 8 backend tests, not 12; conflicts are in `handlers/reader.py`, not `ReaderScreen.tsx` |
| Rollout yields to the goal's tracks; at most two Reader phases open | The workbench is not in the goal's definition of done; Codex capacity is limited (B7) |

This table records the integration review. The owner's restoration (§20.8) supersedes six of its rows: symbolic checks (now every declared kind sympy can check, §5.3), no field signal (now RW2d, a successor), no LLM role index (now RW4e's proposals), `find_sources`' three policies (the restoration added a narrow `post_attempt`; §20.9 withdrew it again in favour of the sources spec's policies, and `authoring` is internal to that spec), display-only compare (now "Keep" as proposals, RW4d), and deferred generation (now a background request, §9.3 item 8). The reasons recorded here still explain why each restoration is built the way it is.

### 20.2 Implementability findings by phase

- **RW1a.** Buildable now. The port is about 700 lines with three Python conflicts; the rail split is a mechanical move of a 3,991-line component and is the largest part. One small migration.
- **RW1b.** Buildable on `main`: the Decider client and `ChoiceQuestion` exist. It needs two `DECIDER_TASKS` entries, config routes, a Decider question set, and a producer prompt for the two new styles. The Decider adds about 2 s to typed intents only.
- **RW2a.** Buildable on `main`. New: two tables, the step editor around `LiveMarkdownEditor`, a write queue, and opening a practice session from the Reader (`start_session`). The frontend is the bulk; the practice-screen button touches `PracticeScreen.tsx`, which `learner/text-gaps` also edits (+13 lines).
- **RW2b.** The largest phase. New: two chat routes, one decider route *(§20.11: the hint and chooser routes are now the adaptive-hints spec's shared `hint_writer` and `hint_chooser`; only `workbench_check` is the Reader's)*, the contract checker, PF-pattern checks, feedback, the chooser's typed state assembly (capability ledger, facet timeline, predictor), the diagnoser context extension, and the text-gaps adapter. Latency depends on the ChatGPT-plan provider; without it the phase works but likely misses the 10 s target on Codex.
- **RW3a.** Small: one backend fix and a frontend margin layer over the Guide plan the rail already loads.
- **RW3b.** Medium: per-target retrieval and a listwise task, two tables, background scheduling on source open. Its gap targets need learner-text gaps merged.
- **RW4a.** Medium: `find_sources` is a thin composition; the organizer and compare are listwise tasks; the tutor needs a pinned-span provider; the library UI changes.
- **RW4b.** Small to medium: a role-filtered search and a hand-off to `import_exercises`.
- **RW5.** Medium to large even narrowed: two evaluators kept in step by a golden corpus, a renderer, a verifier using the sandbox, a figure route with image input. Independent of everything else.
- **RW6a.** Small to medium, all reads. Its summary is reused by RW6b.
- **RW6b.** Medium, blocked on DL2 (not built) and its `due_hint` handling.

*Added by the restoration (§20.8), checked against `main` at `57d31c63` and the branches named:*
- **RW1b (widened).** Two more question styles (in RW1a's migration), the intentions grouping (frontend and the Decider's option set), the `reader_formative` adapter, and possibly the learner-text widening migration. The adapter waits for `learner/text-gaps` (merge chain).
- **RW1c.** Medium. The Reader context gains a span provider (practice, feedback and library already have one, `source_retrieval/tutor.py:70`); the modes and offers are a prompt and response-schema change plus buttons. Its "another way" and "that didn't help" fall back cleanly until the sources spec's intents merge.
- **RW2b (widened).** Still the largest phase. The discriminating question adds a hint-writer output (candidate questions with predicted answers), a small compiler from those predictions to a `CompiledInstrument`, and a call to the existing EIG function; the step graph and error-kind guess are output fields of the existing check call. No new route.
- **RW2c.** Medium, mostly reads: `help_outcomes` is a query over existing rows; `find_examples` is a composition of `find_sources` and one listwise task; compare methods reuses the feedback screen. *(§20.9: the worked-example search is now the sources spec's `worked_example` intent and compare methods reads by id, so RW2c is S–M.)*
- **RW2d.** Small to medium, but a successor: two sources in `field/signals.py`, receipts and parity tests.
- **RW3b (widened).** The value order reads existing posterior and status rows; the try-first kind and the contrast lists are judge and retrieval inputs. Medium to large in total.
- **RW4b (narrowed to a view).** Small: the backend moved to the sources spec.
- **RW4d.** Medium: the source-object writes exist (`reader/source_objects.py`); the comparison prompt gains typed relations; the UI adds keep, my take and the "compared" mark. *(§20.9: the comparison itself is now the sources spec's `compare_sources`, so RW4d keeps the Reader's parts and is S–M.)*
- **RW4e.** Medium: a field in four judges' schemas, the source-object writes, a positive filter in `find_sources`, and a batch CLI with `--dry-run`.
- **RW5a (widened).** Large: `integral` and `sum` in both evaluators with corpus parity, shading and PMF panels, animation, region crops (the crop function exists).
- **RW5b.** Medium: three panels and an RK4 integrator in both evaluators, and one judgment field for offers.
- **RW7a.** Medium: a judge route, one table, the Guide view. **RW7b.** Small to medium, but a successor on DL1's reading block (merged).

### 20.2a What the restoration does not make buildable

Nothing in §17 became buildable by being written down. The generated-code figures, page-image retrieval, the OS-level service, local rerankers, learned policies, numeric weak observations, alternative reference routes, formal proof checks, cross-learner hints, a causal help model and the whole-library claim index each wait on something §17 names: a design outside this spec, data that n=1 does not yet have, or the owner's opt-in.

| Item | Verdict | Where |
|---|---|---|
| A1 Margin cues from the existing Guide first | **Folded** (RW3a), with the split-overlay fix it needs | §6.2 |
| A2 Resumable scratchpad first | **Folded** (RW2a), reusing `LiveMarkdownEditor` and the draft-queue pattern; "leave" vs "abandon" | §5.1 |

### 20.4 Remaining risks

1. **Hint latency.** RW2b's value depends on fast hints. Without the ChatGPT-plan provider (and the owner's sign-in), Codex hints will likely exceed 10 s. Mitigation: authored hints as the fallback, the time-boxed chooser, bridging after the hint, the latency block; the hint service's latency report covers both surfaces (adaptive-hints §6.3).
2. **Reconciliation quality is a model capability.** The contract catches structural failures (no citation, wrong checkpoint, repeated strategy), not a plausible-but-unhelpful hint. The "doesn't connect" rate is the measure; a fake-model test can't prove it.
3. **The source-visible hint-equivalent** is an interpretation of existing semantics. The RW2a reviewer must confirm it against `resolve_attempt_activity_policy` and both certification projections; if they disagree, it becomes a successor.
4. **The follow-up "no successor" ruling** (§9.3) relies on the learner-text-gaps precedent and on DL2 reading `due_hint`. If DL2 lands without it, follow-ups fall back to its fixed 7-day date; if the reviewer rules it a serving change, RW6b takes a tag. Without a count cap, the ruling also rests on the plan's minutes bounding what is served (DL1 §6.1–6.2); a reviewer may judge that uncapped checks change the maintain lane's composition enough to need the tag.
9. **First live EVSI.** RW6b is the first consumer to act on `evsi.rank_feasible`, whose decision parameters (λ, burden, 60 minutes per nat) are labelled heuristic and uncalibrated. A badly scaled nat-to-minutes value schedules too many or too few checks. Mitigations: re-valuation retires checks that stop paying, every check is labelled and undoable, the plan's minutes bound the daily cost, and the valuation-calibration block (§15) measures predicted against realized information.
5. **ReaderScreen churn.** Every phase touches the Reader frontend; the rail split must land first and phases must stay serialized (at most two open).
6. **Data volume.** At n=1, the holdout, calibration and intervention reports will be thin for weeks (blocker B2). They are reports, never gates.
7. **The Rust toolchain (B3)** blocks the owner's in-app check of any new desktop UI until it is fixed.
8. **Text-gaps coupling.** RW1b's and RW2b's adapters and RW3b's gap targets assume the text-gaps interfaces as built on its branch (`LearnerText`, `named_gaps_for_card`, the `source_kind` CHECK); a review change there would ripple here.

*Added by the restoration:*

10. **Tier-2 noise.** More learner text reaches learner-text gaps (missed answers, predictions, "doesn't connect" replies), and that pipeline creates prerequisite edges and misconception candidates live. A noisy source could name gaps that aren't there. Mitigations: the pipeline's own judges decide every match ("embeddings shortlist, judges decide"); every creation it makes stays undoable as it is today; the formative-signals block measures the precision of reader-named gaps (§15); `reader_formative.enabled` turns the new sources off without touching the rest.
11. **Discriminating questions are only as good as the predicted answers.** EIG is computed from what the hint writer says each rival would answer; a wrong prediction makes a useless question look informative. Mitigations: zero-EIG questions are dropped by construction; the diagnoser, not the question, classifies at Submit; the report compares predicted EIG with how far the answer actually narrowed the diagnoser's verdict (§15), which is also calibration data for EIG itself.
12. **Coupling to a spec being written.** Five phases consume `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md`, which did not exist when this revision was written. Every consumption is specified as "works without it, gains it when it merges" (G53), and the shared pieces (`find_sources`, `PracticeConstraints`, the valuation adapter) are built once by whichever lands first. A different interface there means editing the consuming rows here, not redesigning them.
13. **Scope.** The restoration roughly doubles the agent effort (§20.5). Mitigations: every restored phase is independent of at least one other and has its own kill switch; the order puts the owner's pain points (RW2b's discriminating question, RW1c's tutor, RW6b's follow-ups) before the breadth (RW5b, RW4e, RW7); and the Reader still yields to the goal's tracks (§13).
14. **Two successors.** RW2d and RW7b change what is served. Each needs plan parity under its switch and a reviewer's sign-off under the playbook; neither blocks any other phase.
15. **A learner-text table rebuild.** Widening `source_kind` rebuilds `learner_text_gaps`, which three tables reference. It is small and young, but a botched rebuild would lose named gaps. Mitigation: temp-then-rename with `foreign_key_check` before commit, a durability-lane test, and a fixture with rows in every referencing table (G36).

*Added by the UX integration (2026-10-13):*

16. **Speculative routing sends unsubmitted text.** Routing while the learner pauses means text they typed but never sent can reach the Decider's provider. Mitigations: it is disclosed with the Decider's route, it stops at 80% of the daily call cap, and `reader_intent.speculative_enabled = false` returns to routing on Enter only.
17. **Opening the workbench before the item exists.** The learner writes steps against a stem the import has not yet turned into an item, and an import can fail or split a multi-part exercise into several items. Mitigations: the steps belong to the session from the first keystroke; Check, Help and Submit wait for the bound item and say so; a failed import keeps the steps and offers "try again"; if the import yields more than one item, the learner picks the part they are working on and the steps stay; no attempt is ever written without an item (G58).
18. **Coupling to the sources spec's names.** That spec is a draft for owner review; this revision consumes its names as committed at `ae82eb08` (`value_candidates`, `PracticeConstraints.intent` values, `find_sources` intents and policies, `compare_sources`, `tool_requests`). A rename there is an edit to the consuming rows here, and G53's fakes pin the names a Reader phase was built against.
19. **Building another spec's piece first.** When a Reader phase lands before the sources spec's phase that owns `find_sources`, `compare_sources` or `value_candidates`, it builds that piece to the other spec's design without that track's reviewer. Mitigation: the piece lives in the sources spec's module with its tests and gates, and the orchestrator asks that track's reviewer to review it as part of the Reader phase.
20. **The UX document read as requirements.** It is long and specific. Phase agents could treat every wireframe detail as binding, or drop the rules that matter. Mitigation: the gates G54–G58 and the Presentation lines hold what matters (attention, voices, states, return, opening at once); everything else is presentation guidance, and a disagreement is recorded in the phase's review file and settled by this spec until the owner rules.

*Added by the adaptive-hints fold-in (2026-10-13):*

21. **Coupling to a second spec being written.** RW2b now consumes `spec_adaptive_hints_v1.md`, a draft for owner review, for its writer, chooser, kinds and maps, and shares a table and routes with it. Mitigations: this spec cites that one by section and duplicates none of it; §20.11 records the names RW2b is built against and every conflict's resolution; the shared pieces are built once by whichever lands first (G64); a rename there is an edit to the consuming rows here.
22. **Waypoints and sampling may not suit this learner.** The direct evidence for waypoints is mixed (adaptive-hints §1.2), and the Decider may put nearly all its probability on one kind, so sampling buys little comparison (that spec's §14 question 1). Mitigations: waypoints are never unsolicited and always have a smaller step one key away; "too big a jump" is counted by kind; the per-kind rows show goals reached and the expertise-reversal split (§15); the learner can always take any rung; and if the support stays too thin after a month, that spec's D4 revisits an exploration floor.

### 20.5 Build order, dependencies and effort

Re-run for the restored scope (§20.8). "Was" gives the first revision's effort where a phase existed.

| Order | Phase | Depends on (merged or in flight) | Effort (was) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | RW1a | `main` only (DL1, the ChatGPT-plan provider and the guide-overlay fix are merged). The port's `tutor_qa.py` hunk is small against `learner/text-gaps` | M–L (M; §20.9 added the return stack, shared components and gates) |
| 2 | RW3a, RW6a, RW2a (two at a time) | RW1a's rail split | S (S), M (S–M), M–L (M; §20.9 added the split, opening before the import binds and book visibility by entry) |
| 3 | RW1b, RW4a | RW1a; `learner/text-gaps` merged for RW1b's adapter; DL1 for learner picks | M (S–M), M (M) |
| 4 | RW2b | RW2a; the ChatGPT-plan sign-in; `learner/text-gaps` merged; the adaptive-hints spec's H2 (or RW2b builds the service's core once, §20.11), its H1 optional | L (L) |
| 5 | RW1c, RW4b, then RW3b, RW4d | RW4a; the sources spec for intents, candidates and the comparison (each works without); RW3a + text-gaps for RW3b | M (new), S (S–M), M–L (M), S–M (new; §20.9 moved the comparison to the sources spec) |
| 6 | RW5a, RW4e, RW7a | RW1a; RW4a; RW1a | L (M–L), M (new), M (new) |
| 7 | RW2c, RW5b, RW2d, then RW7b | RW2b + the sources spec's S3 for the example; RW5a; RW2b (successor); RW7a + DL1 (successor) | S–M (§20.9), M, S–M, S–M (all new) |
| any, after DL2 | RW6b | DL2 merged with `due_hint`; RW2a; `main`'s EIG/EVSI modules; the sources spec optional (if its S2a has merged, RW6b reuses `value_candidates` and is nearer M–L). R1 recommended (receipt field names); R2 later for router-valued serving | L (M–L) |
| parallel | The sources spec's track | Its own spec; no Reader frontend | Owned there |
| separate | The four-count-caps stage | Its own successor (§18) | Owned by its stage |

**Totals.** Counting S as about one day, S–M two, M three, M–L five and L eight to ten, the first revision's eleven phases came to about 37 agent-days (five to seven weeks, serialized on the Reader frontend). The restored scope has nineteen phases and comes to about 68 agent-days: **roughly nine to twelve weeks** through the implement–review–fix loop with at most two Reader phases open. The increase comes from eight new phases (RW1c, RW2c, RW2d, RW4d, RW4e, RW5b, RW7a, RW7b; about 22 days) and wider RW1b, RW3b, RW5a, RW6a and RW6b (about 10 days), offset by about a day where RW4b's backend moved to the sources spec. The sources spec's track runs in parallel and is not counted here. **After the UX integration (§20.9)** the same count gives about 70 agent-days, still roughly nine to twelve weeks: RW1a and RW2a grow by about two days each (the return stack, the shared voice and state components, the experience gates; the half-window split, opening before the import binds), and RW4d and RW2c shrink by about a day each (the comparison and the worked-example search are the sources spec's). A learner-visible milestone lands every one to two weeks: inquiry and cues (order 1–2), the scratchpad (2), the discriminating workbench (4), the tutor that looks things up (5), the flagship figure and reading paths (6), follow-ups whenever DL2 lands.

### 20.6 Start now, or wait?

**Start RW1a now; hold everything else until its dependencies land.** RW1a mostly ports finished, tested work and splits the rail. No in-flight branch touches `ReaderScreen.tsx` or `learnloop/reader/`, and its only overlap (`tutor_qa.py` with learner-text gaps) is a few lines. It needs no model route and no successor. It is also the prerequisite for every other phase, so it removes the main merge hazard early. Two conditions: the integration queue (now R1, learner-text gaps and facet identity; DL1 and the ChatGPT-plan provider have merged) keeps priority for merges and reviews, and RW1a merges after whichever of those is ready first. RW3a, RW6a and RW2a can follow straight after RW1a. **RW2b should wait** for the ChatGPT-plan sign-in (latency) and learner-text gaps (its adapter). **RW6b must wait** for DL2. The restoration does not change this answer: RW1a's only additions are the friction audit, which should be written before its UI changes, and a transcript fixture. The sources spec's backend track can start at the same time; it touches no Reader frontend file.

### 20.7 Owner decision (2026-10-12): follow-ups are valued, not counted

**The owner:** "Follow up checks shouldn't be hardcapped artificially at one a session/2 a day. We want learnloop to prioritize EVSI/EIG and multiple follow ups might be high ROI for this to search for the true misconception or decide between hypotheses/decide between actions."

**What changed:**

| Before | After | Where |
|---|---|---|
| At most one follow-up per session and `followups.max_per_day` (2) a day | No count cap. Value of information chooses, numbers and dates follow-ups; the plan's finite minutes and a spend cap bound them | §1, §9.3, §11, §16 D17 |
| An LLM chooser (`followup_chooser`) picked the one capability most worth checking | The existing machinery values every candidate: probe EIG over the open hypotheses (`probe_families.py:467`, `probes.py:786`, `followups.py:1482`), EVSI for the pending decision (`evsi.py:199`), ranked and stopped by `evsi.rank_feasible` (`:366`), with greedy conditional EIG so duplicates drop out. The model only judges relevance (`followup_relevance`) | W4, W13, §3.2, §9.3 |
| One date rule (effective due, 1–14 days) | Dated by purpose: discriminating checks next day, retention checks at effective due; `max_wait_days` 14 is a time cap | §9.3 item 4 |
| — | Daily re-valuation retires follow-ups whose question has since been answered, with the reason shown and undo | §9.3 item 7 |
| One follow-up line with undo | Every check listed with date, purpose and the valuation as LearnLoop's estimate; undo each or all | §9.1, §9.3 |
| — | Valuation receipt on the digest row; a valuation-calibration report block | §9.4, §15 |
| Gates G26–G27 checked the caps | G26–G27 check the stop rule, conditional valuation, dating and minutes; G35 checks reuse | §14 |
| RW6b: DL2 and RW2a; size M | Also `main`'s EIG/EVSI modules; R1 recommended for receipt names; R2 later values pending follow-ups as block candidates; size M–L | §13, §18, §20.5 |

**Every number that remains is a cap on money or time, never a learning threshold** (W4): `followups.max_daily_cost_usd` (money), `followups.max_wait_days` (time a claim waits), `followups.fallback_days` (a dating default when there is no FSRS state), and the plan's own minutes and lane shares (DL1). The valuation's λ, burden and nat-to-minutes scale are not new: they are the existing decision parameters, labelled heuristic in `robust_composition.py`, and RW6b's receipts are data for calibrating them (§20.4 risk 9).

**Not changed here:** the same principle applies to count caps that live in other specs and code: `probe.episode.session_qualifying_observation_cap` (4) and `maximum_observations` (4) for probe episodes (`config/schema.py:224`, `:209`); `scheduler.followup.max_interventions_per_lo_per_session` (1) for post-attempt follow-ups (`schema.py:40`, enforced at `diagnosis/followups.py:339`); `teach_back.session_cap` (1); and `plan.project_probes_max_per_day` (4) in the daily plan. **The owner has since decided (2026-10-12) to lift them on the same value-of-information basis, as a separate serving stage with its own successor;** §18 records the decision and §20.8 its place in the restoration. RW6b neither applies nor changes them.


### 20.8 Owner decisions (2026-10-12): restoration

**The owner** (after reading the coverage audit, `docs/reviews/reader-dialogue-coverage.md`): "Restore all of it into the spec." "Build the router and sources path as its own backend track now." "Lift the other 4 caps on the same value of information basis." And: "make sure most of the intentions and ideas are included in the spec, with the most ambitious/impossible to implement at this stage usefully explicitly labeled as deferred in the spec instead of entirely absent."

**What the decisions mean for this spec.**
1. **Every cut the audit found is restored** into the phase where it belongs, or listed in §17 as a labelled DEFERRED item with what it would take and a trigger. The audit's 74 non-BUILT rows (50 narrowed, 9 deferred, 5 dropped, 10 elsewhere) are accounted for in the counts below and in the "Restored" column added to the audit.
2. **The backend for canonical sources as practice and probes is a separate spec**, `spec_source_practice_and_probes_v1.md` (branch `sources/canonical-practice`). This spec consumes it in five phases and designs none of it (§18). That takes the audit's RS1 and its RW4c out of this spec.
3. **The four other count caps** are lifted in a separate serving stage with its own successor (§18). Not built here.
4. **W1 is rewritten** to separate three tiers of signals (§2): what was "not evidence, so not an observation at all" is now "never evidence, may steer hypotheses and routing", consistent with P3 invariant 6 and learner field F2/F3.

**What was restored, by the audit's recommendations:**

| Restoration | What | Where |
|---|---|---|
| RS1 (canonical sources as practice, probes and explanations) and RW4c | Built by the sources spec; the Reader consumes it: "Practice this question", the exercise view, "Check me", RW1c's offers, the workbench's "a short problem instead", RW6b's candidates and intents | §18; §7.3, §7.4, §6.1, §4.6, §5.2, §9.3 |
| RS1 step 3 (explanations from the sources for the tutor) | The Reader-side tutor: its own query, three modes, another way, that didn't help, offers | RW1c, §4.6 |

Randomized hint-quality experiments stay "not planned".

**Two questions the owner asked alongside, answered where they belong.**
- *"Is there any way we could better calibrate the EVSI instead of having it run on uncalibrated assumptions?"* This spec contributes data, not a method: RW6b's valuation receipts (predicted EIG and EVSI against the realized posterior change and whether the decision flipped), RW2b's discriminating answers (predicted EIG against how far the diagnoser's verdict narrowed), and RW2c's logged draws. The calibration method — fitting λ, the burden and the nat-to-minutes scale, which `robust_composition.py:44`–`:55` labels heuristic — belongs to the decision-value spec (§9 there) and any EVSI-calibration track; §15's valuation-calibration and discrimination blocks are where its inputs appear. *(§20.9 adds the method's owner and the learner's side: see there.)*
- *A human-centred UI/UX design pass over the brainstorm.* That is separate work, `docs/specs/ux_reader_workbench_v1.md` (experience design, written in parallel for this restored scope). It governs how these phases look and feel; this spec governs behaviour, contracts and data, and its wording rules (W2, G16) and restraint rules (W11, W12) are constraints that design keeps. *(§20.9 integrates it and resolves the disagreements between the two.)*

**Other UX proposals adopted:** the three voices and the well's markers (W2, G55); the seven model-action states and their landing places (W14, G56); the attention budget (W12, G54); "where this goes" on every free-text box (W5); the key map and keyboard reachability (W11, G57, the friction audit); the experience measures — return rate, detour cost, proactive load, waiting behaviour (§4.1, §15); the learner-side calibration signals (§15); the owner checkpoints and first-week checklist as optional companions (§15). **Rejected:** only the cold-check confirmation dialog (D42), replaced as above. **Modified:** "later: 2 more", "will be planned", "2–4 pinned", U2's test, and the two meanings of "check me", as in the contradictions table.

**Open UX questions** Q1–Q13 are owner decisions D29–D41 with the UX document's recommended defaults; D42 and D43 settle the two contradictions above that needed a decision.

**The owner's EVSI-calibration question, with what now exists.** Yes — each input of the value of information can be checked against what happens, and three places share the work:
- **the sources spec's S5 records the inputs** (its §11.4): the predicted outcome distribution P(E) against the observed outcome (log loss and a reliability table, prequential by date); predicted EIG against the realized entropy drop; whether a pending decision flipped as EVSI predicted; predicted against actual minutes; and the value of a nat, fitted as the minutes a downstream decision saved per nat resolved once later cold outcomes say which action was right;
- **the `evsi/calibration` track fits the parameters** (λ stays the numeraire; the burden and the nat-to-minutes scale are fitted) and ships any change as a successor, so a calibration lands everywhere at once;
- **this spec supplies Reader data at no cost to the learner** (§15): RW6b's receipts with P(E) per chosen check; RW2b's discriminating questions, whose predicted answer per rival is a direct test of the likelihoods (the answer given, then the diagnoser's verdict at Submit); realized minutes; and the learner's revealed costs — undo of a scheduled check with an optional reason, and skips in the plan — as evidence about λ and the burden. The learner is never asked to rate usefulness. Until the parameters are fitted, every valuation says "LearnLoop's estimate; its scale is not yet calibrated".

### 20.10 Spec–UX consistency review (2026-10-13)

**What was reviewed.** An independent critic (Codex, three rounds, read-only, at `073ab684`) compared this spec and `docs/specs/ux_reader_workbench_v1.md` with the brainstorm. It found **no absent brainstorm idea and no unlabelled cut or narrowing**, counting §17's labelled deferrals and the sources spec's ownership of the backend. It found **seven contradictions between the two documents**. Each is resolved below in both documents; none was rejected, because each was checked and found right.

| # | Contradiction | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **When the tutor's offers run.** §4.6 said nothing runs until pressed, then "practice and passages run when shown"; the UX document had buttons that do nothing until pressed | **D44: fetch early, show on press.** The lookups start when the answer lands (the sources spec's D5, so a press never waits); nothing is shown, and no impression or exposure is recorded, until the press; a comparison runs only when pressed. An empty lookup says so instead of opening an empty list. §4.6, UX §4.2, G59 |
| 2 | **The flagship figure failed this spec's own verification.** W11's density, p·½λe^{λx}·[x<0] + (1−p)·½λe^{−λx}·[x≥0], has total mass ½, so its 30% readout (the true left-side integral is 15%) and its "checked" label were wrong; and the narrative said raising p moves probability to the right side, when P(X ≤ 0) = p | The density is corrected to the dialogue's (B 591–611: 30% left and 70% right at p = 0.30), with a redrawn plot. The prediction becomes "if λ doubles, what happens to P(X ≤ 0)?" — the learner answers *it rises* and discovers it stays at p — so the moment keeps a real, instructive miss. The wrong density is now G45's negative control. §8.1, UX moment 9, W11, W12 |
| 3 | **A hint's checkpoint.** Rule 2 forbids continuing from the questionable step; the UX hint was labelled "from your step 3", the questionable step, and rule 3 put a hint under its checkpoint while UX §0 put it under the step it is about | **D45:** a hint has an `about_step` (where it sits, quoted) and a `continue_from` (where it builds from, never the questionable step or its dependents); the label names both, *on your step 3 · continues from step 2*. Rules 2–3, the "not where I'm stuck" rule, the assists table, G7, G60; UX §5.5–5.6, moment 6, W6, W7 |
| 4 | **"Check my work" was not counted.** §5.4 records each displayed check as a hint; W5 showed a completed check with "help used: none", and the submit line omitted it | Every example counts it: *book open · 3 hints (a check, a level-1 question, a level-3 hint)*; the check button says *counts as a hint* before use. §5.1, G62; UX moments 5 and 7, §5.4, §5.5, §5.9, W5, W12 |
| 5 | **"Where this goes" misstated cue feedback.** UX §4.10 gave every box, "not relevant" included, the copy "LearnLoop's model may read it to name a gap"; §6.2 keeps cue-feedback text out of learner-text gaps (as §9.3 keeps undo's reason) | Per-box copy: a table in UX §4.10 says, for each box, whether a model may name a gap from it or what it is used for instead (cue judging, check valuation, the learner's own relation, a figure revision). W5, G62 |
| 6 | **The chooser's fallback.** §5.5 offers level 1, then one level up per further Help press; UX §5.5 offered rung 1 with no progression | Both now say: rung 1, then one above the last level offered or taken on that step, per further Help press on the same step (made precise here: "on the same step"), with an honest *why?*. §5.5, G62; UX §5.5 |
| 7 | **Locators before feedback.** §9.3 item 10 allowed no locator during a check on your own; W13 and W16 showed "Exercise 3.19" and "Exercise 4.7", and W13 also showed "from your reading of §5.4" and *why* (P3 invariant 10 withholds source cues and purpose text from a cold administration) | **D46:** the book's name only, until feedback; then the exercise number, page, link and *why this check* appear. The plan line before the check keeps its *why*. §9.3 item 10; requests to DL2 (§18 (b)) and to the sources spec, whose header hid only the link (§18 request 8); G61; UX §4.6, §4.9, W13, W16 |

**What did not change.** No phase, table role, successor, kill switch or deferral moved. D44 adds no call: the sources spec already runs these lookups eagerly; it only fixes what the Reader shows and when it records the impression. D45 adds one column (`about_step_id`) to RW2b's assists migration, which has not been written. D46 removes text from a header.

**Prevention.** G59–G62 turn each resolution into a test, and G45's negative control would have rejected W11's density before it showed: the spec's verification was right and the wireframe was never run through it.

### 20.11 Adaptive hints folded in (2026-10-13)

**What was done.** `docs/specs/spec_adaptive_hints_v1.md` (branch `docs/adaptive-hints`, committed at `67adef8a`) answers the owner's request of 2026-10-13: hints as "waypoints" and not only next steps, written for the learner's current work when they ask instead of minted when an item is created, through one hint service for every surface. Its §8.2 proposed twelve edits to this spec. All twelve are applied below, seven with an adaptation; none is rejected. The experience design is updated where the ladder's presentation changes (UX §5.5–5.8, §5.11, moment 6, W6–W7). The rule for conflicts: **that spec wins on hint generation** (kinds, the writer and its contracts, approach maps, the chooser and how it picks, hint spend); **this spec wins on Reader presentation and on evidence mapping** (the ladder, labels, the step anchors, what an attempt records). That spec owns the service, the maps and the chooser; this spec cites it by section and duplicates none of it.

**The research that shaped the workbench's use of it** (read and cited in adaptive-hints §1): hints differ in kind as well as amount, and subgoal labels and backward worked examples improved transfer and proof efficiency in controlled studies; the expertise-reversal effect (more guidance helps lower prior knowledge and hinders higher, strongest in higher education, with no cut line) argues for choosing the kind from the learner's state; and **for waypoints the evidence is weaker than the excerpt the owner read** — unsolicited waypoints at random intervals cost low-prior learners time and steps and did not significantly help high-prior ones (Cody et al. 2022), learners who could not bridge to a waypoint tried, failed and gave up, and waypoints offered only when needed helped the weakest and cut help avoidance (Alam et al. 2023). So in the workbench a waypoint is one kind among several, chosen from state, never unsolicited, with a smaller step toward the same goal one key away (§5.2).

**The twelve proposed edits.**

| # | Proposed edit (adaptive-hints §8.2) | Ruling | Where | Adaptation, and why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | §5.2 ladder: kinds per level (L1 approach question including "first move", discriminating; L2 localize; L3 waypoint with a horizon, labelled subgoal plan, next step, error-specific, discriminating, worked parallel, authored; L4 backward worked chunk and the partial continuation; L5 walkthrough); level 3 becomes "one of the level-3 kinds" | **Applied, adapted** | §5.2's table and "levels decide evidence, kinds decide form"; UX §5.5–5.6 | The forward partial continuation, which the proposal keeps at level 4 but its own §4.2 table omits, is named `continuation` (C10). With two or more rivals open, rung 1's kind is `discriminating`, because the ladder shows it there (C1). The kind contracts are cited, not copied |
| 2 | "Authored hints first" becomes "authored hints are seeds" and the fallback | **Applied** | §5.2 | Same behaviour, clearer role; under the chooser's fallback they serve rung 3 (C6) |
| 3 | Reconciliation contract: a placement rule (a hint names its route and target node; the target's distance matches the horizon); rule 5 satisfied by a different kind or slug; the per-kind contracts | **Applied, adapted** | §5.2 rules 5, 7 and 8; G7 | Numbered 7 and 8, because rule 6 is the Reader's "asks before it repairs", which §20.8 and the UX cite. Placement starts from the node the `continue_from` step reaches, so D45's split decides where a hint builds from (C3). Rule 8 cites adaptive-hints §4.2's contract column. Failing 7 or 8 downgrades as failing 1, 2 or 5 does |
| 4 | Defined effects for the "doesn't connect" shortcuts ("different approach" switches route or kind; "too big a jump" shortens the horizon or the grain; "notation" becomes a note) | **Applied, adapted** | §5.2; D21; G33; UX §5.7 | This spec's four shortcuts and meanings stand: "I'm using a different approach" keeps the learner's route, the opposite of the proposal's reading (C2), and "that's not where I'm stuck", which the proposal lacks, moves the hint. Every reply keeps the level (C16); "the notation" is exempt from rule 5 (C17) |
| 5 | §5.5: one Decider request for (kind, level) and horizon, **sampled** from its probabilities with the propensity logged, replacing "no randomization of help quality"; §17's row against deliberately worse help kept | **Applied, adapted** | §5.5; D8; G63; §15; §17 | Two workbench additions: when the learner takes a rung other than the suggested one, the kind at that level is drawn from the Decider's probabilities conditional on the level and that probability is logged; and the fallback keeps this spec's rung progression (C6) |
| 6 | More chooser inputs: the map placement from the last check, the route preference, per-kind history on the facets, time since the last hint and whether the work changed, a closed probe episode's recommended move | **Applied** | §5.5 | Per-kind history comes from RW2c's `help_outcomes` once it lands, which reads the service's per-hint outcomes instead of deriving them again (C13) |
| 7 | One shared `help_assists` with a `surface` column and the hint columns, created by whichever of RW2b and H0 lands first, with RW2b's check and bridge rows in it | **Applied, adapted** | §5.7; §11 | The table needs two kind columns, `hint_kind` (the proposal's `kind`) and `assist_kind` (the workbench's), and a discriminating question becomes a `hint` row (C9); the shortcut value `different_approach` is renamed `learner_route` (C2); every CHECK holds both specs' values |
| 8 | Shared routes: `workbench_hint` → `hint_writer`, `workbench_level_chooser` → `hint_chooser`; add `approach_map` | **Applied** | §5.8; §5.9; §11 | Consequences: `workbench_check` stays the Reader's and now also carries RW2c's compare line, which is not a hint; the ledger task `workbench_hint_fit` becomes the shared `hint_fit` (C11); hint calls fall under the service's money caps (C8); `workbench.level_chooser_enabled` gives way to the shared `hints.chooser_enabled` (C19) |
| 9 | "Check my work" reads the approach map: step verdicts name the map node a step reaches | **Applied** | §5.2 | The proposal cites §5.3; "Check my work" is in §5.2 (§5.3 is symbolic checks). The node is placement, never shown |
| 10 | §17: the "full solution graphs" row partly superseded by approach maps; the deferral kept for full step-level graphs with every branch | **Applied** | §17; §1 | The row is renamed "Full step-level solution graphs with every branch" and its trigger now waits for approach maps to be live |
| 11 | §15 workbench block: per-kind rows with propensities beside "help level taken vs offered" | **Applied, adapted** | §15 | Read from the service's report for `surface = 'workbench'`, not recomputed; adds goals reached after a waypoint and suggested against taken kinds by help style; the "experiments" note names the sampled kinds as a comparison that withholds nothing |
| 12 | §16 D8: the first offer is a (level, kind, horizon) choice, sampled, default style "balanced"; fallback "authored hints, then level 1 and up" | **Applied, adapted** | §16 D8 | The fallback is this spec's progression — rung 1, then one rung up per further Help press on the step (§20.10 #6, G62) — with the authored hints serving rung 3, not "authored hints, then level 1 and up" (C6) |

**Conflicts resolved.**

| # | Topic | The adaptive-hints spec | This spec (before) | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Asking before repairing** | `discriminating` is one kind the sampled chooser may pick when two hypotheses are open | With two or more rivals open, the first offer *is* the discriminating question (D24, G37) | **Generation: that spec.** Which rung is suggested is the sampled judgment, whose instructions favour asking first; "guide me" leans away. **Presentation: this spec.** Rung 1 *is* the discriminating question whenever rivals are open, so it is always one key away; its candidates are ranked by EIG (W4), a zero-EIG question is never shown, one at a time. §0, §5.2, §5.5, D24, G37; UX §5.8; §18 request 5 |
| C2 | **"Different approach"** | A request to switch route on the map, or kind at the same level | "I'm using a different approach" keeps the learner's route (§20.9, rule 4) | **This spec** (presentation, and a contradiction already settled once): the learner's route is kept, the route switch is only "show me the book's way", and a change of kind comes from rule 5 after any reply. The stored value is `learner_route` so the shared column cannot be read both ways. §5.2, §5.7; §18 request 3 |
| C3 | **Where a hint starts** | Placement node reached by the quoted work, no later than the first questionable point | D45: `about_step` (where it sits) and `continue_from` (a standing step that does not depend on the questionable one) | **Compatible; this spec is stricter.** The map placement's starting node is the node `continue_from` reaches; rule 7 measures the horizon from there. Rule 7 |
| C4 | **Levels 4–5 and `hints_used`** | `primed = true` and `hints_used` += 1 | D6: `primed = true` only | **Evidence mapping: this spec.** Classification is the same either way (primed attempts are `repair_activity`), but the count differs; the server's fold should have one rule on every surface. §5.4; §18 request 4 |
| C5 | **A discriminating answer** | Part of the learner's work, so part of the submitted answer if kept | An assist row, in the diagnoser trace and the next hint's context, never a step | **This spec** (evidence: the blind grader sees the composed steps only, W8). Unchanged §5.2, §5.7 |
| C6 | **The fallback** | Authored hints in order, then `next_step`; its §8.2 item 12 proposed "authored hints, then level 1 and up" | Rung 1, then one rung up per Help press on the same step (§20.10 #6, G62) | **Presentation: this spec**, for the ladder; authored hints fill rung 3 under fallback. The practice screen keeps that spec's own fallback. §5.5, D8 |
| C7 | **The help style control** | In the hint panel's `⋯` menu and Settings; never on the screen | A three-way control in the workbench header, global and remembered, with a per-session override (D31) | **Presentation: this spec** in the workbench. One stored preference read by the chooser on every surface (Settings), the header's override on `workbench_sessions.help_style`. §5.5; UX §3.3, §5.11; §18 request 8 |
| C8 | **Hint spend** | Money caps only (`hints.max_daily_cost_usd`), no count of hints | `workbench.max_calls_per_session` (24) and `workbench.max_daily_cost_usd` covered every workbench call | **Generation: that spec.** Hint calls fall under the service's money caps, with no count; the workbench's caps bound only its own calls (checks, the compare line). §5.8, G12 |
| C9 | **Two meanings of `kind`** | `kind` is the hint kind | `kind` is the assist kind (`check`, `hint`, `discriminate`, …) | Both columns, `assist_kind` and `hint_kind`; a discriminating question is a `hint` row with `hint_kind = 'discriminating'`. §5.7; §18 request 1 |
| C10 | **The forward continuation** | Its table has only `worked_chunk` at level 4, though its §8.2 item 1 keeps "the partial continuation" | Level 4 is the next portion forward | Kind `continuation` added beside `worked_chunk`. §5.2; §18 request 2 |
| C11 | **The feedback ledger task** | `hint_fit` | `workbench_hint_fit` | The shared `hint_fit`: one service, one task. §5.2, §11 |
| C12 | **Learner text from "doesn't connect"** | A `hint_feedback` source kind (its §15, which names this spec's kind `workbench_stuck`) | `workbench_text` with role `hint_feedback` | **This spec** in the workbench; that spec's practice-screen kind joins the one widening migration if it is ready, so `learner_text_gaps` is rebuilt once. §18 request 7 |
| C13 | **Hint outcomes derived twice** | Per-hint outcomes (its §6.1) | RW2c's `help_outcomes` derived them from assists | Defined once, there; `help_outcomes` reads them and keeps its own derivation for help that service does not own (the S5 pattern). §5.10 |
| C14 | **Build order** | H3 needs RW2b and H2; "if RW2b lands first, it builds `help_assists` and calls the service" | RW2b built its own writer and chooser | If RW2b is ready before H2, it builds the service's core once, to that spec's design in `learnloop/hints/` with its tests (the sources-spec pattern, W13). §3.3, §13, G64 |
| C15 | **Reveal basis** | `hint:<kind>:L<level>` | `workbench_hint:L<n>` | **Evidence mapping: this spec**, extended with the kind: `workbench_hint:<kind>:L<n>`. §5.4; §18 request 6 |
| C16 | **The level after a shortcut** | "Too big a jump" asks for a shorter horizon or a finer kind; the level is not stated | The next hint is requested at once, without the ladder's take step | The next hint keeps the level of the hint it answers, so a reply never raises the help untaken; a higher rung is the learner's to take. §5.2, §5.5, G33, G63 |
| C17 | **"The notation" and rule 5** | Rule 5 requires a different kind or strategy after any reply; notation is "a notation note" | Rule 5 the same | The notation shortcut keeps the idea and restates it in the learner's symbols, so it is exempt from rule 5. §5.2; §18 request 5 |
| C18 | **Latency target** | Writer p50 ≤ 8 s, reported | Check and hint p50 ≤ 10 s, reported | Hints take the service's target; checks keep 10 s; both reported, not enforced. §5.8 |
| C19 | **The chooser's kill switch** | `hints.chooser_enabled` | `workbench.level_chooser_enabled` | One chooser, one switch: `hints.chooser_enabled`; the Reader key is withdrawn. §5.9, §11 |

**What did not change.** No phase, successor, table role or evidence rule moved: the level alone maps to `hints_used`, `primed` and reveals; there is no new reveal or interaction-event kind and no DERIVED table; the grader stays blind to hints, kinds and maps (W8); hints stay tier 2 (W1). `help_assists` replaces `workbench_assists` in the same role (RAW_LEDGER) with more columns. RW2b stays L: the service replaces its own writer and chooser, which offsets the kinds' interface and the placement rule. The gates gain G63 (kinds, horizons and the draw) and G64 (one hint service), and G5, G7, G8, G12, G33 and G37 are extended. Risks 21–22 are new (§20.4).

**The experience design** (UX document, same commit): the suggested rung names its kind in plain words and, for a waypoint, how far ahead it points; hints carry kind labels; a waypoint offers *a smaller step toward this goal* (`Ctrl+Shift+H`) and every hint *show me the book's way*; the four shortcuts say what they do; rung 1's discriminating question is usually, not always, suggested; the help style's lines name kinds and the setting is shared with the practice screen; the UX measures and first-week checklist gain goals reached after a waypoint.
